# The Unified Model: rulings

> **Kept in the repository as the standing authority for the theory (October 2026).** This is the adjudicator's
> working document, as it stood when the pass closed. Paths in it (`$NOTES`, `factcheck-3.md`, `verify-1.md`
> and the like) refer to the working papers now in `research/2026-10-pass/`. Three keys were renamed when the
> work was merged, after this was written: the people group `emily` is `cassette`, the chapter id `partners`
> is `hen-and-wa`, and the free tag `toysnhk` is `one-you-should-not-have-killed`. Record ids were not changed.
> Where a record as it now stands is more careful than a ruling's description of it, the record governs.
>
> **After the pass closed (3 October 2026).** Records added from recordings of play settle some things this
> document leaves open, and the theory's text follows the records. R26: "Arnold" and "F10-N4" are now quoted;
> Helpi's lower-case "the entity" is the game's own word (first tier); "Shadow Bonnie" is in Scott's guide to
> Ultimate Custom Night (second tier); the robot's label on screen is "Maskbot". R19: Security Breach's cast
> list was read from its credits roll and agrees with the transcription, so it no longer takes "(a fan wiki's
> account)". R31: no text of FNaF3 says whose Fazbear's Fright is beyond "Local amusement park" and "the
> owners", so "not the company's" is now marked "most likely". R19 (4): the ending below ground shows the place
> burning and coming down as the two run out, and nothing from outside or afterwards. See `open-checks.md`.


Adjudicator's rulings for the rebuilt theory. Written 2 October 2026, from the six analyst memos
(`digest-contested.md`, `digest-threads.md`), the current `theory.ts`, and the evidence records themselves.
Every record id in backticks exists in `src/data/evidence/*.json` (machine-checked). The records a ruling
leans on were opened and read; some supporting ids are cited on the analysts' description of them.

How to read this file:

- **Confidence** is `high`, `medium` or `low`; "medium-high" and "low-medium" are used where a ruling sits between
  two. A chapter takes the confidence of its weakest load-bearing ruling.
- **[CHANGE]** marks a ruling that differed from the theory as it stood before the rebuild (the `theory.ts` of
  1 October 2026). Section 5 lists every such change. The site has since been rewritten to these rulings, so
  the revision notes below, section 5 and section 6 are a log: what was asked for and has been carried out, and
  the risks as they stood. Where the log and a ruling differ, the ruling governs.
- There are 33 rulings. R33 was added after the third review; it adds no record and says what four other
  rulings come to together.
- "The model reads X as Y" always means inference. "The records show" or "stated" means a record says or depicts it.
- Book-only names are not used. Descriptors: the Cassette Man, the Cassette Man's daughter, the crying child, the
  older brother, the fifth child. The entity id `henrys-daughter` and the group id `emily` appear here only as
  ids: the first has since been renamed `cassette-mans-daughter`; the second is a key in the code that is never
  printed, and is to become `cassette` when the branches are merged, as the chapter id `partners` is to become
  `hen-and-wa` and the tag `toysnhk` `not-have-killed`. Where these files say `partners` they mean that
  chapter. Record ids are kept stable (R26, "Ids").
- Scott's 2018 and 2024 interview statements rest on machine transcripts of archived video. They are marked
  **(a transcript)**, the form the site prints, and are never the only support for a ruling about the story.
- The site prints four tags for how a record was read, in these exact forms: "(a transcript)", "(read from an
  image)", "(as described)" and "(a fan wiki's account)". A count, a comparison or a detail taken from a fan
  wiki takes the last. The rulings use the same four forms.
- The site's markers and marks, which the rulings' grades map onto (R27 has the rule): "most likely" marks a
  short inference (medium-high, or a firm or strong inference); "the model reads" marks this archive's own
  interpretation (medium, and low-medium where a ruling calls it a reading); "may be", given with its reason,
  marks a possibility held loosely (low); where two readings are close the model "leans" to one. A chapter
  carries the mark of its main claim; a beat of the storyline carries the mark of the weakest thing it tells.

rest on OCR and independent readers. R3's "one house" row and the paragraph after its table, a note in R4, R17's
records, R25's Old Man Consequences row and the ninth open risk were reworded; step 20 of section 3.2 now uses
R28's wording for the fire. No ruling and no confidence changed. R5 already cited
`fnaf4-plush-night6-pupils-only` in its corrected sense (the plush is whole at Night 6). A compact version of the
rulings for writers is in `rulings-brief.md`.

dating rows 12, 13 and 15, steps 6 to 9, 11 and 12 of the order, section 5.1 rows 3 to 6 and 8 to 14, the

- **Section 3**: rows 2, 3, 6, 8, 9, 31, 35, 37 and 38 and steps 1, 3, 4, 5 and 22 to 28 are reworded or

Eight rulings outside the group have a sentence or a passage changed for consistency: R1, R8, R12, R15, R16,
R24, R25 and R28. R28 has a second "Review:" line. In R15 "the builder" becomes "the Cassette Man" or "the man

## 1. Thesis

**The model in one paragraph.**

Five Nights at Freddy's is one story about what a machine keeps of a person, and about a company that keeps
putting it back on stage. By the 1970s a costume maker builds two kinds of thing. For a restaurant company he
builds suits that are costume and machine at once. For his own family he builds machines that copy whoever is
near them. William Afton, a designer of performing machines, kills an old friend's daughter, and five more
children whom he lures in the company's own costume. Their bodies are never found, and the children stay on in
the mascots. The games show one hand in that: the Puppet's, and the Puppet is the friend's dead daughter. Afton
then finds, in his friend's words, "a new way to desecrate", and the machines take his own children too. For
years the mascots hunt the night guards, until five ghosts corner their killer: he dies in the rabbit suit and,
decades later, comes back in it, and his son goes looking for him. Then the friend calls four haunted machines
into one building and stays as it burns. The game that tells it ends on six graves, and the later games reopen
it. The company scans old circuit boards into a game, and a rabbit that presents as the killer gets into the
code: something he left behind, on the model's reading. Under the company's Mega Pizzaplex a child sets loose
the costume maker's copying machine, which had called her in another child's voice.

[Brought level with the printed chapter in round 4d: three sentences are split as the chapter `thesis` prints
them ("builds two kinds of thing"; "The games show one hand in that"; "The game that tells it"). No claim is
changed.]

**Standfirst (proposed).** "A killer in the company's own costume, the machines that kept his victims, and the
copies that came after: one story, built only from the games and Scott Cawthon's own words."

Shorter fallback: "One story, from a costume workshop in the 1970s to the ruin of the Pizzaplex, built only from
the games and Scott Cawthon's own words."

**How firm each clause is.** The thesis is told in a confident voice; this table is what it is entitled to. The
paragraph carries one plain marker, "on the model's reading", on what the rabbit in the code is. The chapter
that prints the thesis carries the others where they belong. After the first review the paragraph no longer
gives an order for the first murders, puts no costume on the daughter's killer, and does not say where the
bodies went. After the second it no longer says "Nobody designed that", "It does not end Afton" or "burns with
him inside". After the third it no longer says "partner" or "the two men behind the restaurants", no longer
says that the game inside the game "works where fire did not", and tells the nights the player plays. It is
also shorter: what left the paragraph is in the last two rows.

| Clause | Confidence | Ruling |
|---|---|---|
| A company that keeps putting it back on stage | high as a pattern, on these acts: a new restaurant in 1987 with the old machines kept for parts (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`, `fnaf2-call2-older-models`); a promise to reopen and the originals on stage again in FNaF1 (`fnaf2-news-reopen-smaller-budget`); its own history sold as games and as a VR game (R23); machines sent to homes; the Pizzaplex. Fazbear's Fright is not one of them: a local amusement park runs it (`fnaf3-coming-soon-newspaper`). Pizzeria Simulator's franchise counts only if the trap was the company's | R31 |
| A costume maker builds two kinds of thing. For a restaurant company he builds suits that are costume and machine at once | high, for the suits and for the contract with the company | R2 |
| For his own family he builds machines that copy whoever is near them | high for the two machines; medium-high that the second's violence copies his own | R21 |
| William Afton is the killer, a designer of performing machines, and the father | high | R1 |
| "An old friend" | stated: the Cassette Man calls the one he damns "old friend" (`ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`) and says he "unwillingly helped to create" a monster (`ffps-insanity-monster-helped-create`). The speech does not name the one addressed: that he means Afton is the usual reading and the model's, most likely (R1, link e: strong), and it is not "implied" by the record, which gives it as a reading. That the two were partners in the business is the model's reading, medium, and the paragraph does not say it. That they are the "W.A." and "Hen" of 1979: medium and low | R2 |
| He lures the five in the company's own costume | high that police thought the man "dressed as a company mascot"; that the man was Afton is R1's link e (strong, and an inference); medium that the costume was the yellow rabbit. No record puts a costume on the daughter's killer, and the sentence gives the costume to the five only | R1, R8 |
| An old friend's daughter, and five more | medium-high for each event. The order (she first) is a convention, low-medium, and the paragraph does not assert it | R7, R8 |
| Their bodies are never found, and the children stay on in the mascots | high for the first (stated). Medium that the bodies were put into the mascots, which no record shows and the paragraph does not say. Medium-high that the children stayed | R8, R14 |
| The games show one hand in that: the Puppet's, and the Puppet is the friend's dead daughter | shown in one minigame, Give Gifts, Give Life; medium-high that the Puppet is the Cassette Man's daughter. The man who calls the machines "prisons of my making" says he never learned how the dead came to stay in them (stated; no longer in the paragraph) | R7, R8, R15 |
| Afton then finds "a new way to desecrate" | stated, by the Cassette Man, that a "new" wrong followed what he "had already done" (`ffps-insanity-new-way-to-desecrate`); that it was done to the same victims is a reading, from the next sentence of the tape ("small souls... now set to new purpose"): the quoted sentence names no object. What it was: low-medium that it is the Afton Robotics sheet headed "S.calable C.reation of U.lterior P.resence", which the paragraph no longer names. When the Funtime machines were built is R12's convention | R12, R15, R32 |
| The machines take his own children too | high for Elizabeth (taken by Circus Baby) and Michael (scooped and worn); medium that the boy bitten by Fredbear is his | R3, R13, R32 |
| For years the mascots hunt the night guards | high that they do, in two restaurants: those nights are two of the games, set at least a year apart. The company explains it twice as a quirk of programming; no ruling says why they hunt, and the model rests nothing on it | R12, R24, R31 |
| Until five ghosts corner their killer; he dies in the rabbit suit and, decades later, comes back in it | high for the scene as shown: five grey, crying child figures corner the purple figure, the yellow rabbit suit snaps shut on him, he bleeds and slumps, and the figures fade. That he dies there is a firm inference (R1, link a: strong), from the tapes on what a sprung suit does and the body in the suit thirty years on. That the figures are the ghosts of the five is the model's reading (the join of R1's link e: strong). The paragraph says both flat; the chapter `springtrap` states each once with its supports (R12's rule). "Until" needs him to die after FNaF1's restaurant closed: medium, lower end. "Decades": the attraction that finds him opens thirty years after a closing. That it is the suit and the room of the five murders: medium | R1, R8, R12, R24 |
| His son goes looking for him | high: Michael says so, standing in the burnt attraction | R13 |
| Then the friend calls four haunted machines into one building and stays as it burns | high for the call and the fire; "then", after the rabbit is found, is medium-high; "stays" is his own "I am remaining as well"; the game does not show who lights it | R12, R28 |
| The game that tells it ends on six graves, and the later games reopen it | high that the graves are shown, after the credits, and that the game presents the fire as the killer's end; medium that the fire freed the children; medium-high that a Fazbear Entertainment trades afterwards | R14, R28, R33 |
| The company scans old circuit boards into a game, and a rabbit that presents as the killer gets into the code | high for the scan and the personality; medium-high that it presents as the killer. That the scan followed the fire is the model's convention | R18, R33 |
| Something he left behind, on the model's reading | medium, against a copying program at low-medium. It leans on the convention in the row above | R18, R33 |
| Under the company's Mega Pizzaplex a child sets loose the costume maker's copying machine, which had called her in another child's voice | high for the scene; medium-high that it is the 1979 machine itself; how it came to be there is not shown | R21 |
| Not in the paragraph: in UCN a voice holds someone in a night without end | stated that a voice refuses release. Low-medium that the one held is the killer: a lean, told in the UCN chapter with the Michael reading beside it | R16, R33 |
| Not in the paragraph: a follower in a rabbit costume; burnt rabbit remains under its Mega Pizzaplex; the game inside the game | medium that the follower is Vanessa (was medium-high: the rooftop page counts against it at full weight and the cast list at support weight); low-medium that the remains are Afton's; low-medium that Princess Quest works as a remedy. What the model holds of Afton after the fire, taken together, is R33: his image returns (high); something of him persists in at least one form (medium); the strands are not one history | R19, R20, R33 |

**What the thesis no longer says.** That a designer "learns that something of a dead person can persist inside a
machine" and "uses that first to kill": the records have the killings first and the "new way" after, and the man
who calls the machines his own making says he does not know how the dead came to stay in them. That "what keeps
the dead in machines is called Remnant". That fire is "the characters' repeated answer": it is one man's answer,
once. That the Mimic walking out of a fire shows fire failing. And, after review: that Afton killed the Cassette
Man's daughter "in the company's costume" (no record shows one), and that the five children's bodies "go into the
mascots" (the model reads it so, at medium; no record shows it). After the second review: "Nobody designed that",
which rested on one man's ignorance and sat against a minigame in which the Puppet acts; "It does not end Afton",
which told a lean as a fact; and "burns with him inside", where his word is "remaining". After the third review:
"one of the two men behind the restaurants", "his partner's daughter" and "his old partner", because no record
says partner, founder or owner and the game's words are "old friend"; "the partner, who built those machines",
because in 1979 the building is Murray's and the Cassette Man's own word is "making"; that the game inside the
game "works where fire did not", because one ending of Help Wanted 2 shows the rabbit crushed and RUIN, most likely the later of the two, still
has a rabbit's shadow in the mask; "the newest building", because no record calls the Pizzaplex new, the latest
or the largest; and UCN's lean, which is told in its own chapter and not set beside the other things that carry
something of the killer (R33). The order "fire, then scan" stays in the telling and is marked a convention.

**The saga's recurring ideas, with the records that let the telling lean on them.**

| Idea | Where the records carry it |
|---|---|
| Put back together | The promise to the dying child (`fnaf4-i-will-put-you-back-together`); the errand done (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-put-her-back-together`); the machines' own complaint (`sl-real-ending-they-always-put-us-back`); "I can put myself back together" (`scottgames-site-2017-fnafworld-source-ejection-dialogue-one`); the threat (`ucn-nightmare-fredbear-lines`); Special Delivery's Springtrap repeating it (`sd-springtrap-pulled-apart-put-back-together`); a 1987 attraction built on it (`fnaf2-call3-mangle`); the parable (`ffps-candy-cadet-five-kittens`) |
| He always comes back | `scottgames-fnaf3-teaser-he-always-does`, `ffps-scraptrap-always-come-back`, `ucn-afton-always-come-back`, the decoded line of `hw-mobile-princess-quest-ending`, and, in an adaptation the main games never place, `into-the-pit-yellow-rabbit-always-come-back` |
| Suits that are costume and machine; a person a machine cannot see, or takes for a machine | The suits: `fnaf3-tape2-dual-suits`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-springlocks-hen`. Cannot see: `security-breach-freddy-cannot-see-rabbit-lady`, `secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-suit-cannot-see`. Takes for an endoskeleton, which is seeing wrongly and not failing to see: `fnaf1-call1-stuffed-into-suit`, `fnaf2-call1-endoskeleton-glitch` (R19) |
| A room that is not on the map | `fnaf3-tape3-safe-room`, `fnaf3-tape6-safe-rooms-sealed`, `fnaf3-call1-boarded-room-lead`; the three faint clusters that Sister Location's breaker map draws and its Extras map leaves out (`sl-breaker-map-faint-clusters`, `sl-extras-map-entertainment-and-rental`); `secret-of-the-mimic-mockup-pizzeria-layout`. A door someone "poured concrete over" is a sealed room, like the false wall of the tapes, and the record says nothing of a map (`ruin-concrete-poured-over-door`). The Private Room itself is on the Extras map, labelled "Private": it is restricted, not unmapped |
| Copies | A program copied between bodies (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago`); personalities recreated from boards (`hw-handunit-vintage-control-boards`); a personality on a swappable part (`sd-cpu-whole-new-personality`); a borrowed voice twice (`hw-glitchtrap-uses-tape-girl-voice`, by a fan wiki's identification of the clip; `ruin-mimic-reuses-gregory-lines`, two lines), which is also the best support for reading the two speakers as one thing (R18); a company that restages its own crimes as product (`hw-tape-13-fe-hired-developer`, `hw2-fazerblast-fnaf-themed`) |
| The trusted voice that is not who it seems | Machines that speak as a friend: `sl-baby-i-kidnapped-you`; `secret-of-the-mimic-clock-out-real-dispatch`; `ruin-real-gregory-not-at-pizzaplex`. An unheard voice that a follower answers (`codb-mask-plush-voice`): the record does not say what it is. The reverse case is FNaF4's toy: a plush that talks, whose twin sits by a handset (`fnaf4-plush-locked-in-room`, `sl-private-room-handset-beside-plush`); on R5 that is a person speaking through a toy, at low-medium, and not a machine |
| The lure is a present | Ice cream (`sl-baby-ice-cream-story`); "gifts" pushed by a cartoon bear (`hw2-hub-helpi-pushes-gift`); "He's not really dead... Follow me." (`ffps-fruity-maze-follow-me`); a figure that beckons from behind a curtain at the end of a level built as a party (`hw-pizza-party-ending`: the room is the company's staging, the beckoning is the intrusion). Give Gifts, Give Life is not a lure: its gifts are what the Puppet gives the dead (R8) |
| Parents and the children they lose or use | The Cassette Man and his daughter (`ffps-completion-shut-out`); Afton and Elizabeth (`sl-girl-daddy-let-me-play`), Michael (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father`), the child whose rooms are on his facility's monitors (R3); Edwin Murray, who "let a machine raise my kid" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-refusal`); a father a therapist asks about by the name Bill (`security-breach-cd-vanessa-father-bill`); Cassie's father (`ruin-cassie-dad-faz-wrench`) |
| Rest for the victims, Hell for the killer | `ffps-completion-rest`, `ffps-completion-peace-perhaps-more`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`, `fw-clock-ending-pieces-in-place`; and `ucn-omc-ending`, where rest is offered to a listener who is not "the demon" (who that listener is, R16 leaves open, so the line is not a record of a victim offered rest) |

## 2. Rulings

### 2.0 Rules of weight used in every ruling

These are the standing rules the rulings below apply. Each is argued in the ruling named. After the third
review W1, W2, W5, W6 and W7 are reworded and W10 and W11 are new, so that one written test covers material
that helps the model and material of the same kind that does not.

| # | Rule | Argued in |
|---|---|---|
| W1 | Scott's written rulings bind: he ruled the first novel a separate continuity from the games; FNaF4 1.1 is canon and the Halloween Edition is not; Sister Location's Custom Night gameplay is not canon and its cutscenes are; Help Wanted is canon; a game that contradicts a theory wins. That books are not evidence here is this project's source policy and not a ruling of his: his later words about the story series point the other way (R27). | current `canon-rules`, kept; R27 |
| W2 | A later game made to mend an earlier one is the authority on the disputed point: FNaF4 is read through Sister Location, Sister Location through Pizzeria Simulator, Security Breach's base game through RUIN and what follows. A ruling that uses W2 names the later game and the point on which it contradicts the earlier one. | R27 |
| W3 | Ultimate Custom Night: gameplay and roster are staging; voice lines and cutscenes are text. | R16, R27 |
| W4 | FNaF World: the adventure is outside the story; the frame voice is a deliberate echo at support weight; never the only support for a fact. | R17 |
| W5 | A Fazbear product inside the story (the levels of Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear and Help Wanted 2) has three layers. **The level as built** is the company's staging: it shows what the company chose to stage and is not an observation of events. A staged prop may agree with what an unstaged record already carries; it is never the only support for a fact, and a date on it is never "printed". **The frame** around the levels (tapes, emails, the hidden call, what is seen with the mask off, the endings) is story. **Intrusions** are story too, attributed to whatever intrudes. A thing is an intrusion when the game marks it as not the company's: it is behind a secret, it glitches, it addresses the player, or it works against the level's own instructions. A level is demoted in this way only when the game that contains it frames it as a product inside the story. | R18, R27 |
| W6 | A tale that someone or something inside the story tells (Candy Cadet, UCN's cartoons, Mr. Hippo) can agree with the model and can never establish anything. | R25 |
| W7 | Cut, unused or data-only content (unused voice lines, data labels, unverified file names) is not evidence, and is not mentioned as agreeing or disagreeing with the model. A texture read outside play, whose place on screen nobody on the project has confirmed, has the status "texture, placement unconfirmed": it may be cited as that, and a date on it is not "printed" until it is seen in play. | R27 |
| W8 | A sprite's colour identifies nobody by itself: Scott ruled that the Purple Guy is not literally purple, and Sister Location says the machines took the son for the father. | R1 |
| W9 | Each game has a layer of things seen that are not there (FNaF1's "IT'S ME", FNaF3's phantoms, FNaF4's nights). What appears only in that layer is not thereby a physical object. | R4, R8 |
| W10 | Branches. An ending or an optional route is discounted for what it makes happen, never for what it shows to be already so. What does not depend on the branch counts on every branch. | R27; used in R3 and R19 |
| W11 | The standing of a whole work is decided by three questions, in this order: what Scott ruled in writing; what the work says of its own status; and whether the main line takes it up, or it takes up the main line in its author's own hand. How fantastical its content is, is never a test. | R27; used in R16, R17, R23 |

### R1. Is the purple killer William Afton, and how firm is each link in that chain?

**Ruling.** Yes. The man the minigames draw in purple, who dies inside the yellow rabbit suit, is the man
Pizzeria Simulator credits as William Afton, the "Mr. Afton" who designed the Funtime animatronics and the father
Michael addresses. The chain has five links and none is weaker than "strong".

| Link | Claim | Status | Records |
|---|---|---|---|
| a | The yellow rabbit suit snaps shut on the purple figure in a hidden room, and he bleeds and slumps (shown); he dies there (a firm inference: R12's rule); the thing later found after a lead about a boarded-up room, with what looks like a body inside it, is that suit | strong | `fnaf3-n5-springlock-death`, `fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`, `fnaf3-call1-boarded-room-lead`, `fnaf3-call2-found-a-real-one`, `fnaf3-springtrap-corpse-screens`, `fnaf3-springtrap-name`; Scott: `scottgames-fnaf3-teaser-still-here`, `scottgames-fnaf3-teaser-he-always-does`, `scott-2018-interview-purple-guy-in-springtrap` (a transcript) |
| b | FNaF3's Springtrap is Pizzeria Simulator's salvaged rabbit | strong | `ffps-scraptrap-always-come-back`, `ffps-scraptrap-still-me`, `ffps-scraptrap-alley-remains`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-springtrap`, `fnaf3-it-burns-springtrap-hidden` |
| c | That rabbit is William Afton | canon (stated): in Scott's written guide to Ultimate Custom Night, "William Afton (AKA Scraptrap)", verified at its source; on that game's roster, "Afton"; and on Pizzeria Simulator's cast list, "PJ Heywood - William Afton", a line read from a fan wiki's transcription of the credits, where the part is the salvaged rabbit by elimination (the list gives that voice no other name) | `scott-ucn-character-guide-afton`, `ucn-roster-afton`, `ffps-credits-william-afton`, `scott-2018-afton-name-for-ffps-springtrap` |
| d | William Afton is the designer "Mr. Afton", father of Michael and of the girl later named Elizabeth | strong | `sl-intro-mr-afton-board`, `sl-girl-daddy-let-me-play`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-springtrap`, `ffps-scrap-baby-make-you-proud-daddy`, `ffps-completion-connection-terminated-elizabeth` |
| e | The same man killed the child outside and the five. The join is not the sprites' colour (W8): it is the five ghosts who corner that figure, and the Cassette Man, who in one sentence vows to "undo what he has done" and to heal "a wound first inflicted on me", and who sends that man to Hell | strong | `fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`, `ffps-insanity-wound-first-inflicted`, `ffps-insanity-monster-helped-create`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`, `ffps-fruity-maze-follow-me`; the minigames that draw him: `fnaf2-take-cake-to-the-children`, `fnaf2-foxy-go-go-go` |

**Confidence.** High for the identification as a whole. Only link c is stated in words; the others are short
inferences that every later game relies on. Link c does not rest on the cast list alone: it would stand on
Scott's guide and the roster without it. The credit is where a game first prints the name, and it is cited as
a quoted line, with no tag (R27, "Cast lists", added in round 4d).

**Records against.** `fnaf1-news-suspect-captured` and `fnaf1-news-charged-vs-convicted` (a man was caught and
charged, yet the killer is free in every later game; handled in R8 and R11). `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-they-thought-i-was-you`
(the games' own statement that father and son can be confused).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the body in Springtrap is Michael ("Miketrap"). Scott listed it
among misunderstandings (`scott-2017-matpat-final-theory`, `scott-2017-misunderstood-not-reveals`) and removed its one
clue, the "robotic" voice (`scott-2017-michael-voice-not-robotic`), and his own guide names the occupant.

**What the writers must not do with it (W8).** Purple is a drawing convention. Scott called "Purpleguy being
literally purple" a misunderstanding (`scott-2017-matpat-final-theory`), and Michael rots purple in cutscenes Scott
ruled canon (`sl-custom-night-decay`). So: the purple man of FNaF2's and FNaF3's minigames is the killer (link e);
the purple man in FNaF4's back room is "most likely William" (`fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`), not "William";
the purple, decaying walker of Sister Location's cutscenes is Michael and is not the killer. The name "Purple Guy"
is printed in a game (`fw-purpleguy-sprite-disclaimer`) and is Scott's own word, so it may be used (R26).

**Would change if.** A game named someone else as the body in the suit, or gave "Mr. Afton" a different first name.

Review: group A found that the chain holds, and asked for two repairs, both made. Link e now rests on the ghosts
and on `ffps-insanity-wound-first-inflicted`, not on a sprite's colour; that record's own note says the wound is
not named, so "his daughter's death" stays an inference from the Completion speech. And
`scottgames-fnaf3-teaser-he-always-does` is tied to the purple figure only by the colour of its text: it is a
third citation for link a, and no chapter should lean on it.

**Depends on.** Nothing. **Carries.** R2 (W.A.), R3, R7, R8, R13, R16, R18, R20.

### R2. Are "Hen" and "W.A." the Cassette Man and William Afton? When is Secret of the Mimic set?

**Ruling.** Five separate answers.

1. **Secret of the Mimic's night falls in 1979, most likely on 7 September.** The year is on a Fazbear work
   order ("9/07/79"), which a fan wiki captions as found in the technician's van, and is bounded by a credential
   that expires in 1979. Both were read from images of props, and whether they can be read in play is unchecked. The year also ends the game's teaser and is the
   code that Help Wanted 2 hints at as "the distant past". The day has two supports, set out below, and either
   alone gives 7 September: the order, read month-first as the day of the job, and the gate mailbox, whose last
   mail came "five weeks ago" under the header "Aug 3rd". [CHANGE]
2. **Murray's Costume Manor was Fazbear Entertainment's contractor**, and on the night Fazbear claims everything
   Murray had been working on for it as "legally ours" and sends a man to fetch it. Stated: on the call on the
   road, by a voice that says "this is dispatch with Fazbear Entertainment", and on two work orders on Fazbear
   paper. [CHANGE] [Round 4d: where that voice gives way to a machine's is not stated; see the rule below]
3. **A party that signs "WA" and "W.A." says it now "technically" owns Murray's land, bids for his software
   development and his whole catalogue, asks an insider for his staff's contact details, and has franchise
   locations to give.** Stated, in letters that never say "Fazbear"; the letter about the land makes an offer, and
   no record shows Murray's answer. That letter opens mid-sentence and names no reader: that the reader is Murray
   is a short inference ("the house your parents built"). That the party is Fazbear Entertainment is an
   inference.
4. **"W.A." is William Afton.** An inference from the initials and the trade.
5. **"Hen" is the Cassette Man.** The model notes the possibility and does not tell it. The name itself carries
   no weight, because no game gives the Cassette Man a name for it to match.

The model tells the makers' story with 1 and 2 as fact; 3 as fact for what the letters say and "most likely" for
whose letters they are; 4 as "most likely"; 5 as an open question.

**Confidence.** (1) year high; day medium-high. (2) high. (3) what the letters say: high; that the party is
Fazbear Entertainment: medium-high. (4) medium. (5) low. That "the diner" of the springlocks message is Fredbear's
Family Diner: medium-high. That springlock suits were in use before the 1979 night: medium-high. That the
springlocks message was written while Fiona Murray was alive: medium. That Afton and the Cassette Man were
partners in the restaurant business: medium, and it is the model's reading. What the games state is less: the
Cassette Man calls the machines "prisons of my making", says he "unwillingly helped to create" a monster, and
calls the one he sends to Hell "old friend". No record says partner, founder, owner or business.

**Records for.**
- The year: `secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-1979` ("DATE: 9/07/79", typed in the header and handwritten beside the
  signature), `secret-of-the-mimic-credential-form-1979` ("EXPIRATION YEAR: 1979": an upper bound on the night, not
  a second printing of its date), `secret-of-the-mimic-teaser-1979`, `hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger` with
  `hw2-mystic-hippo-gift-distant-past`.
- The day: `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-foreclosure` ("Mail - Aug 3rd"; last mail "five weeks ago"). The game's other
  dated paper writes month first: `secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-memo-1974`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-order-forms-1973-1977`.
- The contract: the call on the road (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contractor-murray`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contract-legally-ours`); two work orders on Fazbear paper
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-1979`, "Extract Blueprints from MCM Facility";
  `secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-retrieve-assets`, "Retreive Fazbear Ent. assets from the premises"); Edwin's own
  "They treat me like some contractor" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-friends`; the log does not name "they", and
  that they are the people behind Fazbear Entertainment is a reading);
  `secret-of-the-mimic-edwin-pc-alt-fazbear-designs`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mockup-pizzeria-layout`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-rnd-proto-pizzeria`.
- The party signing W.A.: `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-confidential-wa`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-wa-far-behind`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-staff-contacts-wa`. That it is Fazbear: "considering how far behind you are" is a
  client's complaint, and the workshop's own memo fears "losing some of our best clients due to repeated
  tardiness" (`secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-memo-1974`); "new franchise locations" are a restaurant chain's to give;
  "legally ours", on the call on the road, is the same claim from a voice that names itself the company's
  dispatch; Edwin's "they" were friends before they were clients (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-friends`).
- W.A. as William Afton: the initials; the full name printed later (`ffps-credits-william-afton`); the trade, a
  designer of performing machines (`sl-intro-mr-afton-board`); no other W.A. in any game.
- Hen: Hen is to be warned about the new springlocks (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-springlocks-hen`) and sends a
  change order (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-build-to-spec`). No record gives Hen's sex; the rulings do not write
  "he". The Cassette Man's words are
  `ffps-insanity-small-souls-prisons`, `ffps-insanity-monster-helped-create`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`.
- The diner: two suits that the game's files label Fredbear and Spring Bonnie stand in Murray's mock-up
  pizzeria, and in one ending the technician wakes inside the first of them (`secret-of-the-mimic-rnd-proto-pizzeria`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-ending-clock-out`); the diner's name is on a poster among the game's textures
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-fredbears-diner-poster`); and the later games put a yellow bear and a yellow rabbit on
  one stage (`fnaf3-stage01-fredbear-spring-bonnie`) and call "the original restaurant" Fredbear's Family Diner
  (`fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner`). No game puts the two suits in a place of that name: the tapes' pair
  belongs to Freddy Fazbear's Pizza (`fnaf3-tape2-dual-suits`, `fnaf3-tapes-freddy-fazbears-pizza`).
- Springlocks in use before the night: Edwin's "It would be so much easier to keep using the Springlocks" and
  hire teens to wear the suits "like we always do" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-build-to-spec`).

**Records against.**
- No game writes either name out. `into-the-pit-strange-photo` names no one. `ffps-hry223-label` is unverified
  and is not evidence (W7).
- The date: the work order and the form were read from wiki images of props, not from play (W7's status
  "texture, placement unconfirmed").
- Hen: in Secret of the Mimic the making is Murray's. Edwin builds and Fiona designs; Hen is the customer. So
  "prisons of my making" fits the workshop at least as well as its client. `fnaf3-tape2-crank-from-manufacturer`
  speaks of "the manufacturer" of the suits and does not name it; taking that for an outside maker is a small
  reading, which supports the contract and cuts against the Cassette Man as their literal builder. `fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner` speaks of one "original restaurant owner", unnamed.
  `ffps-credits-cassette-man` gives him no name.
- The diner: `secret-of-the-mimic-ad-chicas-party-world`, a second eatery among Murray's clients. The poster's
  place in the game was not checked, so it carries the name and nothing about whether the place was open.
- "While Fiona was alive": see "What follows for dating".

**The two supports for 7 September, and the assumption under each.** Each support gives the day without the
other; the day is lost only if both assumptions fail. First, that the header "Mail - Aug 3rd" dates the last mail. The
game's other mailbox headers are timestamps of the message itself ("Admin - Dec 1st - 8:00 am":
`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-david-party`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-party-canceled`). By that convention the gate's
message was written on 3 August, the foreclosure notice came five weeks before it, in late June, and all the box
says of the night is that it falls on or after 3 August. Second, that a work order's date is the day of the job
and not the day it was issued. The best argument that both hold is the fit: 3 August to 7 September is exactly
35 days, and otherwise that is a coincidence. If the first fails, the day rests on the work order alone and the
mailbox no longer confirms it. If the second fails, the mailbox still puts the night about five weeks after 3
August. If both fail, the night is on or after the order's date and no day is known. [Corrected in round four:
this read "If either fails, the night is 'after 3 August 1979' and no day is known", which was wrong for each
case taken alone]

**The voice on the radio: one rule.** [Rewritten in round 4d, after three records were re-worded.] What is
stated: on the road a voice that names itself Fazbear's dispatch gives the contract and the errand
(`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contractor-murray`, `secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contract-legally-ours`); at
the cabinet F10-N4 the game says that this machine "had impersonated Dispatch"
(`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-reveal`); and in the ending where the technician walks away a Dispatch voice on the
van radio says "We've been trying to get ahold of you all night" and dismisses him
(`secret-of-the-mimic-clock-out-real-dispatch`). What is not stated: where the machine's voice takes over. The
usual reading is that the voice on the road and the voice that dismisses him are the company's, and that some or
all of what he heard in between was not; the records' notes say that this "is not stated" and "is not settled",
and that who speaks the briefings inside "is not certain" (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-endoskeleton-any-costume`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-retrieve-schematic`; a third briefing is filed under the cabinet's own lines,
`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-briefing-origin`). So the rule: inside the building a machine speaks as Dispatch
on his radio, and no record says where its voice takes over. The contract and the errand are quoted from the
call on the road, as stated there, and are confirmed on the company's paper: the blueprints, and its "assets".
Nothing said on the radio after that call is quoted as Fazbear's word, and nothing said before the cabinet's
reveal is quoted as the machine's: it is "the voice on the radio". The voice that dismisses him is "a
dispatcher's voice, most likely the company's own", and belongs to one ending of three. R20, R21 and R31 follow
this rule.

**Who made the mascots: not settled.** Murray's workshop makes springlock suits (stated); that the suits of
FNaF3's tapes are its work is "most likely". Unfinished prototypes
of Freddy, Bonnie, Chica and Foxy stand in his research floor in 1979 (shown; the "Proto" names are file labels).
The Cassette Man says the children are trapped in "prisons of my making" (stated). The games do not say how
those fit. He may be the client who specified them, which is the Hen possibility (low); he may have built later
machines on designs the company took; "my making" may mean the restaurants. The chapters say "the man who calls
those machines his own making", never "the man who built them" and never "his partner".

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *W.A. and Hen are two otherwise unknown people.* For W.A. it loses, narrowly: the initials, the full name
  printed later, the trade, and no other W.A. in any game. It does not lose for Hen, which is why 5 is low.
- *W.A. is Afton, writing for a firm of his own while Fazbear holds the contract.* The later games show Afton
  with his own company's name on his machines (`ffps-scup-blueprint`, "Afton Robotics, LLC", where the trap's
  hardware is marked "Fazbear Entertainment, Inc": `ffps-lefte-blueprint`), explaining them to an unnamed
  questioner, in a venture HandUnit calls "another contender in children's entertainment"
  (`sl-handunit-freddy-fazbear-closing`). No record calls him an owner, officer or founder of Fazbear
  Entertainment. So rulings 3 and 4 pull against each other: the more W.A. looks like the later Afton, the less
  sure it is that he writes for Fazbear. The alternative does not win, because "how far behind you are" is a
  client speaking, franchise locations are a restaurant chain's, and the call on the road makes the same claim
  that night. It is why 3's second half is medium-high and not high. On either reading Murray's work passes to the
  side W.A. is on.

**What follows for dating.** Firm: everything in Murray's logs and letters precedes the 1979 night, and
springlock suits were in use before it ("like we always do"). By that night Fazbear Entertainment has trained
technicians and "proprietary technology" (`secret-of-the-mimic-credential-form-1979`), and unfinished prototypes
of its four mascots stand in its contractor's building. Not firm: that the springlocks "for the diner" were
being readied while Fiona Murray was alive. The message is addressed "Fiona", but after the mother is gone a
voice speaks as Fiona in Edwin's logs, which the model reads as the first machine's (R21), and an employee
hears "Fiona's voice" with him (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-m1-as-fiona`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-heard-fiona`); the message is unsigned; and it sits in the mailbox of the mock-up
kitchen, on a research floor whose opening party a memo dates to 21 July of a 1970s year with its last digit
unread (`secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-rnd-floor-opening-memo`; the wiki reads 1975). If that floor opened in 1975 and
David Murray died that December (row 6's estimate), a note to the living Fiona in that kitchen leaves almost no
time for her death and for the years in which the first machine "watched him grow up"
(`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-miss-my-child`). One of four things gives: the note is to the machine; the mailbox
does not date the note; the floor opened earlier than the wiki reads; or David did not die in 1975. The model
picks none. So "while Fiona was alive" is medium, and "by the early 1970s" for the diner's springlocks is low.

**One thing shown and unexplained.** Chica's Party World is a Murray client with a costumed Chica in the 1970s
(`secret-of-the-mimic-ad-chicas-party-world`), and "ChcasPrtyWrld" is the first client in the directory Scott
hid in his 2016 page source for Sister Location (`scottgames-site-sl-source-client-directory`). It is the one
business named on both sides of 1979.

**Would change if.** A game printed either name in full, or showed a second W.A. For the day: the work order,
seen in play, proved unreadable or read otherwise.

**Depends on.** R1. **Carries.** The makers' chapter; the first rows of the dating table; R21.

Review: the third review asked for ten changes and all ten are made. The ruling had joined a stated claim (the
contract) to an inferred one (that the letters signed W.A. are Fazbear's), had called a role "exact" that no
record describes, and had told the two men as partners, which is the model's reading at medium and not a
record. Hen falls to low. The year is no longer called "printed twice". The research-floor memo, which the model
had not cited, is now used against two of its own dates.

### R3. Is FNaF4's household the Aftons? Who is the older brother? Who is the crying child?

**Ruling.** Yes, on a different footing from the current theory. [CHANGE: the argument, and the confidence]

Three statements, in descending order of certainty:

1. **Afton's facility has FNaF4's rooms on its monitors, with a Fredbear plush of FNaF4's design and a handset
   on the desk.** Shown, in
   Sister Location's own time. A restricted room in the Afton Robotics facility has a keypad that, on the code the
   wiki gives as 1-9-8-3, switches its monitors to FNaF4's gameplay bedroom, bed and Plushtrap hallway; a Fredbear
   plush of FNaF4's design sits on that desk with a handset in its lap; and on Night 2, in the main run of the
   game, the facility's panel restarts two systems labelled "Obsv.". That anyone watched from there in the week of
   the bite is not shown. It is an inference, and it has a difficulty of its own (see "The watching post and the
   year").
2. **The house is William Afton's own home, and the two boys are his sons.** Inference.
3. **Which sons: the older brother is the son Sister Location names Michael, and the crying child is a third
   Afton child, a younger son whom no game names.** This adds a step to 2, which could hold with the boys the
   other way round or with an elder son no game names; it follows from 2 and from R6.

**Confidence.** (1) high for what is on the screens and the desk in Sister Location's time. That the child was
watched from that desk in 1983: low-medium. (2) medium. (3) medium, lower end, for the assignment of the two boys (that the crying child is a son of Afton's
at all is statement 2, at medium; where one figure must stand for "Afton's younger son", use the lower). None of
this depends on who UCN's player is (R16). [Revised after review: (1) no longer says "kept that house under
observation"; (2) was "medium, upper end"; (3) was "medium"]

**Exactly what is shown and what is inferred** (for the chapter's wording):

| Item | Shown | Inferred |
|---|---|---|
| The room | A room marked "Private", restricted "due to the sensitive nature of the materials", in a facility whose map is signed Afton Robotics LLC (`sl-private-room-restricted`, `sl-extras-map-entertainment-and-rental`) | That William himself sat there |
| The code | Four key presses make the monitors change; the digits 1-9-8-3 rest on four wiki pages and capture titles, because the keypad has no display (`sl-private-room-keypad-1983`) | That 1983 is a year; it is FNaF4's only printed year (`fnaf4-fredbear-and-friends-1983`) |
| The monitors | Three still views with scan lines: the gameplay bedroom, the bed with its pinwheel quilt, the Plushtrap hallway, all empty, all seen from high in the room (`sl-private-room-monitor-bedroom`, `sl-private-room-monitor-bed`, `sl-private-room-monitor-hallway`, `sl-private-room-feeds-empty`) | That they are live; that anyone watched in 1983. The game shows the means and the place, in Sister Location's own time |
| The plush | A Fredbear plush of the same design (`sl-private-room-fredbear-plush`) | That it is the same object |
| The handset | A grey handset with antenna, keys and speaker grille in the plush's lap, unlabelled (`sl-private-room-handset-beside-plush`) | That someone spoke through the toy with it (R5) |
| The panel | "Obsv.1" and "Obsv.2" among eight systems to restart, in the canonical run (`sl-breaker-panel-obsv-systems`, `sl-breaker-map-obsv-identification`) | That "Obsv." means observation |
| The map | Faint clusters joined by lines to the facility; Obsv.2's line runs to the Private Room (`sl-breaker-map-faint-clusters`) | That Obsv.2 is FNaF4's house (a drawing-to-drawing match, `sl-breaker-map-obsv2-house-layout`, `fnaf4-house-map-cheat`); that Obsv.1 is the minigame neighbourhood (`sl-breaker-map-obsv1-layout`) |
| The dots | One white dot among grey dots in each cluster; inside the facility four grey dots sit in the four rooms where the animatronics are (`sl-breaker-map-dots`) | That white is a person and grey a machine. The reading does not hold up: the corridor cluster has five grey dots where FNaF4 has one Plushtrap (`sl-breaker-map-corridor-cluster`), and the technician is not marked on the night the map is shown. What the dots mean is open |
| One house | A clock in a picture-hung hallway in each: a grandfather clock with a pendulum in the 8-bit hallway, and a clock with a round face in a wooden case at the dark far end of the 3D Left Hall (`fnaf4-left-hall-grandfather-clock`). The two cannot be matched in detail, the still does not show whether the 3D clock stands on the floor, and the record rests on independent readers of the image, not on the analyst's own sight. The facility's map draws the 3D house (Obsv.2) and the 8-bit house with its street and restaurant (Obsv.1) as two separate clusters, each joined to the facility and each with one white dot in the bedroom's place (`sl-breaker-map-obsv1-layout`, `sl-breaker-map-obsv2-house-layout`, `sl-breaker-map-dots`) | That the minigame house and the gameplay house are the same home (`fnaf4-house-rooms`: the game never says so). The clock is support, not a pillar. Do not write "the same grandfather clock" |

**How much rests on "one house".** Statement 1 is about the rooms on the monitors, which are the 3D rooms of the
nights. The family (two boys, a pink bedroom, a countdown to a birthday) lives in the 8-bit house of the
minigames. Statement 2 joins them. It does not need the clock: the desk whose monitors show the 3D bedroom holds the
8-bit house's plush (`sl-private-room-fredbear-plush`), the facility's other "Obsv." system has the plan of the
8-bit house, its street and the restaurant (`sl-breaker-map-obsv1-layout`, a reading of a drawing), and the game
itself runs the nights and the countdown as one child's week (`fnaf4-minigame-countdown-to-party`). So Afton's
company is tied to the household whether or not the two houses are one building. What stays open is whether the
3D bedroom is that child's own bedroom or a second place built to its plan; two clusters on the map fit either.

**The watching post and the year.** Sister Location shows the apparatus in its own time, which is years after
FNaF4's week. The Private Room belongs to "Circus Baby's Entertainment & Rental"
(`sl-extras-map-entertainment-and-rental`), a business HandUnit dates after "the unfortunate closing of Freddy
Fazbear's Pizza" (`sl-handunit-freddy-fazbear-closing`), and the model's own order puts that closing and that
business after 1983 (section 3.2, steps 10 and 11, both by convention against step 6). No record makes the room
older than the business. So "the child was watched in 1983" needs one of two things: a watching post older than
the business that houses it, or the five, a Freddy's closing and Circus Baby's venture all before 1983. No record
chooses, and the model does not: it rates "watched in 1983" low-medium and says what it costs. What speaks for it
is the code, which is FNaF4's year, and that Scott built the room to say something about FNaF4. R12's convention
is the other party to this problem. [Round 4d: the verb is "leans". The model leans to the watching in 1983,
on the code alone, in the chapter on the household, because R4's and R5's leans need it; the order of events
does not tell it (section 3.2, "Three sequences the model does not tell"). "The model takes the step" and "the
model does not tell it" are both true, of the chapter and of the order, and a text that has one should have
the other in the same place.]

**Records for statement 2.**
- Statement 1 itself: the facility that has those rooms on its monitors, and that toy on its desk, is Afton's.
  Cameras high in a child's bedroom and over his bed are easy only in one's own house
  (`sl-private-room-monitor-bed`, `sl-private-room-feeds-empty`).
- The family's shape is compatible, no more. Sister Location gives Afton one son and one daughter
  (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father`, `sl-girl-daddy-let-me-play`). FNaF4's house holds two boys and a second
  bedroom with a pink bed that nobody is shown using (`fnaf4-second-bedroom`, `fnaf4-house-rooms`), and no parent
  ever appears (`fnaf4-no-parent-shown`). The fit needs a third Afton child whom no game mentions.
- The child is left alone at the restaurant, and two days later is shut in a staff parts room; by whom is not
  shown (`fnaf4-left-behind-at-diner`, `fnaf4-locked-backstage`). A purple man is at work in that back room
  (`fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`).
- "Put back together" is the plush's promise and, later, the errand Afton gives his son
  (`fnaf4-i-will-put-you-back-together`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-put-her-back-together`): an echo, not an
  identification.
- Sister Location is the game about Afton's children, and Scott agreed, when an interviewer put it to him, that
  he had put things into it to ground FNaF4's story; he did not say which things, and that they include what
  the game shows of FNaF4's house is the archive's reading (`scott-2018-interview-sl-made-to-ground-story` (a transcript), `scott-2017-sl-cleared-fnaf4-misconception`,
  `scott-2018-interview-each-game-fixed-the-last` (a transcript)). The Private Room and the panel are the only places
  this archive has found where Sister Location points at FNaF4. He never said what the misconception was.

Two things are no longer counted. The plush's talk about the brother does not separate "his own family" from "a
household he watched", since any watcher would know what the brother does, and two of the three lines once cited
are not the toy speaking on R5's own account. And Midnight Motorist's purple car is a colour and nothing else
(R29).

**A loose end.** The only "Daddy" in FNaF4 belongs to another child, and he knows the rabbit's springs: "He is a
finger trap, he says" (`fnaf4-spring-bonnie-plush-finger-trap`). No ruling places that child or that father.

**Records for statement 3.** The games name one Afton son, by his own mouth (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father`).
The brother's last words are remorse (`fnaf4-night6-brother-apology`); the grown Michael goes underground on his
father's errand for his dead sister and it costs him his body (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-put-her-back-together`,
`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-come-find-you`).

**Records against.** `sl-private-room-feeds-empty` (nobody is on the monitors; nothing shows when they were made);
`fnaf4-house-rooms` (the game never states that the two houses are one, and the facility's map draws them as two
clusters); `sl-handunit-freddy-fazbear-closing` (the business that houses the room postdates a Freddy's closing);
`fnaf4-second-bedroom` (two bedrooms for what the model counts as three children); no record names the family;
the keypad digits rest on wiki text. Two lines often used here carry no weight: "I know it was an accident"
(`sl-fake-ending-girl-voice`), spoken by a machine that takes Michael for his father and fitting the daughter's
death at least as well as the Bite; and "birthday boy" (`sl-funtime-freddy-bon-bon-birthday-boy`), a party
machine's stock phrase.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.** Statement 1 is equally true on both.
- *A household under study.* Afton watched, or experimented on, a family that was not his. It loses on balance
  only: cameras over a child's bed are easiest in one's own house, the promise the plush makes comes back as the
  father's errand, and Sister Location is otherwise wholly about Afton's children. That is a medium case, and the
  chapter names this alternative.
- *Rooms built to be observed.* The label "Obsv."; systems that run off the facility's own breaker panel; a line
  from each cluster to the facility; one white dot in each; a third cluster that is only the Plushtrap corridor
  (`sl-breaker-map-corridor-cluster`); empty rooms; and Scott's trailer, "DEEP BELOW GROUND / WHERE MEMORIES
  SLEEP" (`sl-trailer-deep-below-ground`). On this reading the 3D rooms are a place attached to the facility, and
  the difficulty about 1983 disappears. Its price is that the nights are no longer the nights of the 1983 week,
  which R4 clause 2 needs. The model does not adopt it and cannot exclude it.

A caution about two of the supports. "A girl's room" and "a child with the run of the restaurant" would fit the
Cassette Man's household as well as Afton's: R2 ties "the diner" to "Hen", and he too has a daughter. No record
supports that reading. It is mentioned to show that those two supports pick out nobody.

**The branch.** The Private Room is entered only on the night the Real Ending overrides. The room, its desk and
its wiring belong to the facility on either branch, and the breaker panel is in the main run (R27).

**What the current theory did.** It rested the family on the purple car and on "a joint hypothesis through UCN"
(Michael as UCN's player, the crying child as the one he should not have killed). The first is a motif. The second
falls with R16. Neither is needed.

**Would change if.** A game showed a different family in that house, or showed the Private Room's views to be
something other than that house, or dated the facility.

Review: all nine of group A's findings on this ruling are accepted. Statement 1 is cut back to what is shown, in
Sister Location's time; "watched in 1983" becomes an inference with its own confidence and its own problem; the
bullet about a "he" who belongs to the restaurant misdescribed two records and is rewritten; the plush's
household talk, the purple car and the sentence about "nothing to correct" are withdrawn; the family's shape is
called compatible; the second alternative and the trailer record are added; statement 2 goes from "medium, upper
end" to "medium" and statement 3 to "medium, lower end". Statement 3 never said why Michael is the bully and not
the boy who was bitten: the bitten boy dies (R6) and Michael lives to be scooped (R13).

**Depends on.** R1; R6 (for statement 3); R27 (the branch); R12 (for "watched in 1983"). **Carries.** R4, R5, R6,
R13.

### R4. Whose nights does FNaF4's gameplay show, and what are the Nightmares? Which bite is FNaF4's?

**Ruling.**

1. **The bedroom is a real place: Afton's facility has it on its monitors in Sister Location's time.** Whether
   the nights are waking events in that room or the child's nightmares in that bed, the games do not settle. The
   model leans to waking events. [CHANGE: the current theory offers "a patient's nightmares or a memory" and omits
   the reading its own later game supplies]
2. **The one in the bed is the crying child, on the nights of his last week.**
3. **What he fights is not what is there.** The games' own word for it, later, is "illusion". What, if anything,
   stood in those halls is not identified.
4. **FNaF4's bite is not the Bite of '87.** It belongs to the week whose only printed year is 1983.

**Confidence.** (1) high that the room is a real place on the facility's monitors in Sister Location's time;
low-medium that the nights are waking events in it; whether the facility could watch it in the week of the bite
is R3's open problem. (2) medium, lower end. (3) medium that the Nightmares are not physical as seen; low for any
account of what was physically present; low that the illusion was William Afton's doing. (4) high. [Revised
after review: "the nights are events in it" was medium; in the second round (2) was medium and "William's doing"
low-medium]

**Which nights the model counts as story.** Nights 1 to 5 alternate with the countdown days and end before the
party: the child's last five nights (clause 2). Night 6 comes after the bite, when he cannot be at home in bed;
the model reads it as the same terror continuing as he dies, and rates that low. Nights 7 and 8 follow the scene
in which he fades and carry no story text but the locked box (`fnaf4-locked-box-ending`,
`fnaf4-night8-no-story-text`): they are challenge nights, outside the sequence.

**Records for.**
- The room: R3, statement 1. The cameras look down from high corners, positions the player never has
  (`sl-private-room-feeds-empty`). This shows that the bedroom is a place. It does not show that what is played
  there happened.
- The child: the nights alternate with a five-day countdown to his birthday (`fnaf4-minigame-countdown-to-party`,
  `fnaf4-its-your-birthday`); the room is a small child's (`fnaf4-bedroom-setting`); Scott's store text makes the
  player "a child whose role is yet unknown" (`scott-2015-fnaf4-store-child-role-unknown`).
- Not what is there: Scott's trailer, "What is it that you think you see? What game do you think you are playing?
  What have you brought home?" (`fnaf4-trailer-what-you-think-you-see`); UCN's Nightmare Fredbear, "This time, there
  is more than an illusion to fear" (`ucn-nightmare-fredbear-lines`); Nightmare Freddy, "I am remade, but not by you"
  (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`); the between-nights game has its hallway on the monitors too
  (`sl-private-room-monitor-hallway`, `fnaf4-fun-with-plushtrap`).
- The bite: `fnaf4-fredbear-and-friends-1983`, `sl-private-room-keypad-1983`, `scottgames-site-fnaf4-html-83` with
  `scottgames-site-fnaf4-html-87` (the digits hidden in Scott's page source went from 8 and 7 to 8 and 3 in the
  two weeks before release; that they point at the two years is the usual reading, and he has not said so),
  `fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner` (the original restaurant "closed for years" by November 1987),
  `fnaf4-give-fredbear-a-kiss`, `fnaf4-the-bite`. Scott treated "1983 vs 1987" as a misunderstanding
  (`scott-2017-matpat-final-theory`); cite that record for that and no more, since which way it resolves is in a
  theorist's video and not in his words.

**Records against.** For (1): the game's own name for the attackers is "Nightmare" (`fnaf4-nightmare-animatronics`);
`fnaf4-bedside-items` (rare flowers, pills and a drip by the bed, not limited to the nights after the bite: a
hospital bed, or a child dying); `fnaf4-nightmare-fredbear-4am` and `fnaf4-night6-fade-order` (Night 6 falls after
the bite); two more nights follow the scene in which the child fades (`fnaf4-locked-box-ending`,
`fnaf4-night8-no-story-text`); a reversed fragment of FNaF1's first call, from a later year, can be heard in the
room (`fnaf4-reversed-phone-guy`); `sl-private-room-feeds-empty`; `scottgames-site-2019-fnafworld-58-mock-quotes`
(Scott knew the dream reading and only joked about it). Against (4): `fnaf4-toy-collection-girl` and
`fnaf4-mangle-pieces-in-house` (toys that look like the 1987 models), which read as deliberate misdirection.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the nights are a dream or a coma: the dying child's, or the
brother's afterwards. It explains the bedside items, the name "Nightmare" and Night 6, which the model's reading
does not explain well. It loses as the whole account because Scott said FNaF4 could not be solved from itself
(`scott-2018-interview-fnaf4-too-broad` (a transcript)) and then built a game in which that bedroom is a place on a
company's monitors. That answers "there was no such room". It does not answer "he dreamt it in that room", which
is why the model's lean is only low-medium.

**Note on the two clusters.** The facility's map draws the house of the nights (Obsv.2) apart from the 8-bit house
with its street and restaurant (Obsv.1), and the Plushtrap hallway as a third cluster
(`sl-breaker-map-corridor-cluster`). That fits one home drawn at two scales, and it fits a home plus separate
watched rooms built to its plan (R3's second alternative). "A real place on the facility's monitors" is true on
both; "the child's own bedroom at home" is the model's reading (medium), and the only fixture the two houses
visibly share is a clock in a hallway (`fnaf4-left-hall-grandfather-clock`, read from a dark still by independent
readers).

**What is open: the panel's dots.** One white dot sits in the bedroom's place with four grey dots round it
(`sl-breaker-map-dots`). The model no longer cites this for anything. Grey is not "one machine" (five grey dots
for one Plushtrap), white is not "a person present" (the technician is unmarked), and the map is drawn in Sister
Location's present while the monitors show empty rooms. Do not write that the nights are happening during Sister
Location.

**Would change if.** A game showed what the dots are, or showed the bedroom as a set built somewhere other than a
home.

Review: all five of group A's findings are accepted. The camera proves the room and not the nights, so "waking
events" drops to low-medium and the records that favour a dream are now listed; the nights the model counts are
named; the dots move from "records for" to "open"; clause 1 is put in Sister Location's time; and
`scott-2017-matpat-final-theory` is cited only for what Scott himself said. The dependence on R16 is wider than
the old text admitted: with Michael in UCN's office, "more than an illusion to fear" reads most naturally as said
to someone who once faced the illusion, and the nights would be the brother's.

Review: in the second round R16 was re-rated, and this ruling moves with it, as both reviewers said it must.
"The illusion was William's doing" needs William in UCN's office, which is now a lean at low-medium, so it falls
to low. Clause 2 keeps its own support (the countdown, the room, Scott's store text) and falls only to "medium,
lower end": with Michael in that office, "this time, there is more than an illusion to fear" would be said to
the one who faced the illusion before, and the nights would read as the brother's.

**Depends on.** R3; clause 2, clause 3's word "illusion" as applied to the child, and "the illusion was
William's" all lean on R16. **Carries.** R5, R6.

### R5. Who speaks through the Fredbear plush?

**Ruling.** The model's reading is a watching, talking toy worked from Afton's side: the games put its twin, with
a handset, on the desk of the room whose monitors show the child's bedroom, in Sister Location's time. The
speaker would be "whoever sits at that desk", and nothing says who that is. The child's own mind is the named
alternative, and it explains every line with one mechanism. [CHANGE: the current answer, "nothing identifies the
voice as his father's", misses that the games put the toy and a handset on the father's desk; its mechanism (a
self-acting companion) is replaced by the handset]

**Confidence.** Low-medium that the plush is a device spoken through from the watching side. Low that the voice
is William's own. [Revised after review: were medium and medium-low]

**Records for.** The desk: `sl-private-room-fredbear-plush`, `sl-private-room-handset-beside-plush`,
`sl-private-room-keypad-1983`. Inside the house, two lines know what the brother is about to do: "You know he is
hiding again. He won't stop until you find him." (`fnaf4-plush-he-is-hiding-again`), and "Be careful.", just
before the brother comes out from under the bed (`fnaf4-plush-be-careful`). Scott's "What have you brought home?"
(`fnaf4-trailer-what-you-think-you-see`). The promise "I will put you back together"
(`fnaf4-i-will-put-you-back-together`) is later an errand William sets his son
(`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-put-her-back-together`): an echo, and no more.

**Records against.**
- Most of the plush's lines are not the toy speaking, on this ruling's own account. Its text appears inside the
  restaurant, where no plush is drawn (`fnaf4-left-behind-at-diner`, `fnaf4-he-hates-you`,
  `fnaf4-plush-follows-child`), and its first lines appear on a black screen over the eyeless sprite
  (`fnaf4-plush-locked-in-room`, `fnaf4-plush-eyes-by-scene`). The lines appeal to what the child already knows:
  "You know what will happen if he catches you", "Don't you remember what you saw?"
  (`fnaf4-fredbear-suit-employee-chase`, `fnaf4-dont-you-remember-what-you-saw`).
- One voice: the text has one colour and one manner throughout (`fnaf4-speaker-text-colours`). The device reading
  needs two mechanisms, a radio toy at home and a remembered voice elsewhere. The child's own mind needs one.
- In the same scene in which the child can glimpse the purple man in the back room, the plush tells him which
  way to run. The records do not order the two: the figure is "seen only on backtracking", and the line fires
  "if the child walks the wrong way" (`fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`,
  `fnaf4-dont-you-remember-what-you-saw`). So this is only weak support for "the speaker is not that man". Its tone is protective (`fnaf4-tomorrow-is-another-day`).
- The handset carries no label: a radio, a phone or a remote would all look like it
  (`sl-private-room-handset-beside-plush`). It is seen in Sister Location's time, in a facility that the model's
  own order does not show to exist in 1983 (R3).
- FNaF World gives both of the plush's closing sentences to two bodiless dots (`fw-opening-put-you-back-together`,
  `fw-clock-ending-still-your-friends`).

**The pupils.** The plush has pupils that follow the child wherever it shares a room of the house with him, and
none on the opening black screen or outdoors (`fnaf4-plush-eyes-by-scene`, `fnaf4-plush-two-sprites`). It has them
at Night 6 too, in a scene on black, after the bite, when he cannot be at home (`fnaf4-plush-night6-pupils-only`,
`fnaf4-night6-fade-order`). So the pupils mark the plush as an object that is there and looking at him. They are
not a sign that someone is working it, and the model no longer uses them as one. Whether the toys went with him
after the bite, nothing shows.

**Strongest alternative, and where it stands.** The child's own mind. It covers every line with one mechanism,
including the lines spoken where no toy could be. On the lines alone the two readings are level. It loses on one
thing: the same toy sits with a handset on a desk in Afton's facility, beside monitors that show that bedroom, in
the game Scott made to ground FNaF4. That is why the model still prefers the device, and why it prefers it only
at low-medium.

**Errors to remove.** The Night 6 promise is not spoken "over nothing but two white dots": the plush is whole and
looking at the child (`fnaf4-plush-night6-pupils-only`, `fnaf4-night6-fade-order`); the two-dots screen is FNaF
World's. The colour argument for a second speaker at Night 6 fails on measurement (`fnaf4-youre-broken`,
`fnaf4-speaker-text-colours`). Outdoors the plush has no pupils, so the chapter's "Its pupils follow him
from furniture, cars, flowers and drains" is wrong for the flowers and the yard.

**Would change if.** A game showed who holds the handset, or showed the plush speaking with no one at the desk.

Review: five of group A's six findings are accepted in full. The evidence is rebuilt on the two lines spoken
inside the house; the pupils are no longer a sign of a toy being worked; the handset is called unlabelled and
late; `scottgames-site-fnaf4-property-of-fr` is dropped, because a hat and a bow tie do no work here; and "put
you back together" is an echo, not "the father's phrase", since a father's promise over a dying son c. 1983 would
start his project before the five on the model's own order. The finding that the child's own mind should stand
level with the device is accepted for the lines and for the confidences, which fall to low-medium and low. It is
not accepted as the ruling: only the device reading accounts for the twin and the handset on Afton's desk, so
that stays the model's lean.

**Depends on.** R3, R4. **Carries.** "Put you back together" as an echo between the plush's promise and the
father's later errand (thesis colour only).

### R6. What happens to the crying child after the bite?

**Ruling.** He dies. After that no record places him anywhere: the promise to put him back together is never
shown kept. The model does not put him in Golden Freddy, and it no longer claims he is UCN's accuser.
[CHANGE: the current theory identifies him with "the one you should not have killed" and calls him flatly "a
different figure" from Golden Freddy; both statements go]

**Confidence.** Medium-high that he dies. Low for every account of what follows.

**Records for the death.** The plushies fade one by one, then the plush, then the child (`fnaf4-night6-fade-order`);
"You're broken." (`fnaf4-youre-broken`); a flatline tone, held at `implied` because it rests on a description of the
audio (`fnaf4-flatline`); his absence from every later scene.

**The four leads, none conclusive.**
- FNaF World's frame voice repeats the promise, lays out FNaF3's path to Happiest Day, and ends "Rest."
  (`fw-opening-put-you-back-together`, `fw-clock-games-five-tasks`, `fw-clock-game-395248`,
  `fw-clock-ending-still-your-friends`, `fw-clock-ending-pieces-in-place`). In 2016, then, the promise pointed at
  FNaF3's freeing of the children, the last of whom takes a golden bear's mask (`fnaf3-happiest-day`). Support
  weight only (R17), and the trophy it awards is drawn as the generic weeping child of FNaF2 and FNaF3, not as
  FNaF4's boy (`fw-crying-child-character`).
- UCN ties its accuser to FNaF4's Nightmares and Golden Freddy to Fredbear (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`,
  `ucn-nightmare-fredbear-lines`, `ucn-death-coin-fredbear`). Under R16 these no longer need the crying child.
- He was bitten by a golden bear.
- On R11's reading it is the same suit. R11 makes the yellow bear suit of FNaF1 and FNaF2 the second of the two
  springlock suits, which is the stage bear (`fnaf3-stage01-fredbear-spring-bonnie`, `fnaf4-the-bite`). So the suit
  that the model gives to Golden Freddy is the one that bit him. This holds only as far as R11 does (low-medium).

**Records against "no place".** The same four leads, read together, are the best case that the crying child and
the yellow bear's child are one, or share it. The count is what stops the model: wherever the dead are numbered,
the yellow bear takes the fifth place among five children "linked to the incident" at the pizzeria, and the crying
child was never one of those (R8).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** One presence that is both: he is in Golden Freddy, alone or beside
the fifth child. After R16 this is the strongest alternative, and the chapter should name it as such. With
William in UCN's office, UCN's own links run from the accuser to FNaF4: one Nightmare is "remade... by the one
you should not have killed", Nightmare Fredbear throws "put you back together" at the player, and the Death Coin
used on Golden Freddy brings Fredbear. On their face those records lean toward this child, and R16's own table
admits that nothing ties the fifth child to FNaF4's Nightmares but the colour of a bear. It would also make "put
you back together" a kept promise. It loses on the count, which the review tried and could not break: five
linked to the incident, five ghosts, six graves, six dolls, and the crying child is not one of the five. No scene
joins him to any machine. It is the chapter's open alternative, not dismissed.

**A loose end.** Nights 7 and 8 are played after the scene in which he fades (R4).

**Would change if.** A game showed the FNaF4 child after Night 6, in any form.

Review: group A found that the ruling holds and asked for three additions, all made: the fourth lead (R11's bear
is the bear that bit him), the name and the standing of "one presence that is both", and the note on Nights 7
and 8.

**Depends on.** R3, R8, R11 (the fourth lead), R16.

### R7. The Cassette Man's daughter and the Puppet: who, where, and when relative to the five?

**Ruling.** The child killed outside in FNaF2's Take Cake to the Children is the child of Pizzeria Simulator's
Security Puppet minigame, and she is the Cassette Man's daughter. She persists in the Puppet. William Afton
killed her. The model places her death before the five, as a convention with one sentence behind it. [CHANGE:
the current theory says the order "is not given"]

**Confidence.** Same child, and his daughter: medium-high. In the Puppet: medium-high. Before the five:
low-medium, and a convention. Where and in what year: unknown. [Revised after review: "before the five" was
medium]

**Records for.** Two minigames show one event from two sides (`fnaf2-take-cake-to-the-children`;
`ffps-security-puppet-assignment`, `ffps-security-puppet-trapped`, `ffps-security-puppet-dead-child`); the father
describes it while the terminal shows it (`ffps-completion-shut-out`, `ffps-completion-security-puppet-terminal`,
`ffps-completion-my-daughter`); what became of her (`ffps-completion-not-content-to-disappear`,
`ffps-completion-rest`); the machine built to catch a security puppet that answers to a child's bracelet and is
soothed by a lullaby (`ffps-lefte-blueprint`, `ucn-roster-lefty-music-box`).

**The order: one sentence.** "A wound first inflicted on me, but then one that I let bleed out to cause all of
this" (`ffps-insanity-wound-first-inflicted`). The model reads it as: his loss came first, and the rest followed.
The record's own note calls that step an inference, and "all of this" is not "the five". The two other records
once cited here do not order her death against theirs. Give Gifts shows the Puppet handing masks to four children
who are already dead (`fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life`), which puts her death before the masks, not before the
killings. The missing tear streaks (`ffps-security-puppet-no-tear-streaks`) order her death against the Puppet's
look in FNaF2 and say nothing about the five.

**Records against.** The voice in Take Cake spells S-A-V-E-H-I-M (`fnaf2-take-cake-to-the-children`): FNaF2's
child is a "him", Pizzeria Simulator's is a daughter. Withered Chica says "I was the first"
(`ucn-withered-chica-toysnhk`; R25 reads that as first of the five). Help Wanted 2's graveyard is solved in an
order that begins with Chica and ends with the Puppet (`hw2-pq4-graveyard-order`). If that is an order of deaths
the two records agree with each other, and against this ruling.

**One rule for `hw2-pq4-graveyard-order`.** The game does not say what the order orders. The model uses the
order for nothing, in this ruling or in R25: not as support for "Chica's child was first", and not as proof that
