the Puppet's child was last. It stays on file as counter-evidence here. The record's other fact, six stones
paired with the six dolls, is a count and not an order; R8, R9 and R25 cite it for the count only.

**One rule for FNaF2's four death minigames** (Take Cake, Foxy Go! Go! Go!, Give Gifts, SAVE THEM). They are
flashes shown between deaths. The model uses what they depict: who dies, who kills, what the Puppet gives. It
draws nothing from the building they are set in, or from which machine the scene is seen through. So Take Cake
does not locate her death, and it does not show whether anyone was yet in Freddy when she died; SAVE THEM does
not show that five more children died in the 1987 building (R9), or who was on that building's staff.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That FNaF2's child outside is a different victim, a boy. It keeps
"save him" literal. It loses because Pizzeria Simulator, the game Scott made "to resolve some plot points"
(`scott-2018-interview-ffps-made-to-resolve-plot-points` (a transcript)), restages that exact scene from the Puppet's
side while the father narrates it. "Him" stays on file as a tension.

**Where.** No game names the place. Do not write "outside Fredbear's".

**Would change if.** A game dated either death, or showed the Puppet active before her death.

Review: group A asked for "before the five" to drop to low-medium and to be called a convention, as R12 calls
Elizabeth's place on one sentence of HandUnit's. Accepted, together with the single rule for the graveyard order
(R25 no longer counts it as a pointer) and the removal of "that is not the 1987 building", which identified a
building from a sprite. One inference of the reviewer's is not adopted: that Take Cake's Freddy must already be
"someone" when the child outside dies. That follows only if the viewpoint of a death minigame is evidence, and
the rule above says it is not, for any of the four.

**Depends on.** R1. **Carries.** R8 (order), R9 (the rule for the minigames), R14, the first row of the murders in
the dating table.

### R8. The five children: when, where, which suit, and is Golden Freddy the fifth?

**Ruling.**

1. **What happened.** On the night of a June 26th two children were reported lured into a back room at Freddy
   Fazbear's Pizza by a man whom police think dressed as a company mascot. A later clipping links five children
   to the incident. The bodies were never found. A man was identified on video, caught the next morning and
   charged. All stated.
2. **When.** Before the 1987 restaurant opened, most naturally a year or more before. The year is not printed.
   The model prints a bracket: a June 26th between 1980 and 1986 (R24, point 7).
3. **Where.** The older Freddy Fazbear's Pizza, the "old location" of FNaF2's calls, in its hidden back room. That
   building is probably the one FNaF1 later reopens in (R12).
4. **Which suit.** Most likely the yellow rabbit, Spring Bonnie. [CHANGE: stated flat in the current theory]
5. **The bodies.** The mascots carried the signs of it: a foul smell, and what looked like blood and mucus round
   their eyes and mouths. The model reads this as the bodies having been put into the four mascots. Where the
   bodies went is never shown, and they were never found.
6. **The man who was caught.** Who he was is open. He may have been the killer, charged and then free because
   there were no bodies; he may have been the wrong man. The model prints the arrest and builds nothing on
   either reading. [CHANGE: the arrest is absent from the current murders chapter]
7. **Golden Freddy.** In the counts of the dead that the table below lists there are four and one more, and in
   four of the seven the one more is marked by the yellow bear. In the other three the fifth is unmarked or
   hidden; two further scenes show five alike, and one screen shows four and no fifth. The model reads the yellow bear as the fifth child's. How and when that child came to it is
   the one thing every scene withholds. [Kept, re-examined; the sentence "bound to a golden bear like the other
   four were to theirs" goes]

**Confidence.** (1) high. (2) medium-high for "before the 1987 restaurant". That alone excludes only a June
inside the new restaurant's life, which leaves the June of 1987. The model takes the June of 1986 as the latest
on one more step: the old restaurant traded on after the incident (`fnaf1-news-uphill-battle`) and had then
been "left to rot for quite a while" before the new one opened in the autumn of 1987
(`fnaf2-call1-new-location`). The records give a bracket; 1986 is where the model takes it to end. The floor,
1980, is soft (low-medium): the four are still unfinished prototypes on the 1979 night
(`secret-of-the-mimic-rnd-proto-pizzeria`), though the same room holds prototypes that the files label Fredbear
and Spring Bonnie, whose suits were most likely already in use, so a prototype does not show that no
restaurant had the character yet. A year before 1983 is allowed, and "mid-1980s" is no longer the
model's label, since nothing but a logo graphic in an unplaced adaptation supports it (R23). (3)
medium-high for the old location, medium for the safe room. (4) medium. (5) high that the mascots carried the
signs; medium that the bodies were put into them. (6) low for either reading. (7) medium-high for the pattern of
four and one; medium that the yellow bear is that fifth child; low for any mechanism. [Revised after review: (5)
was medium-high; (6) was "the killer, freed", low-medium; (7) was one figure, medium-high]

**Records for.**
- (1) `fnaf1-news-kids-vanish`, `fnaf1-news-five-children`, `fnaf1-news-dressed-as-mascot`,
  `fnaf1-news-suspect-captured`, `fnaf1-news-charged-vs-convicted`.
- (2) and (3): the company's bad name belongs to the old location and predates the new restaurant
  (`fnaf2-call1-forget-old-location`); the new restaurant is sold on machines that "can detect a predator a mile
  away" (`fnaf2-call1-criminal-database`); the older models smell and walk before anything happens in the 1987 week
  (`fnaf2-call2-the-smell`, `fnaf2-call2-older-models`, `fnaf2-old-models-walk-from-night2`); the masks handed out are the four
  old characters' (`fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life`); June 26th cannot fall inside a restaurant open "only a few short
  weeks" before mid-November (`fnaf2-overtime-robots-scrapped`); the restaurant traded on afterwards
  (`fnaf1-news-uphill-battle`, `fnaf1-news-sanitation`), closed, and was "left to rot for quite a while"
  (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`); "the tragedy that took place there" (`fnaf1-news-closing`).
- (4) The one luring the games show has a grinning yellow figure in costume behind a crying girl, with the look
  of the yellow rabbit suit and its ears out of frame, and gives the lie that was told: her dog is "not really
  dead... Follow me" (`ffps-fruity-maze-follow-me`, `ffps-fruity-maze-decay`; that the figure is the rabbit suit
  is most likely, and whether anyone is inside it is not shown); he runs to the yellow rabbit suit at the end
  (`fnaf3-n5-springlock-death`); a purple man fits the head of a yellow rabbit suit on someone in 1983, in a
  game that puts no name on the suit (`fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`); the room is off camera and invisible to the animatronics
  (`fnaf3-tape3-safe-room`). The Night 5 tape puts two reminders side by side: no customer is ever to be taken
  into the safe room, and "the Spring Bonnie animatronic has been noticeably moved"
  (`fnaf3-tape5-no-customers-safe-room`, `fnaf3-tape5-spring-bonnie-moved`). That pairing counts only if the tapes
  belong to the old location before 1987, which is the assumption R11 also needs; it is not a second support.
- (5) `fnaf1-news-sanitation` (foul odour; blood and mucus round the mascots' eyes and mouths),
  `fnaf1-call1-eyeballs-teeth`, `fnaf1-game-over-eyes-teeth`, `fnaf1-call4-empty-heads`, `fnaf2-call2-the-smell`,
  `fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life`. These show the signs. None shows a body going into a suit.
- (6) What is stated: a man identified on video, caught the next morning, "charged" in the text under a headline
  that says "convicted", and no bodies (`fnaf1-news-suspect-captured`, `fnaf1-news-charged-vs-convicted`). By FNaF3
  the events are "unsolved mysteries" (`fnaf3-coming-soon-newspaper`). For "the killer, freed": the 1987
  restaurant ties its machines to "some kind of criminal database", someone then tampers with their facial
  recognition, and the lockdown is aimed "especially" at "previous employees" (`fnaf2-call1-criminal-database`,
  `fnaf2-call4-facial-recognition-tampered`, `fnaf2-call5-lockdown`), as if the company were defending against a
  known man with a record. For "the wrong man": nothing joins the caught man to the purple figure, and the
  killer is at large in every later game.
- (7) The "four plus one" pattern. Seven counts are listed. The yellow bear marks the one more in four of them
  (FNaF1, Give Gifts, Happiest Day, Help Wanted 2); in the Night 5 minigame, the "Bad ending." screen and the
  graves the fifth is there and is not marked as the yellow bear's. Outside the table, SAVE THEM and Foxy Go!
  Go! Go! show five children alike (`fnaf2-save-them`, `fnaf2-foxy-go-go-go`), and the screen players call the
  good ending shows four heads and no fifth (`fnaf3-good-ending-heads`). No count has a place for a child beyond
  the five and the child outside. The fifth body in Give Gifts is the wiki's report; no capture is on file.

| Scene | Four | Plus one | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| FNaF1 | four animatronics | five children; a yellow bear that comes and goes | `fnaf1-news-five-children`, `fnaf1-golden-freddy-office-figure` |
| Give Gifts, Give Life | four masks given | the yellow bear's jumpscare; the wiki reports a fifth body for one frame, and no capture is on file | `fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life` |
| FNaF3, Night 5 minigame | four machines dismantled | five grey, crying child figures | `fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room` |
| Happiest Day | Freddy, Bonnie, Chica, Foxy masks | a golden Freddy mask on the child at the end of the table | `fnaf3-happiest-day` |
| "Bad ending." | four heads | a fifth behind them, eyes lit | `fnaf3-bad-ending-heads` |
| Pizzeria Simulator's graves | four named stones | a fifth hidden in the grass (and a sixth apart) | `ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`, `ffps-lorekeeper-hill-stone` |
| Help Wanted 2 | Freddy, Bonnie, Chica, Foxy dolls, figures and graves | Golden Freddy (and the Puppet as a sixth) | `hw2-memory-dolls`, `hw2-faz-force-six-figures`, `hw2-pq4-graveyard-order` |

**Records against.**
- Date and place: `fnaf2-save-them` (five bodies in the 1987 building; R9); `fnaf1-news-closing` does not separate
  one building from the brand; `into-the-pit-prologue-mci` shows dead children in a party room, six by a fan wiki's count, and its 1985 is a logo graphic in an adaptation that
  the main games never place (R23). `fnaf2-call1-summer-job`: a line of FNaF2's own first call says "summer job", against that game's own
  cheques, which are dated November. The game contradicts itself and the model prefers the cheques; the June
  argument in "records for" depends on that preference. `fnaf4-pigtail-girl-hide-your-body` ("they come to life
  at night... they hide your body") is said c. 1983 and is the one FNaF4 line that sounds like the aftermath of
  the five: it is why a year before 1983 is allowed.
- Suit: `fnaf1-news-dressed-as-mascot` names no character or colour; `fnaf2-call6-yellow-suit` (R11).
- The bodies: the older models were "partly retrofitted" and are "used for parts" in 1987
  (`fnaf2-call2-older-models`), so they were opened up; police were contacted over the blood and mucus
  (`fnaf1-news-sanitation`); and still "the bodies themselves were never found"
  (`fnaf1-news-charged-vs-convicted`). The reading also puts five bodies into four mascots.
- The caught man: no later game mentions an arrest; no record joins him to the purple figure. And if he was
  William Afton, then on R12's convention a man publicly charged over five children goes on to pitch children's
  robots to a board under his own name and to put that name on a company (`sl-intro-mr-afton-board`,
  `sl-extras-map-entertainment-and-rental`). Each reading is low by itself; the pair is the least likely
  sequence in the order of events, and the model does not tell it.
- Golden Freddy: `scott-2014-cant-explain-golden-freddy`; `ucn-death-coin-fredbear`; `fnaf2-golden-freddy-night6-only`
  (the yellow bear wakes only on the night the yellow suit is reported used, so its tie to the suit may be later
  than the other four's); the fifth is always the odd one, given no mask and no machine to dismantle. With
  William in UCN's office, UCN's records lean toward the crying child (R6), and on R11 the yellow bear is the
  bear that bit him.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *The five were killed in 1987 at the FNaF2 restaurant.* Loses on the June date and on the smell and the walking
  before the week begins. The Foxy minigame, in which five children lie dead where the old Foxy is still a
  working act, is no longer counted: the model draws nothing from the setting of a death minigame (R7).
- *Who the caught man was.* Two readings, and the model no longer picks. "The right man, freed" explains three
  FNaF2 details at once (the criminal database, the tampering, the lockout of previous employees) and needs no
  second person. "The wrong man" needs nothing explained away afterwards. The fact to print is only "a man was
  caught the next morning and charged".
- *Golden Freddy is the crying child* (the reading the earlier audit replaced). Loses on the count: the crying
  child is not one of the five children linked to the incident, and the yellow bear holds the fifth place in every
  count, including the two latest games'. See R6.

**Names.** Gabriel, Fritz, Susie and Jeremy are engraved on four stones (`ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`). Reading
the row of five stones as the five is medium; pairing a name with a character is low-medium (R25). The fifth name
is in no game. Do not supply one.

**Would change if.** A year printed with the June 26th; a game showing the luring of the five; a game placing a
different child behind the yellow bear.

Review: all six of group A's findings are accepted. The bodies: "medium", with the records against now listed,
and the wording "the mascots carried the signs of it; where the bodies went is never shown". The arrest: the
model no longer reads the caught man as Afton, because that reading and R12's convention cannot be told in one
sequence. June 26th is printed for two children, with five linked later, and the ruling and dating row 13 now say
so. FNaF2's "summer job" is a line of the game, not sales copy, and is listed against the June argument (R24's
description is corrected). The pigtail girl's line is cited. Golden Freddy is split into the pattern
(medium-high) and the identification (medium).

Review: in the second round, three things reached this ruling from group B. The bracket for June 26th is printed
as 1980 to 1986, and "mid-1980s" goes (R24, point 7). UCN's last cutscene is neutral here: it shows Golden Freddy
last and says nothing of whose he is, so it is neither support for point 7 nor evidence against it (R16). And
where the five are after the dismantling is told once, in R30's table.

**Depends on.** R1, R7 (order, and the rule for the death minigames), R11 (which yellow suit was used in 1987),
R24 (the bracket). **Carries.** R9, R14, R16 (who the accuser is), R24, R30.

### R9. 1987: are the SAVE THEM bodies a second set of murders? What was the Bite of '87? The Toys.

**Ruling.**

1. **Something did happen at the new restaurant in the week of 12 November 1987.** That is certain, and the chapter
   should tell the week night by night. [CHANGE: the current theory gives it one out-of-order sentence]
2. **Five more children probably did not die.** The bodies in SAVE THEM are most likely the earlier five: the
   minigame is a flash, and its setting is not evidence (R7's rule for the four death minigames). The model keeps
   this open and says which way it leans. [CHANGE: the current "this stays open" gains a lean and its reason]
3. **The killer was back.** A report that someone "may have tampered" with the machines ("we're not sure"), the
   lockout of "previous employees" and the suit taken from the back are, on the model's reading, one man with
   inside knowledge.
4. **The Bite of '87 falls in this restaurant's last days**, possibly at the final birthday party. Who bit and who
   was bitten are not recorded. [CHANGE: the timeline has it "unplaced"]
5. **The Toys are machines, not hosts.** No record puts a child in one.
6. **What was done that week, the model cannot say.** It has a culprit, a suit and an investigation, and no act.
   With the five before 1987 (R8), no second five (point 2) and a suit "used" for a purpose the call does not
   give (R11), nothing is left for the suit to have been used for, or for the Night 4 investigation and the
   "tragic" rumours of Night 3 to be about. The chapter must say so in one plain sentence.

**Confidence.** (1) high. (2) low-medium. (3) medium. (4) medium for the last days of the 1987 restaurant; low for
the birthday itself. (5) low-medium. (6) high that the records name no act.

**The week, in the calls' own order** (use this table):

| When | What is said or shown | Records |
|---|---|---|
| Before Night 1 | The first guard complains that the characters try to get into the office, and is moved to days | `fnaf2-call1-second-guard` |
| Night 1 | Forget what you heard about the old location; new machines walk by day and are tied to a criminal database | `fnaf2-call1-forget-old-location`, `fnaf2-call1-walk-around-during-day`, `fnaf2-call1-criminal-database` |
| Night 2 | The older models: from the previous location, smelly, kept for parts, and they "shouldn't be able to walk around" | `fnaf2-call2-older-models`, `fnaf2-call2-the-smell`, `fnaf2-old-models-walk-from-night2` |
| Night 3 | "Whatever is going on out there, however tragic it may be, has nothing to do with our establishment" | `fnaf2-call3-rumors` |
| Night 4 | An investigation; someone may have tampered with facial recognition; the machines stare at adults | `fnaf2-call4-investigation`, `fnaf2-call4-facial-recognition-tampered` |
| Night 5, cheque 11-12-1987 | Lockdown, "especially concerning any... previous employees"; a day post "just became... available"; they will try to reach the owner of Fredbear's Family Diner | `fnaf2-call5-lockdown`, `fnaf2-call5-day-shift-vacancy`, `fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner`, `fnaf2-paycheck-1987` |
| Night 6, cheque 11-13-1987 | Closed. "Someone used one of the suits... a yellow one... Now none of them are acting right." The yellow bear attacks for the first time. One last birthday tomorrow; stay close to the animatronics | `fnaf2-call6-yellow-suit`, `fnaf2-golden-freddy-night6-only`, `fnaf2-call6-birthday-day-shift`, `fnaf2-overtime-robots-scrapped` |
| After | The new machines scrapped for "possible malfunctions"; the originals kept; a smaller reopening hoped for | `fnaf2-news-originals-kept`, `fnaf2-news-reopen-smaller-budget` |

Two points of order the current text blurs: the older models walk on Nights 2 and 3, before any tampering is
reported; and the lockdown (Night 5) comes before the report of the suit (Night 6).

**Records for (2).** FNaF1's clippings, pinned up in a later year, know one incident and five children
(`fnaf1-news-five-children`, `fnaf1-news-closing`); FNaF3 has five ghosts and five masks (`fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`,
`fnaf3-happiest-day`); the public reason for the 1987 closure is "possible malfunctions"
(`fnaf2-news-originals-kept`); the later games count six dead children in all, the five and the Puppet's
(`ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`, `ffps-lorekeeper-hill-stone`, `hw2-memory-dolls`, `hw2-pq4-graveyard-order`); the
minigame's player is the older Freddy, who in that building sits in Parts/Service, and the game answers "save them"
with "you can't" (`fnaf2-save-them`, `fnaf2-save-them-you-cant`).

**Records for (4).** A bite in '87 ended daytime walking (`fnaf1-call1-bite-of-87`). Daytime walking is still
allowed, and boasted of, in the week of 12 November 1987 (`fnaf2-call1-walk-around-during-day`). That restaurant
closes within days, and the old one has been rotting "for quite a while" (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`); no record
has another Freddy's open in late 1987, though undated tapes speak of "the sister location" and "most
locations" (`fnaf3-tape4-sister-location-springlock-failures`, `fnaf3-tape6-safe-rooms-sealed`). The one event left is a birthday at which the guard is to "stay close to the
animatronics and make sure they don't hurt anyone" (`fnaf2-call6-birthday-day-shift`).

**Records against.** For (2): `fnaf2-save-them` itself (five small bodies among the 1987 cast), the purple figure
present there and drawn with a chest mark that the purple figures of Take Cake and Foxy Go! Go! Go! do not have
(`fnaf2-save-them-purple-figure-chest-mark`: three sprites were compared. FNaF4's back-room figure has a chest
mark too, a smaller yellow bar, viewed in a capture on 3 October and now recorded in
`fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`; FNaF3's figure has none in the capture on file. Neither game says what
the mark is, and nothing is argued from it), and
"tragic" on Night 3. Read alone, FNaF2 suggests victims in 1987, and point 6 is the price of saying otherwise.
For (4): `fnaf2-no-bite-mention` (no call uses the word), and FNaF1's sentence is spoken about FNaF1's own
characters, the older four. That allows a bite early in 1987 at the old location, with "They even let them walk
around during the day!" as the new models' exception; "even" suits one. It cannot be excluded. For (5): "none of them are acting right"; the
1987 cast carries the cake to the crying children in FNaF3 (`fnaf3-bbs-air-adventure`, `fnaf3-chicas-party`,
`fnaf3-mangles-quest`).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** A second set of murders in November 1987, with SAVE THEM as its
record. It is what FNaF2 by itself most suggests, which is why (2) is only low-medium. It loses on every later
count of the dead: no ghost, mask, grave or doll is left over for a second five.

**Noted, not adopted.** That the "previous employee" is the day guard whose post falls empty on Night 5. The
records allow it (the vacancy; the lockout) and none says it. The purple figure with a badge-like mark in SAVE
THEM is not counted for it: under R7's rule that minigame is not a record of who was in the building. Low.

**Would change if.** Any record of 1987 victims: a name, a grave, a ghost; or any game that shows the Bite of '87.

Review: group A's three findings are accepted. Point 6 is new: the model has no act for November 1987 and now
says so. An early-1987 bite at the old location is listed against point 4. And SAVE THEM has one status: its
bodies are not read as a record of 1987, so its purple figure and his chest mark are not read as one either.

**Depends on.** R8, R11. **Carries.** R12 (the killer is alive in November 1987), the dating table.

### R10. The shadow figures

**Ruling.** The games name neither figure and explain neither. The model says so, and adds one lean: the dark
bear is the killer's lure. The dark rabbit is left open. [CHANGE: the current theory mentions the bear once and the
rabbit nowhere]

**Confidence.** Low overall. Low-medium that the dark bear works for the killer. What the dark rabbit does in
FNaF3 (it helps) is medium; what it is, nothing.

**Records for.** The dark bear is the yellow bear's shape in purple, for what a colour is worth (W8), sitting in
the back room in 1987 (`fnaf2-shadow-freddy`); its one line, "follow me.", leads each of the four to the room where the purple figure
takes it apart, and it is absent on the night he is cornered (`fnaf3-eon-shadow-freddy-follow-me`,
`fnaf3-eon-purple-guy-dismantles`). The Cassette Man's words can be read as describing that sequence: "He lured
them all back. Back to a familiar place. Back with familiar tricks." (`ffps-insanity-lured-back`,
`ffps-insanity-trap-again`). The tape names no place, and its record says it "does not say that is what it
means". "Follow me." is also the line under the rabbit behind the crying girl (`ffps-fruity-maze-follow-me`). The dark rabbit
stands in the 1987 office (`fnaf2-shadow-bonnie-office`); in FNaF3 it alone crosses between the other minigames'
rooms and brings cake to the last crying child (`fnaf3-shadow-bonnie-minigame`).

**Records against.** Following the dark bear ends with the ghosts free of the machines and the killer dead, which
would suit a guide working for the children as well. A slumped bear of the same pose sits in Fazbear's Fright
(`fnaf3-office-slumped-bear`); in that light its colour cannot settle whether it is the dark bear or the yellow
one, so the model cites it for neither figure. Special Delivery's "Shadowy Remnant" calls up a dark rabbit
(`sd-shadowy-remnant-surprise`) and explains nothing.

**How this sits with R15 and R30.** Those rulings read the same tape. After the second review neither says what
was taken from the children or where it went: R30's table marks the interval between the dismantling and the
Cassette Man's call "not shown". So the clash noted here in the first round, a taking on the nights the killer
dies and Funtime machines that already exist, is no longer a clash between rulings. It remains a gap in the
records, and R30 says so.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That both are after-images, with no will of their own (W9). It cannot
be excluded. The bear's "follow me." is an act with a purpose, which is why the model leans as it does.

**Labels.** "Shadow Freddy", "Shadow Bonnie" and "RWQFSFASXC" are file or fan names. Use "the dark bear" and "the
dark rabbit" (R26).

**Would change if.** A game named or explained either figure.

Review: group A found that the ruling holds and asked for three repairs, all made: "the killer's colour" is no
longer an argument (W8); "describes" becomes "can be read as", with the clash with step 11 stated; and the
slumped bear of Fazbear's Fright is cited for neither figure, here or in R16.

**Depends on.** R1. **Carries.** Nothing.

### R11. The yellow suit, and Scott's single retcon

**Ruling on the suit.** "A yellow one" is the yellow bear suit: the spare the first two games show slumped, and in
FNaF3's terms the second of the two springlock suits. It is not Spring Bonnie. This is a lean, and it stands on
one assumption about FNaF3's tapes. [CHANGE]

**Ruling on the retcon.** No game or statement locates it. The model withdraws its stake on the yellow suit. If a
stake is wanted, the better one is the suspect "convicted" in FNaF1's clipping, whom the later games treat as
never held. The model needs neither. [CHANGE]

**Confidence.** The suit: low-medium. The retcon's location: low for any candidate; low-medium that the yellow
suit is not it. [Revised after review: "not the retcon" was medium]

**Records for the suit.**
- Inside FNaF2: the yellow bear is the only yellow figure in the first two games that sits limp like an empty
  costume. That is the look of FNaF1's figure (`fnaf1-golden-freddy-office-figure`); FNaF2's is slumped and worn
  like the older models, with wires hanging from an eye socket (`fnaf2-golden-freddy-office`); by a fan wiki's
  activity tables it does nothing for five nights and is active from the night of this call
  (`fnaf2-golden-freddy-night6-only`, `fnaf2-call6-yellow-suit`); it
  stands before the viewer after Night 3 (`fnaf2-cutscenes-its-me`) and sits in SAVE THEM's rooms
  (`fnaf2-save-them-golden-freddy`); its dark twin sits in Parts/Service, "the back" (`fnaf2-shadow-freddy`).
- From FNaF3's tapes: there are two suits (`fnaf3-tape2-dual-suits`), a bear and a rabbit on one stage
  (`fnaf3-stage01-fredbear-spring-bonnie`). The rabbit is at the tapes' location, "noticeably moved"
  (`fnaf3-tape5-spring-bonnie-moved`); that location's safe room is then walled up with "nothing... taken out"
  (`fnaf3-tape6-safe-rooms-sealed`, `fnaf3-tape6-nothing-taken-out`); and it is in that room, in the building with
  FNaF1's layout, when the killer dies (`fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`, `fnaf3-eon-map-fnaf1-layout`). The 1987
  restaurant is a different building (`fnaf2-call1-building-cannot-run-out`, `fnaf2-call1-new-location`). If the
  sealing came before November 1987, the rabbit was behind a wall that month and the spare was the bear.
- The audience in January 2015 took the first FNaF3 teaser's yellow figure for Golden Freddy, and Scott knew it
  (`scott-2018-interview-fnaf3-teaser-read-as-golden-freddy` (a transcript)).

**One assumption, not two supports.** The ruling needs the sealing to predate November 1987. The reason on file
is the suit itself: "by November 1987 the yellow suit is only 'a spare in the back'"
(`fnaf3-tapes-freddy-fazbears-pizza`, and dating row 15). That argument dates the retirement, and it holds
whichever suit the spare was. It dates the sealing only if the spare was the bear. If the spare was the rabbit,
the rabbit was not yet behind a wall that month, and the sealing comes after 1987, in the reopened old building,
where tape 6's "budget restrictions" would echo the "much smaller budget" that follows 1987
(`fnaf3-tape6-nothing-taken-out`, `fnaf2-news-reopen-smaller-budget`). So the tapes' date cannot be used to rule
the rabbit out: the date of the sealing and the suit's identity stand or fall together. The tapes' own wording
does not break the tie. They speak of "most locations" and of a "sister location"
(`fnaf3-tape6-safe-rooms-sealed`, `fnaf3-tape4-sister-location-springlock-failures`), and no record outside the
tapes shows the company running several places at once in either period. One pointer is left, and it is weak:
the tapes read as one run of notices from one working restaurant. The rabbit reading has to split that run
across the old restaurant's closing and its reopening, or to put the whole run after 1987 with the suits back in
service.

**A second assumption.** Tape 4 retires both suits together: "the classic suits are being retired to an
appropriate location" (`fnaf3-tapes-freddy-fazbears-pizza`). If "nothing is being taken out" fixes the rabbit in
the sealed room, it would fix the bear there too, unless the two were kept apart. The room shows only the rabbit
(`fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`). The model has to assume that the bear was somewhere else when the wall went up.

**Records against the suit ruling.** The rabbit is the killer's suit wherever a later game shows him in one
(`fnaf3-n5-springlock-death`, `fnaf4-purple-guy-suits-up-employee`, `ffps-fruity-maze-follow-me`,
`hw-rabbit-figure-appearance`). The two assumptions above. Chica is yellow too, though she is a working
animatronic in plain view, not "a spare".

**What follows.** Two suits, two uses. The rabbit is the costume of the five murders (R8) and of his own death.
The yellow bear is what "someone used" in November 1987, and the yellow bear's attacks begin that night. The
plain sense of "used" is that someone wore it. Print the call's own word and add nothing: what the suit was used
for is not recorded (R9, point 6).

**The retcon, tested against Scott's own constraints** (`scott-2017-one-retcon`, `scott-2017-retcon-seamless`,
`scott-2017-clarification-is-not-retcon`, `scott-2017-story-developed-new-details`):

| Candidate | In the window (to Sept 2017) | Changes something an earlier game printed | Not a clarification | Not merely a new detail | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| The caught and "convicted" suspect of FNaF1 (`fnaf1-news-suspect-captured`, `fnaf1-news-five-children`, `fnaf1-news-charged-vs-convicted`), against a killer at large in FNaF2 and FNaF3 and "unsolved mysteries" (`fnaf3-coming-soon-newspaper`) | yes | yes: the only place a later game overrides printed text | yes | yes | best of a poor field |
| The yellow suit of FNaF2 becoming Spring Bonnie in FNaF3 | yes | no: FNaF2 never named the suit | yes | no: in Scott's terms it is a later detail | allowed, not required |
| The meaning of FNaF4 and the box (`scott-2018-box-meaning-changed` (a transcript)) | yes | arguably | no: he filed it under clarification | n/a | poor |
| A second set of dead in 1987 that later games stop counting (`fnaf2-save-them`; R9) | yes | no: shown, never stated | yes | unclear | noted only; the adjudicator's own addition |

Scott gave one more test: the retcon "went unnoticed by most people", and had it been argued about he would have
addressed it. The records cannot apply that test. What the fandom was arguing about in 2017 is not on file, and
the model does not supply it from memory.

The current chapter's own definition of a seamless retcon ("a later game that fills the gap differently without
contradicting any printed line") describes what Scott calls a clarification or a new detail, which he sets apart
from the one "actual retcon". And its reason for accepting the yellow suit ("the swap removes a tension") answered
a tension the model had made for itself in the Golden Freddy chapter.

**In-universe, the model does not need a retcon here.** "A man was caught and charged" is true whether he was
the killer, later free for want of bodies, or the wrong man (R8, point 6). If Scott's retcon is the conviction,
he wrote a closed case in the first game and reopened it in the next two.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That "a yellow one" is Spring Bonnie: the sealing is later than
1987, and the rabbit went behind the wall only afterwards. It keeps one suit for every crime, and the records
allow it. It loses, narrowly, because the first two games show one empty yellow costume and it is the bear, and
because that bear wakes on the very night the suit is used. That is a lean and no more, which is what
low-medium means.

**Would change if.** A date for the Night 6 tape relative to November 1987; any statement by Scott about the
retcon; a game showing which suit was used in 1987.

Review: group A's four findings are accepted. The tapes' date and the suit's identity are now stated as one
assumption, and the assumption that the bear was not sealed in with the rabbit is stated beside it. The gloss
that the fifth child is in that suit is removed from this ruling: it needs the suit not to have been worn, and
the call's plain sense is that it was. The retcon table's fifth column is dropped, because it rested on a
recollection of the fandom and on no record, and "medium that the yellow suit is not the retcon" falls to
low-medium. The earlier second reason for the tapes' date ("several working locations") is withdrawn: it fits no
period better than another.

**Depends on.** R8, R12. **Carries.** R9; R6 (the bear that bit the crying child); the Golden Freddy chapter's
wording; the retcon chapter, which becomes a short entry among the open questions.

### R12. Order of events: the Funtimes and Elizabeth's death, the five murders, the killer's death in the suit, Michael's Sister Location nights, FNaF1, FNaF3

**Ruling.** One order, with each step marked. The full list is in section 3; these are the orderings that were
contested.

| Ordering | Ruling | Confidence | Fixed by a record, or convention |
|---|---|---|---|
| FNaF2 (November 1987), then FNaF1, then FNaF3 | Yes | high | Records: six joins from FNaF2 to FNaF1 |
| The five murders before the 1987 restaurant opened | Yes | medium-high | Records (R8) |
| FNaF1's building is the old location, reopened | Probably | medium | Inference from four records, one of them weak |
| The closing clipping in FNaF1 is news of FNaF1's own restaurant, not of the old one before 1987 | Probably | medium | Inference. FNaF1 dates none of its four clippings |
| Safe rooms sealed before the killer's death, not after | Yes | medium | Records: the tapes address working staff. [CHANGE: `timeline.ts` has the sealing after the death] |
| The killer alive in November 1987 | Yes | medium | Records (R9) |
| The killer's death after FNaF1's restaurant closed | Yes | medium, lower end | It rests on the row above, and on FNaF1 showing the four at work. [CHANGE: "the games do not date his death"] |
| The killer's death before Fazbear's Fright | Yes | high | Records |
| Circus Baby's venture (the Funtimes, Elizabeth's death) after the five murders and before the killer's death | Probably after the five: low. Before his death: high (he is alive for it) | low / high | One sentence of HandUnit's puts the business after a Freddy's had closed; the exact slot is convention. [CHANGE: "the first child taken is his own daughter" goes] |
| Elizabeth's death before Michael's week underground | Yes | high | Records |
| Michael's week underground after the killer's death | Convention | low | No record. The errand reads as a standing instruction from a father who can no longer be reached |
| Michael's speech after the Fazbear's Fright fire | Yes | high | Records |
| Pizzeria Simulator after the Fazbear's Fright fire | Yes | medium-high | The rabbit that burns in the first is the salvaged rabbit of the second (R1, link b) |
| Pizzeria Simulator after Michael's speech | If the volunteer is Michael | medium | R13 |
| UCN after Pizzeria Simulator | Yes | medium-high | Whoever the player is: the roster holds that game's four, and UCN began as its custom night |

**Records for.**
- FNaF2 before FNaF1, six joins: the new machines are scrapped and "the original characters are being kept"
  (`fnaf2-news-originals-kept`), and FNaF1's cast is those four (`fnaf1-custom-night-names`); a reopening "with a
  much smaller budget" (`fnaf2-news-reopen-smaller-budget`) against FNaF1's rationed power
  (`scott-fnaf1-steam-budget-cuts`, `fnaf2-call1-building-cannot-run-out`); Phone Guy will "take the night shift
  myself" (`fnaf2-call6-night-shift-myself`) and later "worked in that office before you"
  (`fnaf1-call1-phone-guy-previous-guard`); the older models are faceless in 1987 and whole in FNaF1
  (`fnaf2-parts-service-old-models`); daytime walking is allowed in 1987 and ended by FNaF1
  (`fnaf2-call1-walk-around-during-day`, `fnaf1-call1-bite-of-87`); the stuffing explanation is "a minor glitch" in
  1987 and house lore in FNaF1 (`fnaf2-call1-endoskeleton-glitch`, `fnaf1-call1-stuffed-into-suit`).
- Same building, four records: "the tragedy that took place there" (`fnaf1-news-closing`); FNaF3's minigame
  building has FNaF1's layout plus one more room, the room with the yellow rabbit suit
  (`fnaf3-eon-map-fnaf1-layout`), and "every location is built with one extra room" (`fnaf3-tape3-safe-room`); the
  old restaurant still stood, unused, in 1987 (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`); and, weakly, FNaF2's cutscenes, in a
  game set in 1987, show FNaF1's stage with the older Bonnie and Chica whole
  (`fnaf2-cutscenes-old-stage-through-eye-holes`; R25 rates the reading of those scenes low-medium).
- Sealing first: `fnaf3-tape6-safe-rooms-sealed`, `fnaf3-tape6-nothing-taken-out`, `fnaf3-tapes-hello-hello-greeting`
  (the Phone Guy voice, who does not outlive his last week at FNaF1's restaurant: R25, where the tapes' share of
  that identity is medium and rests on an `implied` record).
- Circus Baby's: "Due to the massive success and even more so the unfortunate closing of Freddy Fazbear's Pizza,
  it was clear that the stage was set... for another contender in children's entertainment"
  (`sl-handunit-freddy-fazbear-closing`). "Another contender" most naturally names the pizzeria venture as well
  as the rentals. Baby's one day on stage (`sl-baby-on-stage-one-day`, `sl-baby-ice-cream-story`); the cancelled
  opening and the move to rentals (`scottgames-site-sl-news-gas-leak-cancellation`,
  `scottgames-site-sl-source-bright-future-circus-baby`, `sl-handunit-rented-for-private-parties`). One record
  states an order between the killer's crimes, and no ruling cited it before: "As if what he had already done
  wasn't enough, he found a new way to desecrate, to humiliate, to destroy" (`ffps-insanity-new-way-to-desecrate`).
  It puts the "new" use of the small souls after the killings. It does not date the building of any machine.
- Michael: `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-found-it` ("right where you said it would be"),
  `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-come-find-you` ("I've been living in shadows... I'm going to come find you"),
  `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-springtrap` (the burnt attraction behind him). These fix the speech after the fire and
  the week before the speech, and nothing else. The convention that the week follows his father's death rests on
  the tone of a report to a man who cannot be reached, and on the facility running without its maker
  (`sl-handunit-intro-job-ad`, `sl-technicians-night4-dialogue`).
- UCN after Pizzeria Simulator: `ucn-roster-includes-ffps-four`, `ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`,
  `scott-2018-ucn-for-pizzeria-simulator`.

**The fourth clipping.** FNaF1 shows four clippings in the same way and dates none. The model reads the first
two as the incident (R8), the third as the old restaurant's troubles before 1987 (`fnaf1-news-sanitation`, with
`fnaf1-news-uphill-battle`), and the fourth, `fnaf1-news-closing`, as news of FNaF1's own restaurant: "it will
close by year's end". The record's `when` label, "Year of FNaF1", is the record writer's reading. Read as old
news like its neighbours, the fourth describes the old restaurant's end before 1987 almost word for word: a
long struggle, a year with no buyer, then a building "left to rot" (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`). That reading
leaves FNaF1's own closing with no record, and its "many years ago" would push the five toward 1980. The model
keeps the split, at medium, for three reasons: the four clippings read as one run that ends in the present of
the game that shows them; FNaF1's restaurant is visibly failing, on rationed power
(`scott-fnaf1-steam-budget-cuts`); and Scott's FNaF3 text counts "thirty years after Freddy Fazbear's Pizza
closed its doors" from a closing that leaves the four as relics (`scott-fnaf3-steam-description`). Step 14 and
dating rows 22, 24, 25 and 28 hang on this reading and are marked to match.

**The killer's death.** The placement rests on one thing: a living culprit with inside knowledge in November
1987 (R9, point 3: medium), together with FNaF1 showing the four at work afterwards, so that the dismantling
cannot fall between 1987 and FNaF1's week. Two arguments used in the first version are withdrawn. "After his
death the ghosts fade away" was used as if the children had then gone, which the model's own account denies:
they are spoken of as still held at Pizzeria Simulator (R30). And "the four are whole and in their FNaF1
places" fits the old restaurant before 1987 as well, because on this ruling it is the same building with the
same four on the same stage.

**Records against.**
- FNaF2 before FNaF1: Scott's FNaF2 store text is written as a sequel's: "Welcome back", and "the old and aging
  animatronics are joined by a new cast" (`scott-fnaf2-steam-old-and-aging`). The cheque overrides it: 1987 is
  the only date the game prints, and the six joins follow from it.
- Same building: `fnaf1-help-wanted-ad` says nothing of a reopening; "there" could mean the brand.
- The fourth clipping: the alternative reading above.
- The killer's death: the room was walled up with its contents, yet he and the ghosts go in and out of it, and the
  games do not show him opening it. The rival placement is "before 1987, while the old restaurant rotted", and
  three records suit it: an empty, wet building (`fnaf2-call1-new-location`, `fnaf3-eon-puddles-empty-building`);
  the older models faceless and in pieces in 1987 (`fnaf2-parts-service-old-models`); and Scott's FNaF2 trailer,
  which shows them between the captions "forgotten", "dismantled" and "deactivated"
  (`scott-fnaf2-trailer-captions`).
- Circus Baby's: HandUnit does not say which Freddy's closed. The tapes' "sister location"
  (`fnaf3-tape4-sister-location-springlock-failures`) shows a second venue existed in the springlock years;
  nothing says it was Baby's. A springlock suit "came from my old pizzeria" (`sl-springlock-suit-from-old-pizzeria`):
  the first version filed that as support, but "too dangerous" is Baby's opinion in the present, and a springlock
  suit supplied to her pizzeria fits the years before the suits were retired at least as well. It cuts both
  ways and is counted for neither. FNaF4's pink bedroom is silent on whether its owner was alive in 1983
  (`fnaf4-second-bedroom`). R3's open problem belongs here too: the Private Room is in the building of the
  business that this ruling dates after a Freddy's closing, and so after the 1983 week by convention.
- Michael's week: guards are fired for "odor" in FNaF1 and FNaF2 (`fnaf1-custom-night-pink-slip`,
  `fnaf2-pink-slip-fritz-smith`); if either were Michael, the scooping would come before November 1987 (R13 rules
  that link unproven).

**How late Circus Baby's venture can fall.** The first version said a place after FNaF1's restaurant closed was
"equally allowed". That holds only if the pink bedroom of FNaF4 is not the daughter's. R3 counts that room as
compatible with her. If she is alive and small c. 1983, she cannot be the little girl of Baby's one day six or
more years later, except on the tightest dating, which R24 leans against. If she is already dead c. 1983, the
venture is older than any closing of a Freddy's that the model has. So the later place is allowed only if the
room is not hers.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *He died before 1987, while the old restaurant rotted.* It loses because it leaves no living culprit for
  November 1987. That is its only cost, and it is a medium one: hence "medium, lower end".
- *The closing clipping is the old restaurant's.* See "The fourth clipping". It is the live alternative.
- *Elizabeth died before the five* (the usual fan order). No record supports it, and none excludes it. The model's
  order is a convention with one sentence behind it; the chapter must say so.

**Wording the records allow (added in round four).** The building he dies in has FNaF1's layout
(`fnaf3-eon-map-fnaf1-layout`, shown); that it is "the old restaurant" is this ruling's medium link, so the
phrase takes "most likely". The sealing of the safe rooms is not dated against his death: no "years before".
The thirty years are counted from a closing, in Scott's store text, and not from his death: no "thirty years in
the room" (`scott-fnaf3-steam-description`). The dismantling is shown in four scenes, one after each of Nights 1 to 4, with a growing
pile and no duration: neither "a single night's work" nor "one a night" (`fnaf3-eon-purple-guy-dismantles`). That the
Cassette Man's "lured them all back" describes this dismantling is "can be read as" (R10, R30).

**The killer's end: what is shown, what is inferred, and where each is said (rule added in round 4b).** The two
records of the scene are literal. After Night 5 the player is a crying, grey child figure; four more block the
way out of a room that holds three arcade cabinets and an empty yellow rabbit suit; the purple figure runs from
them, climbs into the suit and appears to laugh; the suit snaps shut on him, blood pours out, and he slumps to
the floor; the grey figures fade (`fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`, `fnaf3-n5-springlock-death`). The records do not
say "dies", "ghosts", "springlock failure" or "safe room". The model says the first two, and the rule is this.

1. *The scene is told as shown* wherever it is described: in the chapter `springtrap`, in the storyline beat,
   and in any caption.
2. *He dies there* is a firm inference (R1, link a: strong; the thesis table's "high" is unchanged). Its
   supports: the tapes, which say what a loosened springlock does to the wearer ("bleeding out":
   `fnaf3-tape3-springlock-bleeding`, `fnaf3-tape3-bleeding-out`); the rare screens thirty years on, which show
   what looks like a decayed human body inside that suit (`fnaf3-springtrap-corpse-screens`); and, as support
   only, Scott's remark that the purple figure's corpse is in the suit all that time
   (`scott-2018-interview-purple-guy-in-springtrap` (a transcript)). The chapter `springtrap` states the inference
   once, with the first two supports. Everywhere else "he dies in the suit" may be said flat.
3. *The five figures are the ghosts of the five* is the model's reading (it is the join of R1's link e:
   strong). Its supports: there are five; they are drawn with the crying-child sprites of FNaF2's minigames; they
   stand in the building when the four machines have just been taken apart, and the player is one of them;
   the killer runs from them. The chapter `springtrap` marks it once. Everywhere else "five ghosts" may be said
   flat.
4. *Limits on the flat forms.* Not "the ghosts kill him" or "the children kill him": they corner him, and he
   climbs into the suit himself. Not "a springlock failure" as something shown: the screen shows a suit
   snapping shut, and the mechanism is the tapes'. "Fade" is what the screen does: not "freed", "released" or
   "gone" (R14, R30). The room is "the room with the suit"; that it is the tapes' sealed safe room is R8's
   medium. And the scene dates nothing (this ruling: after FNaF1's closing, medium at its lower end).
5. *The suit is Spring Bonnie* is a short inference (added in round 4d). The screen shows an empty yellow rabbit
   suit and prints no name: "Spring Bonnie" is the Night 5 tape's name for a springlock suit
   (`fnaf3-tape5-spring-bonnie-moved`), and the record of the scene says so in its notes. The chapter
   `springtrap` marks the identification once ("most likely"); elsewhere "the Spring Bonnie suit" and "dies in
   Spring Bonnie" may be said flat, as that chapter's answer, the beat `s-springlock` and step 15 do. Where the
   suit of the five murders is meant, the grade is R8's (medium) and the marker stays.

**Would change if.** A dated record for Circus Baby's Pizza World; a game showing who opened the sealed room and
when; a year on FNaF1's cheques; a date on FNaF1's closing clipping.

Review: all six of group B's findings are accepted. The killer's death is now "medium, lower end" and rests on
R9 alone; the two withdrawn arguments are named above. The fourth clipping is argued, with its alternative, and
what hangs on it is marked. For Circus Baby's place the HandUnit sentence is read in full and the Cassette Man's
"new way to desecrate" is cited for what it orders and no more; the springlock suit leaves the support list;
and "a later place is equally allowed" is corrected. The last row of the table is split in three. "Seven joins"
was six, and "three records" was four. Scott's sequel-style store text is listed against the first ordering.

**Depends on.** R8, R9, R11, R3 (the pink room; the Private Room). **Carries.** R24 and the whole of section 3; R30
(the interval between the dismantling and the call).

### R13. Michael: is he Mike Schmidt, any other guard, the Pizzeria Simulator franchisee?

**Ruling.**

| Identification | Ruling | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| Michael is William Afton's son | Yes | high |
| The scooped technician of Sister Location is Michael | Yes | high |
| The older brother of FNaF4 is Michael | Yes | medium, lower end (R3, as revised after review) |
| Pizzeria Simulator's franchisee is Michael | Most likely. A theory-status identification, never "strong" | medium |
| Mike Schmidt of FNaF1 is Michael | Not established; the model does not use it. A text may say that the strongest evidence usually cited for it "is in a book, which this archive does not admit", and may not say what the book says (R27, round 4d) | low |
| Fritz Smith of FNaF2 is Michael | One word on a slip ("Odor"), the same word as Mike Schmidt's. Not used | low |
| Jeremy Fitzgerald is Michael | No record | none |
| The guard of FNaF3 is Michael | Michael is on the site after the fire; nothing makes him its guard | low |
| UCN's player is Michael | The model leans against it (R16: William, low-medium). Michael is the full alternative | [CHANGE] |

**Records for.**
- Son, and technician: `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-found-it`,
  `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-they-thought-i-was-you`, `sl-real-ending-scooped-mirror`, `sl-custom-night-decay`,
  `sl-custom-night-ennard-ejected`, `sl-you-wont-die`, `scott-2016-cutscenes-are-canon`. No line joins the technician's
  name to Michael's (`sl-eggs-benedict`); the cutscenes continue the Real Ending.
- Franchisee. The best support is the man himself. He "somehow found this job listing not intended for you",
  and the Cassette Man guesses ("I have a feeling") that he would rather stay than take the way out planned for
  him (`ffps-completion-brave-volunteer`, `ffps-completion-right-where-you-want-to-be`). The game does not show
  whether he leaves. Set that beside Michael's last words: "I should be dead... There is
  only one thing left for me to do now. I'm going to come find you" (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-come-find-you`).
  Then the echoes, in order of weight. "They didn't recognize me at first" becomes his father's "You may not
  recognize me at first, but I assure you, it's still me" (`ffps-scraptrap-still-me`), which wants a listener who
  knew the man. Scrap Baby speaks to him as to someone known (`ffps-scrap-baby-should-have-known`,
  `ffps-scrap-baby-played-into-our-hands`). Weakest: "I put her back together, just like you asked me to" against
  "You gathered them all together in one place, just like he asked you to"
  (`ffps-scrap-baby-completion-monologue`). That line also describes any employee following the tapes, with "he"
  the employer whom Baby wrongly takes for her father (`ffps-scrap-baby-employer`,
  `ffps-completion-not-called-by-who-you-assume`).

**Records against.** The franchisee is never named and never speaks. For Mike Schmidt: the shared first name on
a cheque (`fnaf1-paycheck-night5`) and "Odor" on the pink slip (`fnaf1-custom-night-pink-slip`) are the whole
case, and the slip itself shows no name: it is Mike Schmidt's only as the seventh night after his cheques; Fritz Smith's slip gives the same reason (`fnaf2-pink-slip-fritz-smith`), and smell clings to the machines
themselves in both games (`fnaf2-call2-the-smell`, `fnaf1-news-sanitation`). R12's convention works against the
"Odor" reading as well, since it puts the scooping after FNaF1's restaurant closed. For the FNaF3 guard: Michael
stands in the attraction's wreck when he speaks (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-springtrap`), but "I'm going to come
find you" reads better from a man who has not just spent a week with the thing. Scott has never spoken of who
Mike Schmidt is.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *The franchisee is an outsider nobody planned for.* The listing was "not intended for you", and Scrap Baby
  says "You're not who I expected to see" (`ffps-scrap-baby-not-expected`). It loses to "it's still me", which is
  said to someone who knew the man before he burned, and to a volunteer who, on the Cassette Man's guess, would
  rather stay than leave. It is why the identification is medium and no higher.
- *Michael worked as the guard in the first games on his way to finding his father.* It would give the early
  games a protagonist with a motive. It loses because nothing but a first name and one word on a slip supports
  it; the story can mention it in the open questions and must not lean on it.

**Wording.** Michael is not "the son who was put back together". He put "her" back together
(`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-put-her-back-together`); he is the son who "should be dead".

**Would change if.** A game named the franchisee or tied a guard to the Afton family.

Review: group B's first three findings are accepted. The franchisee is now led by the volunteer and not by the
echoes, the outsider alternative is argued, and "medium, upper end" becomes "medium". Fritz Smith and the FNaF3
guard have rows of their own in place of "no record". "UCN's player is Michael: No" entered a lean as a flat
answer and is reworded. The fourth point was put to the site's owner and not to this ruling: whether the
open-questions chapter should say where readers know the Mike Schmidt idea from. Until the owner decides, the
chapter says what the games give, a first name and one word, and names no other source.

**Depends on.** R1, R3, R16. **Carries.** The Pizzeria Simulator chapter's cast; R12 (Pizzeria Simulator after
Michael's speech).

### R14. FNaF3's good ending against Pizzeria Simulator's still-trapped spirits

**Ruling.** Within the first three games, Happiest Day is the intended release of the five and of a sixth,
Puppet-masked child. The later games do not let it stand as final: Pizzeria Simulator, the game made to resolve
the story, speaks of the small souls as without rest and shows the children's graves only after its own fire.
The model tells Happiest Day as the release that was owed and first attempted, and the fire as the release the
later games stand on. Why the first did not hold, one record says in outline: after the killer "robbed them of
the only thing that they had. Again", "they will never find rest now. Not like this". The model reports that
and adds nothing to it. [CHANGE: from "this stays open" to "Pizzeria Simulator takes a side, and the model
reports it"]

**Confidence.** Medium-high that the good ending is the trilogy's intended resolution. Medium that, on the later
games' account, it did not hold. Low for the reason. As an event in the order of events, Happiest Day is not
firm: which FNaF3 ending happened is open, and step 17 is marked to match. [Revised after review: the reason was
"not stated"; step 17 had Happiest Day as F]

**Records for.** Inside FNaF3: `fnaf3-good-ending-requirements`, `fnaf3-happiest-day`, the game's own captions
"Bad ending." and "the end" (`fnaf3-bad-ending-heads`, `fnaf3-good-ending-heads`), and Scott on FNaF3 as "the end
of a trilogy" (`scott-2018-interview-fnaf3-end-of-trilogy` (a transcript)). Afterwards, the Cassette Man's tape, in
its own order: small souls "in prisons of my making now set to new purpose"; "He lured them all back... He
brought them all together"; "he led them there again. He overpowered them again. And he robbed them of the only
thing that they had. Again"; "they will never find rest now. Not like this"; "I have to call them all back"
(`ffps-insanity-small-souls-prisons`, `ffps-insanity-lured-back`, `ffps-insanity-trap-again`,
`ffps-insanity-tiny-breaths`, `ffps-insanity-tape-call-them-back`). Spirits are still in the machines he gathers
(`ffps-completion-give-up-spirits`); his daughter is still bound and is told to rest "for you, and for those you
have carried in your arms" (`ffps-completion-my-daughter`, `ffps-completion-rest`); the graves are shown after
the credits (`ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`). Scott, asked which FNaF3 ending happened: "The answer is very
interesting. The answer is complex." (`scott-2018-interview-fnaf3-ending-unanswered` (a transcript)).

**What decides, and what does not.** The tense of the tape does not decide. "Now" and "He lured them all back"
fit a freeing followed by a luring back as well as they fit no freeing at all. What decides, at medium, is whom
the tape is about: souls in machines of the speaker's making, lured to a familiar place and overpowered, which
is what FNaF3 shows done to the four before the attraction exists (`fnaf3-eon-shadow-freddy-follow-me`,
`fnaf3-eon-purple-guy-dismantles`). The record's own note says the tape "does not say that is what it means". If
it is, the Cassette Man speaks after Happiest Day's place in the order and still has them without rest.

**Records against.** `fnaf3-good-ending-heads` (the eyes go dark; the fifth head is gone). The only one of Happiest
Day's six whom a later game shows still bound by name is the Puppet's child; the five are spoken of, not shown.
No record says when the gravestones were set: children missing for decades could have had stones for decades.
UCN then has a voice refusing release after the fire (`ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the good ending happened and held for the five, and what
Pizzeria Simulator gathers is something else: what the killer "robbed" from them, or other victims. "Complex"
favours something of this kind over a plain "the bad ending happened". It does not lose outright. It loses as
the telling because the tape's small souls are, on the best reading, the five themselves, and its speaker means
to give them rest. The chapter lists it as the surviving alternative. R30 no longer says what was taken from
them or where it went, so the two rulings no longer contradict each other: both say that the interval is not
shown.

**Wording.** "Shows the graves only after its fire", not "gives the children their graves": the thesis may use
the second as a figure, and a chapter may not tell it as an event.

**Would change if.** Scott answered the 2018 question, or a game showed the five between FNaF3 and Pizzeria
Simulator.

Review: group B's four findings are accepted. Happiest Day is no longer an event marked firm in the order of
events. The reviewer asked the model to choose between this ruling's alternative and what R15 and R30 said
about "what was taken": the model keeps this ruling, adopts the Cassette Man's reason in outline at low, and
withdraws the mechanism from R15 and R30. The present-tense argument is dropped, and the argument that replaces
it is named. "Gives the children their graves" becomes "shows the graves".

**Depends on.** R7, R8, R15, R30 (the account of the five). **Carries.** The ending of the "reckoning" part; R16's
reading of who is left.

### R15. What Remnant is, as far as the games say

**Ruling.** The games give one sheet and one later use of the word. A blueprint marked Afton Robotics, LLC,
is headed "S.calable C.reation of U.lterior P.resence" and labels on it an excavating arm, a "Remnant
Injector" and a "Remnant Reservoir". Its notes say: leave a trace amount on the interior; over-usage or
over-exposure negates the effect; when heated, no observable motion; keep in a heated tank at a sustained
temperature; the substance should be malleable, but not more; and "there is a possibility that overheating
might neutralize the effects permanently". These are design notes. No game shows an injection. The games never
say what Remnant is made of, where it comes from, what its "effect" is, or that it is what holds the dead in
machines. [CHANGE: the thesis's "what keeps the dead in machines is called Remnant" and "the same blueprint
proposes a remedy" both overclaim]

The model's reading, marked as such. The man who calls the machines his own making says he does not know how
the children came to inhabit them; what the games show is the Puppet giving the dead children the mascots' faces. Afterwards, in
the same man's words, the killer "found a new way to desecrate", and the small souls were "set to new purpose".
The S.C.U.P. sheet is on the screen while he says so. The model reads Remnant as the name the killer's firm
gave to what it was trying to make use of, and says no more: not what the substance is, and not whether anything
was drawn from the five.

**Confidence.** High for the wording of the sheet. Low-medium for the reading. Low-medium, as an argument from
silence, that the first hauntings were not the killer's design. [Revised after review: "nobody designed the
first hauntings" was stated flat, and rated high in the thesis]

**Records for.** The sheet: `ffps-scup-blueprint`, `ffps-scup-remnant-reservoir-notes`,
`ffps-scup-overheating-neutralize`. That the machine is Sister Location's scooper is a strong inference and not a
statement: the acronym, the excavating arm and the Afton Robotics mark, beside the room "where they use the
scooper" (`sl-scooping-room-explained`). The sheet never prints the word. What Sister Location shows that machine
do is empty whatever is put in front of it (`sl-ballora-scooped`, `sl-real-ending-scooped-mirror`). The
ignorance of the man who made them, by his own account: "I don't know how those tiny breaths of life came to
inhabit those machines" (`ffps-insanity-tiny-breaths`). The agent the games do show: `fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life`, and "the way you lifted
others into yours" (`ffps-completion-shut-out`). The order, in the one record that states one: "As if what he had
already done wasn't enough, he found a new way to desecrate" (`ffps-insanity-new-way-to-desecrate`), then "Small
souls trapped in prisons of my making now set to new purpose and used in ways I never thought imaginable"
(`ffps-insanity-small-souls-prisons`), then the robbing "again" (`ffps-insanity-trap-again`). That record puts the
new use of the souls after the killings. It does not date the building of the scooper or of the Funtime
machines, whose place after the five stays R12's convention. The monsters hold spirits that "don't belong to you"
(`ffps-completion-give-up-spirits`). Special Delivery uses the word for a collectible that powers animatronics and
is "put... back into" them (`sd-tutorial-remnant-collection`), without saying what it is.

**Records against.** Nothing states the link between the substance and the souls. The Insanity tape names no
machine. Special Delivery's Remnant is a game resource. For "not the killer's design": no record speaks to his
intent either way, and the Cassette Man's "I don't know" is one man's ignorance. Scrap Baby's "all of those little
souls" was cited here in the first version as something collected in order to "be complete". It is withdrawn:
her little souls are the children she expects at the party (R32; one reading for R15, R30 and R32).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That "Remnant" is a technical term with no bearing on the dead. It
loses on the machine's own name and on the company it keeps: the sheet is shown while the Cassette Man speaks of
small souls "set to new purpose".

**On fire.** See R28. The blueprint records a risk, written by the people who wanted the effect. That the Cassette
Man chose fire because of it is an inference (medium-low).

**Wording.** "A blueprint specifies", never "Afton Robotics kept... and injected". Not "in the metal": no record
mentions metal or any source for the substance. The phrase came from the adjudicator's memory and not from a
record, which is the fault the source policy warns of.
Not "the games say three things": quote the sheet.

**Would change if.** A game defined the word.

Review: all seven of group B's findings are accepted. The sheet is now quoted whole, where the first version
said "three things and no more" and left out its most suggestive note. Design notes are no longer told as
practice. The link to Sister Location's scooper is marked as an inference. "What the dead leave in the metal"
is removed. "Nobody designed the first hauntings" becomes the Cassette Man's ignorance, the Puppet's act and an
argument from silence. "Then" now cites the one record that states an order, for what it covers. And Scrap
Baby's record has one reading across three rulings.

**Depends on.** R1, R12 (the place of the Funtime machines). **Carries.** The thesis; R14; R28; R30.

### R16. Ultimate Custom Night: who the player is, who "the one you should not have killed" is, who runs it, and who Old Man Consequences addresses

**Ruling.**

1. **Status.** Scott gave no ruling on UCN. The model treats the gameplay and roster as staging and the voice
   lines and cutscenes as text (W3). That goes beyond his one precedent, and rulings 2 to 5 are all conditional
   on it.
2. **The player is most likely William Afton. This is the model's lean, and no more than a lean.** On it, the
   killer, dead in the fire, is kept by one of his victims in a night that does not end. Michael is a full
   alternative, behind by a narrow margin. [CHANGE: the current theory says Michael, medium]
3. **"The one you should not have killed" is, on the model's reading, the one who runs the night.** One
   Nightmare says it was "remade" by him (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`), two performers say a performance was
   "demanded" or "requested" of them (`ucn-summoned-performers`), and a second, whispering voice is layered
   under three rare lines, with no speaker label (`ucn-happy-frog-never-rest`, `ucn-nedd-bear-forever`,
   `ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`); no line says who keeps the night. So the ruling has three parts, each
   with its own mark (set apart in round 4d): (a) that the accuser is someone the player killed is stated; (b)
   that he runs the night, and that the voice which refuses release is his, is the model's reading (medium, at
   its lower end, and inside W3 like everything in this ruling); (c) which child he is, is a lean (low). Part
   (b) does not turn on who the player is: the lines' own words carry it ("remade... by the one you should not
   have killed"; "He's here, and always watching"; "I will hold you here... No matter how many times they burn
   us"), and the second voice under the rare lines is support, held as a description of the audio. He is a child the player killed. Which
   child, the records do not settle. The model leans to the fifth of the five, the child the yellow bear marks, and keeps
   FNaF4's crying child as the live alternative. [CHANGE: the current theory says FNaF4's crying child, flat]
4. **Old Man Consequences.** (a) If William is in the office, the old man's listener is not the man in the
   office. Ruling 2 needs this and cannot be firmer than it. (b) Who the listener then is has two candidates,
   and the model does not choose: the person playing, whom the old man addressed about the game itself in his
   only other speaking scene; or the accuser, told to let the demon go and rest.
5. **The last cutscene, Golden Freddy alone,** is wordless. The model reads it as showing who is left. That
   reading follows from R8 and from ruling 3, and is support for neither.

**Confidence.** (1) medium. (2) low-medium. (4a) low-medium: it stands and falls with (2). (3) that the accuser is
someone the player killed is stated in the lines; that he runs the night, and that the voice which refuses
release is his, is the model's reading of those lines, medium at its lower end, and is told with "the model
reads"; that he is one of William's victims is as (2); which child, low. [Round 4d: the second part had no
figure here, and one sentence added in round 4b gave it as "low", quoting R28. R28's "whose it is, low" graded
which victim he is, as step 21 does ("low for which victim"); it was never a grade for whether the voice is the
accuser's. The marker follows from the kind of claim: this is the archive's own interpretation, which the model
tells, so it takes "the model reads"; "may be" marks a possibility the model holds loosely and does not tell
(R27, "Markers and marks"). The figure is supplied, not moved] (4b) low for either listener. (5) low. [Revised after review: (2) was medium; (3) was low-medium for the
fifth child; (4) was one clause at low-medium]

**How the theory tells it.** The UCN chapter tells the William reading as "the model's lean", gives the Michael
reading in full beside it, and takes the confidence low-medium. Nothing outside that chapter may state the lean
as a fact: not the thesis, not the order of events, not `deaths.ts` or `relations.ts`.

**What Scott said about UCN, which is all there is.** It began as "an Ultimate Custom Night for Pizzeria
Simulator" (`scott-2018-ucn-for-pizzeria-simulator`), to be made without "teasers and cryptic hints"
(`scott-2018-custom-night-no-cryptic-hints`). Most characters "only say something if (when) they kill you"
(`scott-2018-ucn-voice-lines-when-they-kill-you`). And it was the "capstone" he felt the series needed, a game
that "brought in all of the characters" (`scott-2018-interview-ucn-capstone` (a transcript)). He never said whether
it is canon. His one precedent put a custom night's play outside canon and its cutscenes inside
(`scott-2016-sl-custom-night-not-canon`, `scott-2016-cutscenes-are-canon`). UCN's lines are spoken in play, when a
character kills the player. W3, which counts them as text, extends his precedent and does not follow from it.

**The lines that separate the two readings.** "W" fits William better, "M" fits Michael better, "=" fits both.
"Light" marks a row that should move nobody by itself. Every speaker is treated as an effigy made by whoever runs
the night (see below), so a line shows what its maker knows of the listener.

| Record | Words | Fits | Why |
|---|---|---|---|

A custom night continues the guard's seat (`fnaf1-custom-night-pink-slip`, `fnaf2-pink-slip-fritz-smith`), and that game's seat is the franchisee's: a light pull to M |

"Seeing you powerless is like music to me." | W, light | Her killer had power over her (`ffps-completion-shut-out`). So did the franchisee, with Lefty on his salvage table (`ffps-salvage-order`; the taser is noted under `ucn-roster-scrap-baby-controlled-shock`) |

No matter how many times they burn us." | = | "You" and "us" are kept apart, so the burnings are the speaker's. "He tried to release you" suits the volunteer with a way out planned at least as well as the man told not to keep the Devil waiting |
| `ucn-lefty-lines` | "I've been looking for you and now I will never let you go." | W, light | The machine built to catch the Puppet has been looking for the listener |
| `ucn-rockstar-bonnie-truest-friend` | "So good to see you again, my truest friend." | M, light | A Pizzeria Simulator product greeting someone it has met: the man who bought it. The catalogue sells Rockstar Bonnie as an animatronic for the pizzeria (`ffps-catalog-rockstar-bonnie`, added on 3 October from a fan wiki's table of the catalogue; the screen was not captured), which is what the row rests on |
| `sd-springtrap-pulled-apart-put-back-together` with `ucn-nightmare-fredbear-lines`, `ucn-nightmare-not-spared-not-saved` | Special Delivery's Springtrap later says to his own victims what UCN's Nightmares say to the player | W, light | A man repeating what was said to him. Another studio's game |

But the hunted one is drawn as Foxy, and FNaF4's brother wears that mask (`fnaf4-brother-foxy-mask-tv`): a light pull to M |

"a victim that can't perish" | M | The words a voice repeats over the fallen walker, Michael, in Sister Location's last walking cutscene (`sl-you-wont-die`; the game does not say whose voice). Against that, the rabbit's own line is "I always come back" (`ucn-afton-always-come-back`) |

"We know who our friends are, and you are not one of them!" | M | FNaF4's promise and FNaF4's "friends" thrown back at the one who was no friend, and whose earlier nights were illusion |
| `ucn-nedd-bear-forever` | "This is how it feels, and you get to experience it over, and over" | = | Fits the killer of children and the bully of one |
| `ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk` | "I am remade, but not by you. By the one you should not have killed." | = | The listener made such things once: the designer, or the brother with his masks |
| `ucn-circus-baby-lines`, `ucn-ballora-lines`, `ucn-scrap-baby-lines` ("controlled shock") | Sister Location's rooms and jobs | = | Sister Location's characters talk about Sister Location; the shock is UCN's own mechanic (`ucn-roster-scrap-baby-controlled-shock`), Ballora's line is her song (`sl-ballora-song`), the machine on the S.C.U.P. sheet, most likely Sister Location's scooper, is Afton Robotics' (`ffps-scup-blueprint`; the sheet does not use the word); and Sister Location says the machines took Michael for his father (`sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-they-thought-i-was-you`) |
| `ucn-roster-afton`, `ucn-roster-two-forms-at-once` | "Afton" attacks the player | = | So does Springtrap, on the same night. The roster's figures are effigies, "remade" and "given flesh" |

**The balance.** Michael strains on one clean line, "death cannot save you", and on a register (wickedness,
misdeeds, a lurer's cartoon) that is too large for what he did. William strains on more lines, each of them
answerable: the old man (ruling 4a), "He tried to release you", "You won't die" and "a victim that can't
perish", "I don't hate you", and Nightmare Fredbear's three lines. The first version of this ruling counted four
strains for Michael and one for William. That count was wrong. What is left is a lean: the lines that no reading
of Michael absorbs are the game's hardest words, and they are a judge's words to a murderer.

**Every speaker is an effigy.** To answer "Afton attacks the player", the roster must be figures "remade",
"given flesh" and summoned by whoever runs the night (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`,
`ucn-nightmare-freddy-cant-perish`, `ucn-summoned-performers`, `ucn-roster-two-forms-at-once`). The model applies
that to all of them, the Puppet and Withered Chica included. So a line shows what the one who runs the night
knows of the listener, and no UCN line is evidence for where the Puppet's child or the other four are after the
fire (R14, R28, R30). The first version used the Puppet's and Withered Chica's lines as those victims' own
testimony while treating "Afton" as an effigy. It cannot have both.

**The current theory's arguments for Michael, all six, tested.**
1. *"Several characters taunt him with details from Sister Location."* Each cited line has a nearer source than
   Michael (the Sister Location row above). Only "You won't die" survives as an echo.
2. *Afton "attacks the player... which is awkward if the player is Afton."* Springtrap and Afton attack on the same
   night, as do Baby twice and the Puppet twice. Scott's own guide lists the man twice
   (`scott-ucn-character-guide-afton`) and his store text calls the game a "mashup"
   (`scott-ucn-store-once-again-trapped`). Being attacked by an effigy says nothing about who the player is.
3. *Old Man Consequences "treats the player and the demon as two different people."* He treats his listener and
   the demon as two people. This is the argument with most force, and ruling 4 answers it only in part.
4. *Orville's line "puts the player among those burned"* in Pizzeria Simulator's fire, where Michael stayed. Both
   men were in that fire, and the line keeps "you" apart from the "us" who burn. Scored "=".
5. *The accusation belongs to FNaF4*, whose guilty party is the brother. This has real force: it is the Nightmare
   Fredbear row. With William in the office it needs the Nightmares to have been his work first, which R4 now
   rates low.
6. *The moral vocabulary*: Hell is kept for the killer, while UCN's ending tells its player to rest. Each reading
   gives a consistent map (Hell for the father and rest for the son; or Hell for the father, a night that is
   his, and rest offered to someone else). Consistency does not choose between them, and the first version was
   wrong to count it for William.

**Old Man Consequences.** With William in the office, "Leave the demon to his demons. Rest your own soul" cannot
be said to him. The line needs another listener, and two are available.
- *The person playing.* In his only other speaking scene the old man talks to the player about the game itself:
  "you have gone too deep into the code. There is no way back out" (`fw-old-man-consequences`; by its own words
  that scene is the game speaking about itself, R17). UCN reuses that screen, leaves the player nothing to do but
  sink, and closes the program (`ucn-omc-ending-drowning`, `fw-drowning-ending`). Scott called UCN his "capstone".
  On this reading the line tells whoever holds the controls to stop, and to leave the man in the office to the
