  roster. It needs no change of listener inside the fiction. Its cost is that the line then says nothing about
  the accuser.
- *The accuser.* The voice that refuses rest in this game is his (`ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`,
  `ucn-happy-frog-never-rest`). "Rest" is the word the Cassette Man uses to the victims, and "Hell" and "the Devil"
  the words he uses to the killer (`ffps-completion-rest`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`). Pigpatch's proverb
  is about sitting by a river until an enemy's body floats by (`ucn-pigpatch-proverbs`). And the game's other exit
  shows Golden Freddy alone (`ucn-golden-freddy-final-cutscene`). Its cost is that nothing on screen marks a change
  of listener, and the scene opens only when the player, in the office, catches the fish (`ucn-omc-ending`).

The first version argued that a night with every tormentor switched off is the accuser's own act. That used the
night-select screen as an event inside the story, which W3 forbids, and it rested on an `implied` record whose
note says "Who 'demanded' or 'requested' is unstated" (`ucn-summoned-performers`). It is withdrawn. One more
record is on file and names nobody: under the scene a person can be heard screaming
(`ucn-omc-ending-scream-audio`), which suits a listener who is not the one suffering.

**The accuser: what the records fix, whoever the player is.** He is male (`ucn-withered-chica-toysnhk`,
`ucn-mangle-toysnhk`); the face the game gives him is a small child's (`ucn-toysnhk-face`), and "The one you should
not have killed" is Scott's own label for him, in a passage about family photographs that he implies carry no
lore (`scott-2019-ucn-child-face-jason`, `scott-2019-fnaf4-photos-no-lore`); he is not Withered Chica's "first";
the player killed him; one Nightmare says it was "remade" by him (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`); he is named in the same breath as "the fire" (`ucn-jack-o-chica-toysnhk`); a voice refuses release for itself
and for the player (stated; that the voice is his is the model's reading, medium at its lower end: ruling 3 (b)); and the last thing the game shows is Golden Freddy in his FNaF1
form (`ucn-golden-freddy-final-cutscene`).

**Which child.** With William in the office the accuser is one of his victims. Two traits point to the fifth of
the five: "killed" in its plain sense, and the last cutscene, read through R8. Two point to FNaF4's crying
child: one of the Nightmares, which on R4 are what the boy in the bed fought, says he "remade" it, and using
the Death Coin on Golden Freddy, alone at level 1, makes Fredbear attack the player, which no text explains
(`ucn-death-coin-fredbear`). The model leans to the fifth child, because no record has
William kill the crying child, and it rates the lean low. Three supports in the first version are withdrawn.
"Most of you" in the Cassette Man's speech does not leave room for a child: its exception is named in the next
sentence, the "old friend" (`ffps-completion-peace-perhaps-more`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`). "Outlasted
the second fire" had no record. And the last cutscene is not independent support: it reads as the fifth child
only because R8 says the yellow bear is the fifth child's. For Fazbear's Fright the one record is the faint fifth
head (`fnaf3-bad-ending-heads`). A caution, because the source policy asks for one: the fifth child is also the
fandom's standard answer, and its usual support is the Survival Logbook, which this archive does not admit. The
lean here stands on the two traits named and on nothing else.

**Records against.**
- Against William: `ucn-omc-ending` on its plain sense; `ucn-scrap-baby-lines` ("You won't die") and
  `ucn-nightmare-freddy-cant-perish`; `ucn-nightmare-fredbear-lines`; "He tried to release you"
  (`ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`); "I don't hate you" (`ucn-puppet-lines`); `ucn-rockstar-bonnie-truest-friend`;
  `ucn-circus-baby-lines`; `ucn-mr-hippo-just-a-story` (the game's own warning against over-reading);
  `ucn-protagonist-unnamed-killer` (no line names anyone); `scott-ucn-store-once-again-trapped` ("you will once
  again be trapped alone in an office": sales copy addressed to the buyer, but on its face it suits someone who
  has sat in such an office before).
- Against the fifth child as accuser: nothing ties that child to FNaF4's Nightmares except the colour of a bear;
  `ucn-death-coin-fredbear` turns Golden Freddy into the bear that bit the crying child; Happiest Day shows a
  golden-masked child leaving with the others (`fnaf3-happiest-day`); "should not have" is never explained.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** The Michael package, stated as its advocate would. UCN was built as
the custom night of a game whose player is Michael, who chose to stay in the fire. He is told what Michael was
told and nobody else: "You won't die". The monsters with the heaviest lines are FNaF4's, and FNaF4's guilty party
is the brother: one is "remade, but not by you", they were illusion then and are real "this time", and the
plush toys' "friends" exclude him. "The one you should not have killed" is the brother whose death he caused and
did not mean. "He tried to release you" is the way out planned for the volunteer. The Puppet does not hate him.
And the game's one spoken ending tells him that the demon is someone else and that he may rest, which carries
the Cassette Man's sorting through: Hell for the father, rest for the son. It is coherent, and it has the old
man's line on its plain sense. It loses, narrowly, on three things. "This time, death cannot save you" has no
sense for a man death never saved. "Your wickedness", "your misdeeds" and a cartoon of a serial lurer describe a
career and not one prank. And it has to seat the crying child behind Golden Freddy, which R8's count denies.

**Consequences, followed through.**
- *The household (R3)* no longer leans on UCN. It stands on Sister Location's records.
- *The older brother (R3, R13)* is Michael on Sister Location's evidence; the relations that cite
  `ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk` and `ucn-scrap-baby-lines` for it lose those citations.
- *The crying child (R6)* is not identified with the accuser by the model. He is the live alternative for the
  accuser, and UCN's two links to FNaF4 (the Nightmares; the Death Coin's Fredbear) are counter-evidence to the
  model's lean in the UCN and Golden Freddy chapters.
- *Golden Freddy (R8)*: the final cutscene is neutral for R8. It shows Golden Freddy last and says nothing of
  whose he is.
- *The moral map* reads consistently on both readings and is a point for neither. With William in the office:
  Hell is named for the killer, a victim keeps him, and rest is offered to a listener who is not the demon. With
  Michael there: Hell for the father and rest for the son.
- *FNaF4 (R4)*: "the illusion was William's doing" needs this ruling's lean and falls to low; R4's second clause
  falls to "medium, lower end", because with Michael in the office the nights would read as the brother's.
- *What the place is*: the characters call it a prison, a nightmare, an afterlife or a dream
  (`ucn-withered-bonnie-prison`, `ucn-nightmarionne-nightmare-you-wont-wake-from`, `ucn-mr-hippo-afterlife-or-dream`,
  `ucn-mr-hippo-youre-dead`); in the archive's records of the game none says "Hell". Orville's line has "he" trying to release the listener and the
  speaker refusing. So UCN is not "the night the Cassette Man promised him", as the first version put it: on the
  model's lean it is a night one victim keeps him in, against that attempt. The word Hell is Pizzeria
  Simulator's; in the archive's records Ultimate Custom Night never says it. [Reworded in round 4c: the flat
  "never says" was a universal that the records cannot carry]
- *After the fire*: on this reading the man in the office is dead (`ucn-mr-hippo-youre-dead`). `deaths.ts` keeps
  William among the dead of the fire. What persists of him is this ruling's question, R18's and R20's, at their
  confidences; R33 says what they come to together. No data file may say "burned, not ended".

**Would change if.** A line naming the player; a game showing Michael after the fire, or showing William anywhere
else at that time; a statement from Scott on UCN's standing.

Review: group B found that William is still the better reading of the office and that "medium" does not survive.
Both halves are accepted, with all twelve findings. The player is now the model's lean at low-medium, tied to
ruling 4a, which is split from 4b; the person playing is added as a second listener; the set-up argument is
withdrawn and Pigpatch is cited; Orville's line, the custom-night origin, the volunteer and "I was the first"
are rescored "=", and the Puppet's lines and Jack-O-Chica's "W, light"; three records are added; the count of
strains is restated; every speaker is an effigy; the accuser's identity is a lean at low, with three supports
withdrawn and the cutscene made neutral for R8; Scott's four statements are cited and W3 is said to go beyond
them; all six of the current chapter's arguments are tested; and the thesis, section 5 and R28 no longer say
that the fire did not end him. The adjudicator added two things to the table unprompted, both against the
model's lean: the row on the accuser's title, and the pull toward Michael in the cartoon of the Bear and the Fox.
The editor's question was which reading the theory tells: it tells this one, as a lean, with the other beside
it.

**Depends on.** R1, R8, R13, R17 (the old man's first scene), W3. **Carries.** R3's argument (by removal), R4 (two
clauses), R6, the moral-map chapter, R28, R33.

### R17. How much of FNaF World is story

**Ruling.** Three layers, sorted by the game's own words.

1. **The adventure is outside the story.** Its endings and Update 2 call it "an imaginary game" and talk about it
   as a product.
2. **The frame voice is inside, as a deliberate echo.** Two dots in the dark speak FNaF4's last sentences. A
   glitching 8-bit Fredbear then sets the player a task, to "leave breadcrumbs for him", and the player sets five
   clocks; the model reads the five as FNaF3's path to Happiest Day (one is an exact match, the code 395248, and
   four are resemblances). The dots end with "Rest." The frame adds no event. It shows what Scott meant "put you
   back together" to lead to in early 2016. Support weight; never the only support for a fact (W4).
3. **The man at the desk is a teaser for Sister Location**, spoken by a maker of games inside a game about games.
   Colour. He is not to be identified with any character.

Old Man Consequences matters only because UCN brings him back (R16, R25). His own scene belongs to layer 1: by
its words, "you have gone too deep into the code", it is the game talking about itself, and the one addressed is
the person playing (`fw-old-man-consequences`). R16 uses that for the question of whom he addresses in UCN.
[CHANGE: the current theory cites the clock ending in three chapters with no rule, and misidentifies its trophy]

**Confidence.** (1) high. (2) medium-low as a reading of where it points; high that it is deliberate. (3) high.

**Records for.** (1) `fw-normal-ending-still-in-control`, `fw-hard-ending-killing-the-storyteller`,
`fw-update2-game-bombed`, `fw-chippers-revenge-ending` (an optional boss complaining that Freddy eclipsed the
author's gentler games). (2) `fw-opening-safe-place-something-went-wrong`, `fw-opening-put-you-back-together`,
`fw-8bit-fredbear-breadcrumbs`, `fw-8bit-fredbear-true-goal`, `fw-clock-games-five-tasks`, `fw-clock-game-395248`,
`fw-clock-ending-still-your-friends`, `fw-clock-ending-pieces-in-place`. (3) `fw-desk-man-deactivated-my-games`,
`fw-desk-man-made-something-terrible`. Scott's two statements bracket it: FNaF World would "not add to" the story
(`scott-2015-story-complete`), and he should not have "tried to somehow tie it in to a canon game"
(`scott-2018-interview-fnaf-world-tied-to-canon-mistake` (a transcript)).

**Records against.** `fw-crying-child-character`: the one time a game prints "Crying Child" as a name it is on the
generic weeping child of FNaF2's and FNaF3's minigames, which is not drawn as FNaF4's boy. On that drawing the
clock ending's trophy does not point at FNaF4's child (a reading, as the record now says); only the wording
does. `fw-android-port-clock-ending`: the Android port of January
2017, on sale for about a day, rewrote the clock ending's closing screens as a joke ("I caused the bite of 87...
All hail Bouncepot!"). It does not change what the 2016 release says, and its line about the bite is evidence of
nothing; it does show how lightly the frame was held a year later, and it is one more reason the frame voice stays
at support weight. It is filed here as counter-evidence, not outside.

**How the late records were read.** `fw-android-port-clock-ending`, `fw-drowning-ending` and
`fw-chippers-revenge-ending` were added after the analyst's image input failed: their text was read by OCR and
checked against independent readers and the wiki's transcripts, not seen by the analyst. Nothing in this ruling
rests on any of the three.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the whole game is outside. It loses because Scott published the
"put you back together" screen as an advertisement and one clock code is an exact match to FNaF3's.

**Would change if.** Scott ruled on it.

Review: group B found that the ruling holds and asked for two repairs, both made. The old man's scene is given
its layer, and the listener it addresses is named. And the frame's wording is corrected: the player sets the
clocks, at 8-bit Fredbear's bidding, and "FNaF3's path" is a reading of five tasks of which one is an exact
match.

**Depends on.** Nothing. **Carries.** R6 (one lead), R16 (the old man's listener), W4.

### R18. Help Wanted: the in-universe "games are fiction" frame, and what the rabbit in the code is

**Ruling on the frame.** The events are real and decades old. Games about them exist inside the story and are a
few years old. The company says they were lies by "a complete lunatic"; its own emails, as the tape speaker reports
them, say it hired the developer. Scott makes canon only that such a developer exists, who "supposedly" made games
"based on actual events" that "may or may not be a cover up". So:

- The frame does **not** unmake the earlier games' events. They stay the archive's record of what happened. The
  reason, which an earlier version of this ruling did not give: a level is demoted to "what the company staged"
  only when the game that contains it frames it as a product inside the story (W5). Help Wanted frames its own
  levels so. No earlier game frames itself so, and Scott limited what Help Wanted's frame makes canon to the
  developer's existence (`scott-2019-only-game-developer-existence-canon`). The tape's claim that the indie games
  were made "to conceal and make light of what happened" tells us that such games exist in the story and may
  distort. It does not turn FNaF1 to UCN into company stagings.
- Inside the story the earlier games exist as entertainment that may distort. No record says where, so the model
  does not discount any particular fact on that ground.
- "The games are the cover-up" is `strong`, not `canon`. [CHANGE: `home.ts` has it as canon]
- Whatever is built into the VR levels is a company depiction (W5). [CHANGE: the child's hat, watch and shoe
  that "Help Wanted finds... inside Freddy" are a staged prop. They may be shown as agreeing with what the calls
  and clippings already carry; they establish nothing, and they leave `relations.ts`]

**Ruling on the rabbit.** Three statements.

1. **A personality came off old company circuit boards scanned into the VR game, took hold in the code, and acts
   with purpose.** Stated. Which machine the boards came from, no game says.
2. **It presents as the killer.** A man's shape in the killer's working costume, his kind of lure, six
   gravestones on its hidden game's route, six "memories" of his victims in its keeping, and a line that fans
   decode as his.
3. **What it is, the games do not settle.** The model prefers a leaving of the man on old company boards to a
   copying program that had learned him. It is not the whole man.

[CHANGE: "purple rabbit" is wrong; "a personality carried on boards from his machines" overstates what is
recorded, and so did this ruling's own "carried on his hardware", which is withdrawn]

**Confidence.** Frame: high that events are real and the in-universe games came later; medium that the company
commissioned them. Rabbit: (1) high. (2) medium-high. (3) a leaving of William Afton: medium, and it leans on the
convention that the scan followed the fire (R33); a copying program that reached the company's hardware:
low-medium; the man himself: low.

**Records for the frame.** `hw-intro-loosely-based-on-actual-events`, `hw-intro-complete-lunatic`,
`hw-intro-vr-game-to-move-past-ghost-stories`, `hw-intro-closed-police-line-poster`, `hw-tape-13-cover-story`,
`hw-tape-13-fe-hired-developer`, `scott-2019-only-game-developer-existence-canon`,
`scott-2019-hw-rebrand-or-part-of-the-plan`, `scott-2019-help-wanted-is-canon`. Inside the company the old machines
are real: `sd-rebuilds-of-original-animatronics`, `sd-mangle-original-restaurants`, `sd-vintage-bonnie-reports`; and
the Pizzaplex stands on a real buried pizzeria (`security-breach-underground-pizza-place`).

**Records for (1).** `hw-tape-05`, `hw-tape-05-client-sent-junk-to-scan`, `hw-handunit-vintage-control-boards`
("digitally recreating performances and personalities from the past"). Purpose: it singles out whoever is in the
headset (`hw-tape-02`, `hw-tape-07`, `hw-jeremy-self-injury-implied`) and "wants to escape... through someone...
plugged into this game" (`hw-tape-16`).

**Records for (2), "presents as the killer".**
- Look: a grinning, person-shaped figure in a golden-yellow rabbit costume; only the vest, bow tie and eyes are purple
  (`hw-rabbit-figure-appearance`). A person's shape, not a machine's. The tapes call it "he" (`hw-tape-05`).
- Conduct: in a level that ends in a birthday room it steps from behind the curtain and beckons, and the player
  ends on a stage in a performer's place while it dances (`hw-pizza-party-birthday-room`, `hw-pizza-party-ending`).
  The room is the level's furniture and so the company's; the figure's part in it is an intrusion (W5).
- His victims: six unmarked gravestones with braziers on Princess Quest's route
  (`hw-mobile-princess-quest-graveyard`; counted on a screenshot that may be of the arcade version), against the
  six stones that close Pizzeria Simulator (`ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`); and, in Help Wanted 2, six
  "memories", dolls in the masks of the four, the yellow bear and the Puppet, each held out by a costumed arm
  (`hw2-memory-dolls`). These are better support than the decoded line.
- A staged prop that agrees: in the same level Springtrap watches from the window of the FNaF3 office
  (`hw-pizza-party-springtrap-window`).
- Words: when it is near, a low-pitched clip plays, which a fan wiki's audio page identifies as the first
  tape's greeting pitched down, and no other speech plays in the hub (`hw-glitchtrap-uses-tape-girl-voice`;
  that the clip is the figure's own voice is a reading). In the last room of Princess Quest a rabbit-eared creature hangs over a
  line of glyphs whose letter pattern fits "I ALWAYS COME BACK" (`hw-mobile-princess-quest-final-room`). The decode
  is fans' work and the record is `implied`: "Neither is readable or audible as English in the game itself"
  (`hw-mobile-princess-quest-ending`). The line would be that of `ffps-scraptrap-always-come-back`.

**Records bearing on (3).**
- For a leaving on boards. The mechanism this era states is that a board carries a personality: "personalities
  from the past" (`hw-handunit-vintage-control-boards`); "a whole new personality" on a swappable part
  (`sd-cpu-whole-new-personality`); rebuilt machines that speak the originals' lines, claims of identity included
  (`sd-circus-baby-something-bad-inside`, `sd-springtrap-did-not-save-the-others`). Scott's hidden teaser lines:
  "We let something inside." "It was an accident." (`scottgames-hw-teaser-hidden-text`).
- For a copying program. In 1979 the copying mind is already software that has been copied from one body to
  another (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago`). The party signing W.A. bid for Murray's "software
  development", and Fazbear sent a man for the blueprints (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-wa-far-behind`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-1979`). The figure's one utterance in the hub is someone else's recording played
  back, which is how the Murray machine speaks in both games where it speaks (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-turn-around`,
  `ruin-mimic-reuses-gregory-lines`). Help Wanted 2 puts the Murray boy's doll in the same costumed arm
  (`hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger`). And someone at the Pizzaplex "developed software programs that talk to
  you and repeat phrases", whose logs came to show "someone else responding" (`security-breach-cd46-talking-programs`).
- Scott (a transcript): "anything that involves possession of AI or possession of machines or circuitry... I love
  the idea of Glitchtrap" (`scott-2024-interview-glitchtrap-possession-of-ai`). As transcribed, the phrase says
  what is possessed and not by whom. An earlier version of this ruling leaned on the word "possession"; nothing
  is built on it now.

**Records against the model's preference.** `sd-which-board-unknown` and `hw-tape-05`: no game says whose board.
Every record under (2) is surface. A copier that had learned the killer would show all of it and would say "I
always come back" too, so (2) cannot choose between the two readings of (3). Nothing in Help Wanted, Curse of
Dreadbear or Special Delivery mentions Afton, his family or his death.

**Strongest alternative, and why it does not win.** A copying program that reached the company's hardware:
Murray's software, wearing what it learned from the killer as the second machine wore Edwin's rage. An earlier
version of this ruling answered it with the concrete under the Pizzaplex. That answers a body, and the
alternative is a program. What does answer it is weaker and is this. No record puts Murray's software on any
Fazbear board. Help Wanted's own account of what was scanned names nothing of Murray's: only old hardware the
client sent, "circuit boards and things like that" (`hw-tape-05`), and HandUnit adds "scanned photographs for
reference" and "original performance routines" (`hw-handunit-scanned-photos-original-routines`). And nothing in
Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear or Special Delivery carries a Murray mark (orange eyes, the costumes, 4625,
Fiona, David): those appear only in later games, the White Tiger doll in Help Wanted 2 and the rest in RUIN and
Secret of the Mimic. The alternative needs two steps that no record shows (the program on a company board; the
program near the killer), where the model's preference needs one (whose board it was). That is a margin and not
a refutation: medium against low-medium.

**Smaller rulings in this thread.**
- *Two firms, two outbreaks* (medium-high). The VR studio of the tapes and Special Delivery's contractor are
  different companies. After the second scan the company's lawyers act in the next email delivered, naming
  "vintage hardware", before the contractor reports a virus (the emails carry no dates, so "within a day" would
  measure the story by delivery days) (`sd-contractor-storing-animatronics`,
  `sd-circuit-board-scan-virus`, `sd-fe-legal-halt-vintage-hardware`, `sd-virus-traced-to-fe-board`). That they knew
  the risk before the boards were sent is not shown.
- *Which Help Wanted ending the later games build on*: the Trapped ending (medium-high). The plush exists only
  after it, the hidden call needs the plush, and Princess Quest III ends at its door (`hw-trapped-ending`,
  `hw-trapped-ending-plush`, `codb-mask-plush-voice`, `security-breach-pq3-help-wanted-hub-trapped-door`). The kill
  switch was thrown and the thing kept her anyway. [CHANGE: the timeline says "the anomaly merges"]
- *Jeremy* cut his own face while in the visor: implied, medium-high. That he died is not stated. [CHANGE:
  `people.ts` "Presumed dead", `deaths.ts` lists him]
- *The tape speaker's* fate is unknown, and nothing links her to the woman on the hidden call: she fights the
  thing, the caller serves it.
- *Special Delivery's unlisted "vintage Bonnie".* Stated: customers report a machine that is in none of the
  service's databases, "some kind of vintage Bonnie model", with a bad smell (`sd-vintage-bonnie-reports`). By the
  staff's own account it is not one of their rebuilds. Read plainly it is Springtrap or something like him: an old
  rabbit machine that smells, at large. When, the emails do not say. If the scan followed the fire, which is the model's convention,
  this is a physical Springtrap abroad after the fire: low-medium. If the scan came first, it is Pizzeria
  Simulator's "something outside that's been trying to get in for months" (`ffps-tutorial-something-outside`),
  before the fire. The model does not choose (R33).

**How this sits with the other things that carry something of him.** R33 says what the model holds about William
Afton after the fire, taking this ruling, R16, R20 and the email together.

**Would change if.** A game said which machine the boards came from; showed Murray's software on a company
board, or the endoskeleton connected to the VR studio; or showed the figure knowing something only the killer
could know.

**Depends on.** R1, W5; R33 for the order of the fire and the scan. **Carries.** R19, R20, R21, R33.

Review: the third review overturned nothing here and asked for nine changes; all are made. The rival reading is
restated as a program and rated low-medium, not low. "Presents as the killer" (medium-high) is split from "is
something of the killer" (medium). "His hardware" and the flat "a decoded line gives it his words" are gone. The
six stones, the six dolls and the watcher at the window are added on one side, and the reused recording, the
doll and the talk-back programs on the other. The "vintage Bonnie" is no longer placed after the fire at
medium. The reason the frame does not demote the earlier games is written down.

### R19. The rabbit-costumed woman: Vanessa, Ness, one person or a role. Which Security Breach ending stands

**Ruling.**

1. **Vanny is Vanessa**, acting under the control of whatever writes to her. One woman. This is the model's
   reading, held against two records of the game itself (5). [Lowered from medium-high to medium in round 4c]
2. **Ness is Vanessa earlier on**, and **Help Wanted's player is the woman on the hidden call**. The chain plush,
   mask, call, emails, therapy recordings, Princess Quest is one story about one follower.
3. **"A role anyone can be pulled into, not a single woman" survives in one sense only.** The costume has one
   wearer. The mask is company equipment that passes from hand to hand. And the position the thing needs,
   "someone plugged in", is filled by a succession of people. The list breaks in the middle: by RUIN the voice
   that uses the mask's wearer is the endoskeleton's (R21). [CHANGE: the hypothesis is restated; its cited
   support was wrong]
4. **RUIN follows no single ending of Security Breach.** The building it shows is the aftermath of the
   underground route. That Vanessa was freed, as the Princess Quest (Redemption) ending has it, is the model's
   reading. [CHANGE: adds which branch the later games continue; lowered and split after review]
5. **Two records count against (1), and no record explains either.** The rooftop page, which shows a blonde
   woman in a "SECURITY" cap and uniform on the burning roof, looking down, in the ending that has just shown a
   blonde woman unmasked on the ground, counts at full weight: who is inside the
   costume is settled before the endings divide, so the page cannot be set aside as a branch (W10). The cast
   list, which gives "Vanessa" and "Vanny" to two performers as two parts, counts at support weight, the weight
   casting has in R30. The chapter shows the page, cites the list, and says the model has no account of the
   first and only a guess at the second. [The cast list was added in round 4c]

**Confidence.** (1) medium. Two things carry it, the recordings and the name; two records count against it, one
at full weight. (1) is one sentence and one grade: who she is, and that she acts under the control of what
writes to her, are both medium, so a tie from the rabbit in the code to Vanny is theory, medium (confirmed in
round 4d, where the people layer asked). [Was medium-high, called its ceiling while the rooftop page stood alone. Round 4c: the cast
credits are now a record, and the page that leaves the mask on the floor was re-read from its record, which
shows a blonde woman and prints no name] (2) Ness is Vanessa: medium-high; player
is the caller: medium-high; caller is Vanessa: medium. (3) succession of mask wearers: medium; simultaneous
wearers: not supported. (4) the building follows the underground route: medium; Vanessa's release: low-medium.
(5) high that the page is unexplained; the cast list at support weight. That Princess Quest is a remedy that
works: low-medium.

**Records for.**
- (1) The recordings: a therapist greets her by name, and an audit has her shopping for a costume and buying
  fake fur, in secret, with "He said he would always be watching" (`security-breach-cd-vanessa-costume-fur`,
  `security-breach-cd-vanessa-encrypted-conversation`); messages from someone who has read her personal files
  (`security-breach-cd-vanessa-manipulative-messages`); interview notes in which she lacks the experience for
  Chief Security Officer and the recommendation for her "came from the top"
  (`security-breach-msg-marked-for-deletion`). The name: Gregory takes "Vanny" from the hideout,
  and Freddy says it is too like "Vanessa" and "Bunny" to be a coincidence
  (`security-breach-gregory-finds-vanny-hideout`, `security-breach-freddy-vanny-vanessa-bunny`). The likeness:
  under the mask on the rooftop's third page is a blonde woman drawn like her and not named
  (`security-breach-rooftop-unmasked`), and once Princess Quest is finished the mask and a bow tie lie on the
  floor and a blonde woman, drawn as Vanessa is and not named, waits at the doors
  (`security-breach-ending-redemption`). The likeness belongs to both readings: two women would have to look
  alike, as the rooftop's two pages already draw them, so these pages show what the wearer looks like and do
  not tell one woman from two. Scott speaks of Vanny as one character
  (`scott-2024-interview-vanny-underused` (a transcript)); marginal. The recordings and the name carry (1), and
  they are enough for medium. [Round 4c: the first version had "Vanessa at the doors" and counted that page
  as a third leg; the record shows a blonde woman and prints no name]
- (2) Four independent links between Ness and Vanessa: a Luis who reviews her online activity
  (`sd-ness-blood-eagle-calendar`; `security-breach-cd-vanessa-encrypted-conversation`); things the monitoring
  caught her buying, three "lifelike, human male rubber masks" and fake fur for a costume (`sd-ness-rubber-masks`;
  `security-breach-cd-vanessa-costume-fur`; what the masks were for, the email does not say); voices at her
  screen, and a watcher (`sd-ness-voices-at-screen`, where Luis "thought you were on a conference call";
  `codb-mask-plush-voice`; "He said he would always be watching"); a later transfer
  (`security-breach-cd-vanessa-transfer`). The player and the call: `hw-waiver-qa-team`, `hw-trapped-ending-plush`,
  `codb-corn-maze-rabbit-mask`, `codb-mask-plush-voice-opening`, `codb-mask-plush-voice`,
  `scottgames-sb-source-stay-the-course`, `security-breach-pq3-help-wanted-hub-trapped-door`. By the order
  in which the emails arrive, itself a convention (section 3.2), the first of Luis's emails, about her searches,
  comes before the contractor's scan goes wrong (`sd-ness-blood-eagle-calendar` precedes
  `sd-circuit-board-scan-virus`); the masks, the "compliance" searches and the voices come after it. On that
  first email alone the model reads her contact as coming through the VR game and not the second outbreak.
- (3) `hw2-poster-take-off-your-mask` (the mask as staff equipment), `hw2-remove-mask-real-world`,
  `hw2-pq4-vanny-mask-trade`, `hw2-faz-force-ending`, `ruin-security-mask-left-by-faz-technician`,
  `ruin-mask-bot-helpi-vanni`, `ruin-item-gregory-seen-this-mask-before`; the stated aim, `hw-tape-16`. Three served
  the thing in the code: Jeremy in the visor, the Help Wanted player, Ness at her screen
  (`sd-ness-voices-at-screen`). The technician in the mask and Cassie in the mask come after the break: they were
  used by whatever was speaking by then, which R21 takes to be the endoskeleton.
- (4) The building: wrecked, with newspapers that put it down to an earthquake, by a fan wiki's plot summary
  (`ruin-pizzaplex-abandoned-earthquake`); Roxanne
  without her eyes (`ruin-roxy-give-me-back-my-eyes`); a collapsed raceway (`ruin-something-tunneled`, where the
  cause is given by the lying voice); and a page of Gregory's that draws the burnt rabbit beside the mass, found
  next to the M.X.E.S. room (`ruin-gregory-comics-depict-endings`). All of that is seen with the mask off.
  Vanessa's release: the Princess Quest cabinet toppled, with a sword through it that only the mask shows, in a
  record taken from the wiki's plot summary (`ruin-vanny-hideout-glitchtrap-shadow`); the hill of the Redemption
  ending's last page, seen only inside the mask, with a blonde woman under the tree whom the image does not
  name (`ruin-ending-safe-spot`); an unnamed friend with the building's
  maps (`ruin-gregory-friend-maps`); a fourth Princess Quest that continues the third, inside a simulation
  (`hw2-pq4-vanny-mask-trade`).

**Records against.**
- (1) `security-breach-rooftop-vanessa-alive`: after the fall and the unmasking, the post-credits page shows a
  blonde woman in Vanessa's cap and uniform on the burning roof, looking down. The page prints no name.
- (1) `security-breach-credits-vanessa-vanny-two-parts`: the cast list gives "Vanessa" and "Glamrock Chica" to
  one performer and "Gregory", "Vanny" and "Roxanne" to another. The record was read from the wiki's
  transcription of the credits, not from a capture of the credits roll: the comparison is cited with the tag
  "(a fan wiki's account)" (R27, "Cast lists").
  *What it shows.* The game casts the guard and the rabbit-costumed woman as two parts with two voices. A
  credit names a voiced part and not a person in the story, so the list says neither that they are two women
  nor that they are one.
  *What it weighs, and why.* The model counts casting in two ways elsewhere, and both apply here. A credit can
  name a part: Pizzeria Simulator's list gives the rabbit's voice the name "William Afton", and R1 takes that,
  with Scott's written guide and the roster, as the statement of who he is (`ffps-credits-william-afton`; R1,
  link c). This list makes no such statement for
  the woman in the costume: it gives "Vanny" to another performer, on another line. And a credit records
  who is heard: R30 counts two returning performers as support, and not as proof, that two machines are the
  ones heard before (`ffps-credits-returning-voices`). By the same rule two performers here are support, and
  not proof, that two people are heard. In one respect the fact weighs less here than in R30: (1) is about who
  is inside the costume, and it holds that she acts under another's control, which a second voice suits. In
  another it weighs more: one performer taking several parts is common, and both performers do it in this
  list, while one part split between two performers is not. The model sets the two against each other and
  counts the list at support weight. It would not carry the two-women reading alone, and it does not stand
  alone: it is that reading's second record, of a different kind from the first.
  Help Wanted 2's list gives "Vanny" to the same performer (`hw2-credits-vanny`), and by the record's note the
  wiki's transcription of RUIN's cast list has neither part.
- (4) No later game shows Vanessa with the mask off. The Redemption ending's own last page shows the blonde
  woman on a hill with the boy and Freddy's head, from behind and unnamed
  (`security-breach-ending-redemption`). Gregory's comics draw every ending
  except Redemption, which reads either as "the others are his stories" or as "he saw those things"
  (`ruin-gregory-comics-depict-endings`). Princess Quest IV is a game inside a simulation. The underground route
  does not include Redemption.
- (4), a record that was checked and does not count against. A headless Glamrock Freddy body attacks Cassie in
  Fazer Blast, below the hideout where two of Security Breach's endings have Freddy taken apart and a third has
  Gregory leave with his head. The word "PROTOTYPE" runs along the sole of its foot, the right foot by the
  wiki's description (`ruin-prototype-freddy-headless`). The label was viewed on 3 October 2026 in a capture of the game that the
  wiki holds, not in play. So the game marks this body as a prototype: it is a different machine from the Freddy
  of Security Breach's night, and it does not count against the underground route. [Added in round 4b as a record
  against that turned on an unviewed detail; corrected the same morning, when the label was viewed]
- No game calls Ness "Vanessa" or names the caller.

**Errors to remove.** Help Wanted 2 never "calls its trainee's headset 'the Vanny mask'": that is the wiki's label
(`hw2-pq4-vanny-mask-trade`); the name appears in that game only in the cast credits (`hw2-credits-vanny`). RUIN's
mask assistant is Helpi; V.A.N.N.I. is the network he welcomes the wearer into (`ruin-mask-bot-helpi-vanni`).
"Vanessa's therapy notes" are recordings. The two teasers (`scottgames-site-sb-protect`, `scottgames-site-sb-obey`)
show a guard and a figure in a rabbit costume. Nothing in them splits one figure into two, and nothing joins
two into one, so they are cited for neither reading.

**What a machine sees.** Two games state that a machine cannot see a person inside a costume: Freddy cannot see
the rabbit woman (`security-breach-freddy-cannot-see-rabbit-lady`), and the 1979 endoskeleton cannot see a man in a
suit (`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-suit-cannot-see`). By the wiki's account RUIN's mask hides Cassie from the
building's endoskeletons (`ruin-mask-bot-helpi-vanni`), though the mask world is also where the rabbit-shaped
figure finds her and calls the animatronics to her (`ruin-the-entity`). The first two games have a neighbouring
idea, not the same one: there the machines do see the guard and take him for "an endoskeleton without his
costume on" (`fnaf1-call1-stuffed-into-suit`, `fnaf2-call1-endoskeleton-glitch`), and the second game's Freddy head
works on that mistake. That is seeing wrongly, not failing to see.

**Princess Quest.** Four games in which the thing is fought from inside. In the first, a rabbit-eared creature
hangs in a locked room over a line of glyphs (`hw-mobile-princess-quest-final-room`); "Let me out" is fans'
decode of reversed audio and is not on screen (`hw-mobile-princess-quest-ending`). In the second, by a fan wiki's account, an old man gives
the Princess a sword of light (`security-breach-pq2-old-man-fnaf1-office`). The third, by the same account,
replays the follower's capture and ends at a door like that of Help Wanted's Trapped ending
(`security-breach-pq3-help-wanted-hub-trapped-door`).
The fourth trades the mask back for the plush and crushes what comes out of it (`hw2-pq4-vanny-mask-trade`,
`hw2-pq4-vanny-crushes-glitchtrap`, `hw2-pq4-consequences-achievement`). What the games show is an attempt, and
its outcome is open. The crush is the secret ending of a game whose main ending, the one tied to RUIN, has a
costumed arm reaching out of a station (`hw2-faz-force-ending`). The same game shows the figure at work on the
instruction monitors, most likely after the Security Breach night (`hw2-glitchtrap-corrupts-backstage`; R22).
And RUIN, most likely the later of the two (R22: medium-high), still has a rabbit's shadow on the hideout wall, seen through the mask in a record taken from the wiki's
summary (`ruin-vanny-hideout-glitchtrap-shadow`). The rabbit-shaped figure of RUIN's mask world
(`ruin-the-entity`) is a different matter: R21 does not identify it with the rabbit in the code, so the
sentence below rests on the shadow. Three of the scenes in this paragraph were described from the wiki and not
watched (`security-breach-pq2-old-man-fnaf1-office`, `security-breach-pq3-help-wanted-hub-trapped-door`,
`hw2-faz-force-ending`). So: **one ending of Help Wanted 2 shows it crushed, and RUIN, most likely the later of
the two, still has a rabbit's shadow in the mask** (low-medium that the remedy works). [Round 4d: this read "the
next game still has a rabbit in the mask", which gave Help Wanted 2's place before RUIN as fixed] The chapters
do not say that it "works", or that it works "where fire did not": the fire was aimed at haunted machines and
this at a presence in code, and neither is shown to have finished its target. Who made Princess Quest, and who
the Princess and the Old Man are, no game says. It is not `canon` that Vanny killed the rabbit. [CHANGE:
`relations.ts`, `timeline.ts`]

**Small item.** A therapist asks Vanessa "Your dad's name was Bill?", and her reply is indistinct
(`security-breach-cd-vanessa-father-bill`). The archive has a William. Nothing joins them. Open questions only;
low. The people layer displays him by a descriptor, "Vanessa's father", with the name as a marked alias.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** Two women: Vanessa, and a second woman in the costume who looks
like her. It is the plain reading of the rooftop's two pages, and the cast list fits it: a credit names a
voiced part and not a person, so the list shows two voices and does not say two women (round 4d: this read
"the plain reading... of the cast list", which said more than the record and more than this ruling's own
wording line). It has those two records to itself. It loses on the recordings, in which the named Vanessa secretly makes a costume for a
watcher, and on the name, which the game has Freddy call no coincidence; and it needs a second blonde woman
whom no record introduces. The first version called the margin narrow while rating (1) medium-high. With the
credits recorded the alternative has a case of its own, and (1) is medium.

**Would change if.** A game showed two women together outside the rooftop page, or named the caller; a later
game showed Vanessa after that night with the mask off. (1) would rise if a game said outright that the two
are one, or if the credits roll itself, once captured, read otherwise than the wiki's transcription. It would
fall further on any third record for two women.

**Depends on.** R18, W10. **Carries.** R22, R33.

Review: the third review overturned nothing and asked for eight changes; seven are made in full. The rooftop
page is no longer discounted as a branch. "Later games follow the Princess Quest ending" is split into a
building that follows the underground route (medium) and a release that is only the model's reading
(low-medium). The remedy falls to low-medium and "works where fire did not" is withdrawn here, in R28 and in
the thesis. The teasers leave the records for (1), the list of vessels marks its break, and the slips about the
mask, the "first game" and "Let me out" are corrected. One part is not adopted as written: the cast credits are
not entered as a counter-record, because the archive has no such record and the model does not rule on a
report. It is listed as a record to add, with the place it would take. And one slip was not a slip: "RUIN's
mask hides Cassie" is in a record (`ruin-mask-bot-helpi-vanni`), on the wiki's account, and is now cited with
that caveat and with the record that points the other way.

Review: in round 4c the cast credits became a record (`security-breach-credits-vanessa-vanny-two-parts`). They
are entered against (1) at support weight, with the reason casting counts as it does in R1 and R30. With them,
and with the Redemption page re-read from its record, (1) falls from medium-high to medium. Nothing else in
this ruling changes.

### R20. The burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex, and the mass of parts

**Ruling.**

1. **The buried pizzeria is Pizzeria Simulator's building.**
2. **The burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex is a relic of that fire**: what was left of the rabbit the earlier
   game credits as William Afton, standing on a bare metal frame in a recharge station. It is not the
   endoskeleton sealed below. Who put the remains on a frame and in a station is not shown.
3. **What moves it.** The game shows the rabbit itself taking control of a machine. That something else moves
   the rabbit, a presence in the network using it as a body, is a possibility raised by a transcript; the model
   notes it and does not hold it.
4. **The mass of parts is what the fire left of Molten Freddy**, grown with other wreckage. What moves it is not
   said, and the model does not read it as the five (R30).

The games name neither figure. [CHANGE: from "two identities fit, and the games settle neither" to three readings,
one preferred; the "human jaw" argument goes]

**Confidence.** (1) medium. (2) not the sealed endoskeleton: medium; the body is that fire's rabbit: low-medium.
(3) that the rabbit acts that night, taking control of Freddy: stated by Freddy and shown; that something in
the network moves it: low.
(4) low-medium.

**Records for.**
- (1) The sign "Freddy Fazbear's Pizza Place" (`security-breach-underground-pizza-place`), the name on Pizzeria
  Simulator's condemned building (`ffps-completion-slide-pizza-place-condemned`) and Help Wanted 2's name for that
  game's office (`hw2-first-aid-simulated-pizza-place`); rooms of burners, "like the inside of an oven"
  (`ruin-burners-inside-of-an-oven`), under a game that ends with a labyrinth set alight (`ffps-completion-labyrinth`,
  `ffps-completion-fire`); the site found by breaking into "an old elevator shaft" beneath a sinkhole
  (`security-breach-msg-old-elevator`, `security-breach-msg-sinkhole`, `security-breach-msg-power-drain`). The game
  "does not narrate its history", and the two buildings' floor plans have not been compared: a check to make.
- (2) Pieces of a rabbit shell on bare metal legs and forearm, purple eyes, in a recharge station
  (`security-breach-burntrap-appearance`). The endoskeleton, by contrast, is behind poured concrete on a lower
  level, in a room of Murray's costumes, held by nodes spread through the building (`ruin-concrete-poured-over-door`,
  `ruin-mimic-lair-mcm-mascot-suits`, `ruin-fake-gregory-one-node-left-sealed`), and has orange coil eyes
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-mimic-ruin-appearance-link`, `implied`).
- (3) `security-breach-freddy-i-am-not-me`, `security-breach-freddy-take-control`: "He is trying to take control of
  me." Freddy calls it "he"; trying to take control of Freddy is the one thing the scenes on file show it doing
  (the record's words; an earlier wording here had "taking machines over" as its "one power").
- (4) One clear face, a white bear with pink cheeks and a small top hat, on a mass of wires and animatronic parts
  (`security-breach-tangle-composition`); Molten Freddy is that face on a mound of wires
  (`ffps-molten-freddy-alley-wires-eyes`).

**How long the endoskeleton has been sealed.** "Old" is not in the record: Cassie says only "it looks like
someone poured concrete over the door". The age rests on three things, none strong alone. A boy who says in the
same breath "I really don't know what it is" says it has "been trapped down here for a really long time"
(`ruin-gregory-mxes-explanation`). A construction crew at the raceway found the old shaft, wrote "Someone must have known about this, right?" and
traced "something down there pulling power" (`security-breach-msg-old-elevator`,
`security-breach-msg-power-drain`). That crew came after the raceway had been open to guests: it "was closed
very abruptly prior to the new construction" (`security-breach-freddy-raceway-closed-abruptly`). So the
messages are from later building work, before the Security Breach night, and not from when the Pizzaplex was
first built. And the guard's nodes are parts of the
working Pizzaplex, the last of them one of its animatronics (`ruin-roxy-final-node`; the claim is the lying
voice's, and the terminal then confirms that every node is off: `ruin-mxes-hub`), so the guard was wired into
the building by someone who knew what was below. Medium that the sealing is older than the Security Breach
night.

**Records against.**
- (2) No word in the game says whose body it is (`security-breach-burntrap-name-not-in-game`,
  `security-breach-gregory-what-is-that-thing`). Whether anything organic remains could not be verified, so "human
  jawbone" is a look, not a finding. The frame under the shell may be ordinary Pizzaplex stock, a casing "on a
  new Endo" (`security-breach-vanessa-new-endo`, `security-breach-freddy-where-i-was-born`), which would make it a
  shell put on a frame by someone and not a body that walked there. The one record of anyone going down there
  before the night is Freddy's "I have been here before. She brought me here... I cleared the path"
  (`security-breach-freddy-been-here-before`): a lead, and "she" is unnamed. The endoskeleton does wear costumes
  (`ruin-ending-scooper`), and more than one such machine may exist (R21), so a second endoskeleton in a rabbit
  shell cannot be excluded.
- (3) For a presence in the network: a "system wide" glitch in the robots (`security-breach-cd46-glitch-subroutines`),
  and Freddy, cut off from the main network, is the one band member not turned
  (`security-breach-freddy-cannot-connect-main-network`). But that same Freddy, whom no record shows reconnecting,
  is the one machine the burnt rabbit seizes underground: if he is still off the network, what takes him there
  does not come over it. The reading's other support is Scott (a transcript): the figure "was never supposed to
  move", a "decaying movie prop" with "a very specific purpose" he would not state
  (`scott-2024-interview-burntrap-never-meant-to-move`). No later game contradicts the base game on this point,
  so W2 gives no ground to set the scene aside.
- (4) The mass lies near the underground entrance, attacks, and in one ending drags the rabbit away
  (`security-breach-ending-burn-it-all-down`). If Molten Freddy held the five and the fire released them (R28,
  R30), something else moves the mass, or the fire did not empty it. The model says only that the games do not
  say.

**Two records this ruling no longer uses.** The costumed arm that comes out of a station at the end of Help
Wanted 2 (`hw2-faz-force-ending`) was "described from the wiki's ending summaries; the scene itself was not
watched"; the station unfolds from a box in the mask's picture of a room which, with the mask off, has no
station (`hw2-real-hub-pit-pizzeria`, `implied`); and the arm is costumed, where the body under the Pizzaplex is
bare metal with pieces of shell. It most likely belongs to the mask layer, so it shows nothing about the body
either way. It stays in R21 (6). And the "vintage Bonnie" email (`sd-vintage-bonnie-reports`) is not support for
a relic standing in its own ruin: a body that was abroad would have had to come back and be stood in a station
by someone. R33 says what the model makes of the email.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the burnt rabbit is Murray's endoskeleton in a costume. It
loses on the sealing: one machine cannot be loose in a recharge station on the pizzeria level and behind poured
concrete on a lower one, unless it was walled in between two games set close together, and then the builders'
messages and "a really long time" are wrong. This answers a body. The other form of the rival reading, a
copying program in the network, is R18's and R21's question, and is not answered by concrete.

**Labels.** The game never names either figure, in the dialogue, messages and cutscenes reviewed. That
"Burntrap" comes from the game's files and a patch post is a fan wiki's account
(`security-breach-burntrap-name-not-in-game`), so the first-use form no longer says what the files hold: it
is "the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex (known as Burntrap, a working name that Scott has said now stands)"
[round 4d: this read "(the game's files call it Burntrap, ...)"], and it is
never "the burnt rabbit" bare, because Pizzeria Simulator's salvaged rabbit is burnt too (R26). "Tangle" and
"the Blob" are a file name and a fan label; use "the mass of parts".

**Would change if.** A game said whose remains they are, who stood them there, or showed the endoskeleton
outside its concrete before RUIN.

**Depends on.** R1, R18, R19, R21, R30. **Carries.** The Pizzaplex chapter; R33.

Review: the third review overturned nothing and asked for six changes; all are made. (3) falls to low and is
reworded: its direction had come from a transcript, and the game's own record shows the rabbit acting. The Help
Wanted 2 arm and the "vintage Bonnie" email no longer support this ruling. "Old" is dropped from the concrete,
and the sealing's age is argued from the builders' messages and the nodes. Who stood the remains in the station
is marked not shown, with Freddy's line as the lead. (4) says the mass is not read as the five.

### R21. The copying endoskeleton across Secret of the Mimic, RUIN and Help Wanted 2; M.X.E.S.; its relation, if any, to the rabbit in the code and the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex

**Ruling.**

1. **Edwin Murray built two machines.** That much is stated, and so is what the first was for: he shows his son
   an endoskeleton, "This is M1... Think of it as your new babysitter", and his logs speak of a second, M2. The
   rest of (1) is the model's reading, join by join, and no record states any of it. That the voice which
   speaks as Fiona in his logs ("It is me. I'm Fiona.") is M1's: the logs name no speaker. That the cabinet
   F10-N4, which speaks as Fiona and says it "watched him grow up, through this machine", is M1's mind. That M2
   began as a copy of M1's program: an unsigned message to Edwin says "M2 began as a copy of my program". That
   M2 was built after the son's death, which the records imply and never state, and at that voice's urging,
   "try again. Build another": no record orders Edwin's logs. And that its violence is copied from his: a
   grieving father lost his temper with the machine that sounded like his dead son and damaged it, and "it
   mimics rage". Of the boy's mother the records say that she is "gone": that she is Fiona, and that "gone"
   means dead, is the reading the other recordings support, and the log that says it does not speak her name.
   [CHANGE: the current chapter merges the two machines and softens the cause] [Rewritten in round 4d, after
   the records were re-worded: "M1 took on the voice of Fiona, his son's dead mother", "M2 began as a copy of
   that program" and "at M1's urging" were told under "high"]
2. **The thing imitating Gregory in RUIN is a Murray endoskeleton held by Murray's kind of guard**, and most
   likely M2 itself.
3. **M.X.E.S. is Murray's technology.** A terminal in Murray's house in Secret of the Mimic is headed
   "Mechanical X-form Experimental Storage #1" (read from a screenshot). The initials spell M.X.E.S.; that the
   heading is what RUIN's M.X.E.S. stands for is the natural reading and not a statement, so "expanded on
   screen" is never said flat. RUIN's cabinet has the design of the first machine's housing. That one
   machine or one program runs on from 1979 to RUIN is not shown. In RUIN it is the guard. [CHANGE: a
   "subterminal of similar design" understates this]
4. **How the endoskeleton and its guard came to be under the Pizzaplex is not shown.**
5. **It is not the burnt rabbit's body** (R20). **Whether it is the source of Help Wanted's anomaly the games do
   not settle**; the model's preference is that it is not (R18).
6. **Help Wanted 2 joins the two threads on purpose and does not say how.** The model's reading: the machine that
   copies has taken up the rabbit's part, as it takes up Gregory's voice. On that reading, destroying the rabbit
   in the code does not end the threat.

**Confidence.** (1) two machines, and M1 a babysitter: high (stated). The joins are the model's reading, each
medium-high: the voice speaking as Fiona is M1's; the cabinet F10-N4 is M1's mind; M2 began as a copy of M1's
program; M2 came after the boy's death and the urging; its violence copies his. That the mother who is "gone"
is Fiona, and is dead: a strong inference. [ROUND 4D, MARKS MOVED: the first and third joins had stood under
"high", and Fiona's death was "firm" in step 2 of the order. No conclusion changes; the text marks each as the
model's reading] (2) a Murray endoskeleton
under Murray's kind of guard: high; the same individual as M2: medium-high. (3) Murray's technology: medium-high;
one continuous machine or program from 1979 to RUIN: not shown. (4) high that it is not shown. (5) not the
burnt rabbit's body: medium; not the source of the anomaly: low-medium. (6) medium that a bridge is intended;
low for the model's reading of it.

**Records for.**
- (1) The joins, step by step, with the record that carries each. M1 is stated: "This is M1... your new
  babysitter" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-training-m1`). A voice speaks as Fiona in the logs, and they name no
  speaker for it (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-m1-as-fiona`, `secret-of-the-mimic-log-try-again-build-another`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-refusal`, where Edwin answers it "I let a machine raise my kid. You're not real").
  A birthday film shows an endoskeleton behind the boy's cake while a voice that Edwin addresses as Fiona
  speaks (`secret-of-the-mimic-film-m1-birthday-cake`; that the endoskeleton is M1 is the wiki's
  identification). The cabinet labelled F10-N4, which "had impersonated Dispatch", speaks in Fiona's voice,
  says "our home" and wants M2 repaired (`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-reveal`); it says it "watched him grow up,
  through this machine" (`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-miss-my-child`); and an unsigned message calls M2 "a copy
  of my program" (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago`). No record says in as many words that the
  voice is M1's, that the cabinet is M1, or that the program copied was M1's: the wiki gives the voice's lines
  to "Fiona/M1", and the sound files' names are data and are not cited (W7). That Fiona is David's mother is
  the reading the recordings support, and that she and Edwin were married is likewise inferred: the records
  have "his mother" of David in a log that does not name her, "David, Mommy's gone...", "our home" and "Tell
  Edwin, I still love him" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-death`, `secret-of-the-mimic-film-mommy-gone`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-reveal`). David's death is implied by the cancelled party and by "It wouldn't be
  him" (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-party-canceled`, `secret-of-the-mimic-log-refusal`). No record orders
  Edwin's logs, so none is "the last" and none comes "later". The other records:
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-training-m1`, `secret-of-the-mimic-log-m1-as-fiona`, `secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-reveal`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-try-again-build-another`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago` ("M2 began as a
  copy of my program... It mimics rage"), `secret-of-the-mimic-log-incident` ("Stop sounding like him!"),
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-turn-around-lost-temper` ("I... lost my temper. I was always going to fix you"),
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-turn-around`. These are three records by two speakers; that the rage it mimics is the
  rage of that incident is the natural reading, and nobody states it. The smoother version, that it copied
  violence it had been exposed to, is the voice on the radio's (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-briefing-origin`);
  prefer Edwin's own words.
- (2) Shown: its room holds worn mascot costumes (`ruin-mimic-lair-mcm-mascot-suits`); that they are costumes
  of Murray's Costume Manor is the wiki's comparison with the 1979 game's, not a statement of RUIN's; the guard's cabinet
  carries the initials that head Murray's terminal (`ruin-mxes-hub`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mxes-acronym-on-screen`);
  the code 4625 (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-frequency-4625`, `hw2-mimic-teaser-mxes-ending`); `ruin-mimic-reveal`.
  Behaviour: it reuses a child's recorded lines (`ruin-mimic-reuses-gregory-lines`) as M2 reused Edwin's
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-turn-around`). The likeness of the body (`secret-of-the-mimic-mimic-ruin-appearance-link`)
  is `implied` and rests on the developers' naming, so it is support and not the lead. Scott calls RUIN's
  creature "the Mimic" (`scott-2024-interview-mimic-not-the-last` (a transcript)).
- (3) In 1979 the name is on storage terminals: "Mechanical X-form Experimental Storage #1", which offers the
  Cradle program (`secret-of-the-mimic-mxes-acronym-on-screen`), and "Experimental Storage #2", which holds the
  blueprints (`secret-of-the-mimic-experimental-storage-2-mimic-blueprints`). The cabinet whose design matches
  RUIN's is the first machine's housing, with a different nameplate (`secret-of-the-mimic-mxes-machine`). A
  computer that "can keep David safe and provide boundaries" (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-hurt-cradle`) stands
  against "the M.X.E.S. security program was designed to keep it hidden" (`ruin-gregory-mxes-explanation`). The
  rabbit-shaped figure comes out of the cabinet and is pulled back, and the false Gregory tries to jam it
  (`ruin-mxes-hub`, `ruin-the-entity`, `ruin-fake-gregory-signal-jammer`). Candy Cadet tells of a mother, a monster
  locked below and a learned lullaby (`ruin-candy-cadet-story`). Scott, from captions that render the name
  "mexus", calls M.X.E.S. "a character" whose name he chose himself
  (`scott-2024-interview-mxes-name-thought-through`): his word, and nothing here rests on it.
- (4) What the records allow. Fazbear claimed title to Murray's work
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contract-legally-ours`). Of the 1979 endings, one has the machine walk away from
  the burning complex (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-unfinished-business`); one has it hide in the van, attack the
  technician and leave him on the workshop's stage, which leaves the machine at the Costume Manor
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-clock-out`); one puts it to sleep (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-story-time`). The
  record that shows transport by van is Help Wanted 2's teaser, where a machine with a keypad and an "M.X.E.S."
  nameplate sits in a pale blue van with a white stripe, which drives away (`hw2-mimic-teaser-mxes-ending`;
  the wiki's text calls the van white). Someone poured concrete over its door
  (`ruin-concrete-poured-over-door`) and wired a guard through the building above
  (`ruin-fake-gregory-one-node-left-sealed`). One further possibility, low: it never moved. The 1979 site has
  "something below us", a dead elevator and power surges in tunnels, and the party signing W.A. holds the land
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-something-below`, `implied`; `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-old-elevator-below`;
  `secret-of-the-mimic-log-power-surges-tunnels`; `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-confidential-wa`); the Pizzaplex's
  builders find "an old elevator shaft" with something below "pulling power" (`security-breach-msg-old-elevator`).
  No game says the two sites are one.
- (6) The same long costumed arm that holds out the six "memories" holds out the White Tiger doll on code 1979
  (`hw2-memory-dolls`, `hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger`, `hw2-mystic-hippo-gift-distant-past`), and the White Tiger
  is the Murray boy's (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-story-time`); the main ending puts a costumed arm in a recharge
  station and cuts to RUIN's opening (`hw2-faz-force-ending`); the first machine "can fit into any costume we've
  created" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-training-m1`); its mind is a program that has already been copied between
  bodies (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago`); a clip plays near the thing in the code that a fan wiki
  identifies as the tape speaker's greeting, pitched down (`hw-glitchtrap-uses-tape-girl-voice`); both put their victim on a stage inside a suit
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-clock-out`, `hw-pizza-party-ending`).

**Records against.**
- (2) More than one such machine may exist. Fazbear's paper asks for the blueprints, though Edwin says "Every
  copy has failed" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-make-a-copy`). The reviewer reports, from a wiki stub and unseen, a
  swarm of small orange-eyed scrap machines in the route where the machine walks away; the archive has no
  record of them, and one is to be added from play (section 6). No game states the history between 1979 and the
  Pizzaplex.
- (3) That RUIN's cabinet is one and the same physical machine as the 1979 one is the wiki's inference from the
  matching design. And the one time the Cradle program runs, it sets the complex on fire
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-ending-unfinished-business`); it hides nothing.
- (5) "Sealed" is not "cut off". Two of the lines it speaks, "I-I'm Gregory." and "The stupid door won't
  open.", are lines Gregory speaks in Security Breach (`ruin-mimic-reuses-gregory-lines`, from a fan wiki's
  list of his audio), which the model reads as the machine behind the concrete having had
  audio from the building above, that night or later. In RUIN a message in the boy's voice brings a child into
  the building (`ruin-cassie-seeks-gregory`; the record does not say what carried the message or where it
  found her), and the voice claims to have rewritten the mask's assistant (`ruin-fake-gregory-signal-jammer`).
  And inside the Pizzaplex someone's talk-back programs came to be answered by "someone else"
  (`security-breach-cd46-talking-programs`); the recording does not say who. So
  the sealing covers the body, and only the body. What stands against the anomaly being Murray's program is
  R18's three answers: no record puts Murray's software on a Fazbear board; Help Wanted's account names nothing of
  Murray's, only old hardware the client sent; nothing in Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear or Special Delivery carries a Murray mark.
- (6) The dolls the arm holds out wear the masks of the four, the yellow bear and the Puppet, and the game
  calls each "a memory" (`hw2-memory-dolls`); one is won from a Springtrap cutout in a fire
  (`hw2-fazerblast-fire-springtrap`). The model reads them as the six children's, which would make them the
  killer's mementos and not Murray's. And the secret ending destroys
  the rabbit figure and reports "System threats found. Repair complete." (`hw2-pq4-consequences-achievement`), as
  though the rabbit were the whole threat.

**What stays open, and must be said to be open.** Why the guard appears as a rabbit; whether M1's "Fiona" is still
the guard in RUIN (low-medium); how the rabbit in the code and the rabbit-shaped guard relate; which of Secret of
the Mimic's three endings and RUIN's three endings stand. The model does not identify RUIN's rabbit-shaped figure
with Help Wanted's.

**Two things worth a sentence each.**
- *The same trick twice.* In 1979 a machine speaks as Dispatch on the technician's radio (the cabinet "had
  impersonated Dispatch": `secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-reveal`), and in one ending a Dispatch voice, most likely
  the company's own, says "We've been trying to get ahold of you all night"
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-clock-out-real-dispatch`); in RUIN the trusted voice is a machine and a second Gregory
  voice says he has been trying to reach Cassie all night (`ruin-real-gregory-not-at-pizzaplex`). In 1979 the
  impersonator is the cabinet F10-N4, which the model reads as M1's mind, and not M2. High for the two scenes;
  where the machine's voice takes over in 1979 is not stated (R2's rule).
- *The product.* The copying machines were Murray's own, made for his family, and the client was never meant to
  have them. A voice speaking as Fiona, which the model reads as the first machine's, asks him "you can't let
  them know about me" (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-m1-as-fiona`); of the second he says "There's a leak. Someone told them about M2. Well, they can't have it"
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-make-a-copy`). What the contract was for, the company calls only "some tech"
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contractor-murray`). So one endoskeleton that becomes whatever shell it is given
  is what Fazbear came to want once it learned of M2, not what it was paying for; the sentence that describes
  such a product is the voice on the radio's (R2). The line to the Pizzaplex's stock starts from Edwin's own
  words about the first machine, "It can fit into any costume we've created"
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-training-m1`), and from Hen's change order away from worn suits
  (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-build-to-spec`). It ends at casings on interchangeable endoskeletons
  (`security-breach-vanessa-new-endo`, `security-breach-freddy-where-i-was-born`, `hw2-endo-warehouse`,
  `sd-cpu-whole-new-personality`). Medium: both ends are stated, the line between them is inferred. In 1979 there
  are already bodies in mascot costumes in that building (`secret-of-the-mimic-previous-technicians`): the series'
  central image, done by a machine. By the account of the voice in the building, Fazbear had sent men before
  this one and they had not come back; that the company knew what had become of them is inferred. Do not claim
  the killer copied the machine or the machine the killer: no record orders them.

**Strongest alternative, and why it does not win.** One thing throughout: the copier is the rabbit in the code
and the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex. It would make the modern era a single villain. As a body it loses on
the sealing (R20). As a program it is not answered by the sealing; R18 gives the three answers it does have,
and they make a margin and not a refutation. An earlier version of this paragraph gave, as a reason for care,
what material outside the games asserts. That sentence is withdrawn: material outside the games is no reason to
accept a reading and no reason to resist one.

**Would change if.** A game showed the endoskeleton's history between 1979 and the Pizzaplex, connected it or
Murray's software to the VR studio's boards, or showed a second such machine at the Pizzaplex.

**Depends on.** R2, R18, R20. **Carries.** The last chapters; the thesis's closing image.

Review: the third review overturned nothing and asked for ten changes; all are made. The second half of (5)
falls to low-medium, because the sealing answers a body and the rival reading is a program, and because the
machine behind the concrete is shown to have had the building's audio. (3) is narrowed to "Murray's technology".
(1) separates the two machines (high) from the reading of the second's violence (medium-high). (2) leads with
shown records. "The product" no longer says Fazbear was paying for the copying machine, the Clock Out ending is
described correctly, "with Fazbear's knowledge" is marked inferred, the "something below us" possibility and
the reported scrap machines are listed, and the sentence that cited material outside the games is deleted.

### R22. Gregory, Cassie, the Help Wanted 2 player

**Ruling.**

| Question | Ruling | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| Gregory is a human boy | Yes | high |
| Gregory is therapy client 46 | Possibly; the model notes it and does not rely on it | medium-low |
| Gregory has "no record anywhere" | No: Freddy's scan finds no guest profile for him, and Vanessa says the company has no record of him | stated [CHANGE in wording] |
| Help Wanted 2's player is a Fazbear technician working in the wrecked Pizzaplex between Security Breach and RUIN | Yes | medium-high. That the room is the buried pizzeria under it: medium |
| That technician's mask is the one Cassie is handed | Yes | medium-high |
| The technician is Cassie's father | Flagged hypothesis | low |
| The technician's fate | Not shown | high that it is unknown |
| Help Wanted 2's endings | Branches; only the main one is tied to RUIN on screen | medium |

**Records for.** Gregory: the scan comes first, "Your guest profile is unknown to me"
(`security-breach-intro-not-in-database`); "there's no record of him" is Vanessa's word
(`security-breach-vanessa-no-record`), and the model holds her to be under something's control that night, so it
is the weaker of the two; `ruin-missing-gregory-poster`, `ruin-cassie-seeks-gregory`,
`ruin-real-gregory-not-at-pizzaplex`, `ruin-gregory-comics-depict-endings`,
`security-breach-gregory-more-disappearances`. Client 46: `security-breach-cd46-treated-as-child-phenom`,
`security-breach-cd46-talking-programs`, `security-breach-cd46-hacking-pizzaplex`, `security-breach-cd46-rabbit-ears`,
`security-breach-cd46-doing-the-manipulating`, `security-breach-cd46-glitch-subroutines`. The technician:
`hw2-glam-freddy-fazbear-technician`, `hw2-remove-mask-real-world`, `hw2-roxy-repair-mask`,
`hw2-roxy-he-is-still-out-there`, `hw2-hub-helpi-pushes-gift`, `hw2-faz-force-ending`, `hw2-faz-force-achievement`,
`ruin-security-mask-left-by-faz-technician`, `ruin-mask-bot-helpi-vanni`. That the room with the mask off is the
buried pizzeria is the wiki's identification from its layout, and the record is `implied`
(`hw2-real-hub-pit-pizzeria`). Cassie's father: `ruin-cassie-dad-faz-wrench`,
`ruin-item-bonnie-replaced-dad-wouldnt-say`, and two lines of patter (`hw2-carnie-you-got-kids`,
`hw2-mystic-hippo-key-darkness`): a wrench, a refusal to explain and a barker's line, which is why it is low.

**Records against.** Client 46 is never named and never speaks; a counsellor holds a file on the client while the
Pizzaplex has no record of Gregory; the client's story of tragic parents is called false, while one ending leaves
Gregory asleep in a box (`security-breach-ending-alley`). `hw2-achievement-ggy` reads like a tag for Gregory and is
explained by no game: noted, not used. For the technician: "you are a Fazbear technician - are you not?" is a
question inside a training level, and the only line the archive has found that speaks of the player's job
(`hw2-glam-freddy-fazbear-technician`).

**Whoever client 46 is**, one recording matters. Someone at the Pizzaplex "developed software programs that talk
to you and repeat phrases", and logs that at first looked like more of the same came to show "someone else
responding" (`security-breach-cd46-talking-programs`). That is a talk-back program being answered by something
else, inside the Pizzaplex, and R18 and R21 cite it without any condition. **If the client is Gregory**, the
recordings say more than the theory uses: a child who is "the one doing the manipulating" and who is the traced
source of the glitch in the robots. That would make him the first person at the Pizzaplex whom the thing in the
network used. State that part as a conditional.

**The gift.** In Help Wanted 2's main ending the technician is urged by the mask's cartoon assistant to open a
gift, and a recharge station comes out of it (`hw2-hub-helpi-pushes-gift`, `hw2-faz-force-ending`). The station
is not somewhere the technician is led to. The ending is held as described: its record was written from a fan
wiki's summaries and the scene was not watched. By those summaries the view then cuts to a S.T.A.F.F. bot that
holds a rabbit mask out to a girl. That the girl is Cassie, and the mask the one RUIN's Mask Bot gives her, is
the wiki's identification and the model's "most likely" (medium-high, the grade in the table): RUIN's own text
calls the Security Mask "A strange mask left behind by some Faz-Technician"
(`ruin-security-mask-left-by-faz-technician`). So "a rabbit mask is held out to a girl (as described)" is
what may be told flat, and "the mask passes to Cassie" takes "most likely". [Added in round 4d]

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That Gregory is not human. No game supports it; the idea comes from
outside the games.

**Would change if.** A game named client 46 or the technician.

**Depends on.** R19. **Carries.** The hand-off from Security Breach to RUIN.

Review: the third review held this ruling and made five small points; all are taken. The scan leads the
records for Gregory. "Under the wrecked Pizzaplex" is split into the building (medium-high) and the buried room
(medium). The talk-back programs are cited without the conditional. The father hypothesis falls to low. Step
27 of the order now says that the station comes out of a gift.

### R23. Into the Pit: literal event, or a story within the world?

**Ruling.** Neither. It is **an adaptation that the main games never place**. Its events are not on the timeline,
its year dates nothing, and no ruling rests on it. Where its background agrees with the model, a chapter may
show the agreement and must call it that. [Kept in substance. The label changes after review: it was "a ghost
story told inside the same setting", and nobody in the games tells it. One fact is corrected: a logo graphic
in the game reads 1985]

**Confidence.** Medium for the standing, which is the model's convention under W11. Low for any use of 1985 as
the year of the five.

**What sets it apart.** Three things, and they apply to nothing else in the archive.

1. Its plot is a book story's, and the source policy excludes the book. Scott's own account, in a transcript,
   is of a small project with another studio that grew into a full game, after an interviewer describes it as
   based on a story from the series he had announced as "some connected directly to the games, and some not"
   (`scott-2024-interview-into-the-pit-began-as-novelty` (a transcript), `scott-2018-frights-some-connected-to-games`).
   So the model cannot tell which of its content belongs to the games' story and which is carried over from a
   source it does not admit.
2. No main-line game refers to its events.
3. Scott has not placed it: nothing he has written says where, or whether, its events sit among the main games'.

Under W11's test, in order: there is no written ruling; the work says nothing about its own status (the analyst
found no frame that marks it as a game or a tale inside the world); and no main-line game takes it up. That is
"unplaced", which is not "false" and not "a tale".

**Reasons this ruling used and no longer uses.** "A time-travelling ball pit and a creature that replaces a
father" tests how fantastical the plot is, and by that test UCN's endless night, Princess Quest and FNaF3's
minigames would be tales too. "Six bodies against the newspapers' five" was wrong on its face: the model itself
gives the killer six victims, and the mismatch is where and when the sixth dies, which is a conflict with R7
and R8 and not with a newspaper. "Other self-contained tales inside the same game" proves too much: Pizzeria
Simulator and UCN hold tales too. "A novelty that grew" is a remark about how the game was made.

**What agrees, and is shown only as agreeing.** A Freddy Fazbear's with the four mascots; a caller who repeats
the first game's rule about being stuffed into a suit (`into-the-pit-phone-guy-night3-stuffing`,
`fnaf1-call1-stuffed-into-suit`); a yellow rabbit and children dead in a party room (`into-the-pit-prologue-mci`);
a technician's sketchbook with a smell inside Chica and an order to delay looking
