(`into-the-pit-sketchbook-chica-smell`, `into-the-pit-sketchbook-tech-2-missing`), against the first game's clipping
(`fnaf1-news-sanitation`). The empty yellow bear suit the boy hides in, named "the Golden Freddy Suit" by an
achievement (`into-the-pit-golden-freddy-suit-hide`), puts a yellow bear costume in a Freddy's where the killer
wears a yellow rabbit: it agrees with R11's two suits, and corroborates nothing.

**What does not agree.** Dead children in one party room, six by a fan wiki's count, and a sixth party hat
(`into-the-pit-prologue-mci`, `into-the-pit-collect-the-hats-purple-guy`), where the model has five at the
restaurant and a sixth child killed outside one (R7, R8). A rabbit that speaks from after its own ordeal while
standing in the past (`into-the-pit-yellow-rabbit-suffer-like-i-did`). A Funtime Freddy in a 1980s pizzeria, in
one of three hidden minigames that tell other stories (`into-the-pit-secret-minigames-other-stories`). A ball pit
that leads into the past and a creature that takes a father's place (`into-the-pit-jeffs-pizza-ball-pit`,
`into-the-pit-rabbit-replaces-dad`). None of these is a reason for the standing; they are what a chapter would
have to explain if a later game placed it.

**The year.** A logo graphic reads "FREDDY FAZBEAR'S Pizza 1985" (`into-the-pit-past-year-1985`). Its status is
"texture, placement unconfirmed" (W7): the record says the exact screen was not confirmed, shows an unused
variant that reads 1983, and notes that the year "reads like a founding date under a logo". A founding date
under a logo would not date a scene even inside that game. Scott's one written statement about licensed work
and dates cuts both ways (`scott-2024-no-new-lore-on-products`, posted three months before the game): he says he
stops partners inventing lore, a founding date on a product included. Either a 1985 that reached the game is
one he let through, which would make it worth more than a partner's guess; or it is the kind of date he says a
product must not assert, and it tells nothing. The model does not choose. It writes "1985 on a logo graphic in
Into the Pit (placement unconfirmed)", never "printed", and nowhere as the date of the five. The year does fall
inside the model's bracket for the five (1980 to 1986: R24).

**Other items.** The "Strange Photo" of "two men inside of a strange office" names no one
(`into-the-pit-strange-photo`).

**Strongest alternatives, and why they do not win.** *A literal 1985 event in the main story.* Nothing places
it there, for the three reasons above; its content does not refute it. *Wholly outside, like the Halloween
Edition.* Scott ruled that edition out in writing; he has ruled nothing here, and the game shares the setting
and is sold under the series' name.

**Would change if.** A main-line game referred to its events, or Scott placed it in writing.

**Depends on.** W11; the source policy. **Carries.** One "agrees" row in the dating table.

Review: the third review kept the conclusion and changed the label and three of the four reasons; all five of
its points are taken. The standing is "an adaptation that the main games never place", outside W6, resting on
three reasons that would not demote anything else the model uses. The year is no longer "printed". The yellow
bear suit "agrees with R11" and no longer corroborates "at story weight". Scott's written statement on licensed
products is added and shown cutting both ways.

### R24. Dating the whole story: which years are printed, which are derived, and the compressed against the extended reading

**Ruling.** The table is section 3. The rulings behind it:

1. **Printed in a game, seen on screen or heard:** 1983 (FNaF4's television); 11-12-1987 and 11-13-1987 (FNaF2's
   cheques); June 26th with no year, and November 12 and 13 with the year blanked (FNaF1); "'87" spoken. **On
   documents and textures read from wiki images, whose place in play nobody on the project has confirmed
   (W7):** 9/07/79 and 1979 on Secret of the Mimic's work order and form; 1965 to 1977 on Murray's chart, memos
   and order forms; 1985 on a logo graphic in Into the Pit. These are cited with that status and become
   "printed" when someone sees them in play. **On staged props inside company products (W5):** 1970 and "83'"
   on Fall Fest material; they agree with an unstaged record or they carry nothing. The keypad codes 1983 and
   1979 are codes that later text ties to the past; they are not printed dates. [CHANGE: adds 1979; the current
   theory says the game "dates nothing". Split into three kinds after the third review]
2. **Not printed:** Special Delivery's emails. Their dates are the real days the emails reached players. Treat
   them as out-of-universe delivery days; only their order is evidence. [CHANGE: the current theory lists them
   among the printed years]
3. **FNaF1's year is unknown, and not before November 1988.** Both bounds come from the cheques. FNaF1's Night 5
   cheque is dated 11-12; the 1987 restaurant was still trading on 11-12-1987; FNaF1 follows it; so the first 12
   November available is 1988's. The closing clipping, if it is FNaF1's own news (R12: medium), makes a later
   year more natural: "many years", "a long struggle", "a year-long search".
4. **"Twenty years."** Taken literally, with the four still unfinished prototypes in September 1979, the line
   would put FNaF1 at about 2000 or later. The model takes it as loose talk, which is how it is delivered, and
   prints no year from it. Loose or literal, it points away from 1988. [CHANGE]
5. **"Thirty years"** is Scott's figure, from store text. There are three closings it could count from: the old
   restaurant's before autumn 1987, the 1987 restaurant's, and FNaF1's. The model leans to the last.
6. **Compressed against extended: the model stops choosing.** The records lean away from the strict compressed
   convention (FNaF1 in 1988) and do not force the extended one. The model prints no year for FNaF1, FNaF3,
   Pizzeria Simulator or anything after; it prints intervals and order. Sort keys in `timeline.ts` are a
   convention and must be labelled as one. [CHANGE: the "c. 2017-2019 (estimated)" pill for FNaF3 goes] A bound
   is not a year for the event, but as a label it reads like one: FNaF1's label is "a November after 1987", and
   "not before November 1988" is written only where the arithmetic of point 3 is set out beside it. [Added in
   round 4b]
7. **The five: a bracket, not "the mid-1980s".** A June 26th between 1980 and 1986. The ceiling is R8's (before
   the 1987 restaurant: medium-high). The floor is soft: on the 1979 night the four are still unfinished
   prototypes in Murray's mock-up pizzeria, and his staff still speak of "the restaurant project". No record
   favours the middle of the bracket. "1985" is on a logo graphic in an adaptation the main games never place (R23); it falls
   inside the bracket and establishes nothing. [Added after review]
8. **Labels.** "Derived" is kept for arithmetic from printed dates. A date that depends on a reading (which
   building, which closing, which clipping) is "inferred"; a bracket is "estimated". Section 3.1 is relabelled
   to match. [Added after review]
9. **Pay dates nothing and orders nothing.** Each game states its own weekly rate: "$120 a week" in FNaF1's
   advertisement, with a cheque for $120.00 after the fifth night, and "$100.50 a week" in 1987, with a cheque
   for $100.50 (`fnaf1-help-wanted-ad`, `fnaf1-paycheck-night5`, `fnaf2-help-wanted-ad`, `fnaf2-paycheck-1987`).
   The sixth-night cheques are for $120.50 and $20.10 (`fnaf1-paycheck-night6`,
   `fnaf2-overtime-robots-scrapped`); the second is exactly a fifth of $100.50, so 1987's rate is $20.10 a
   night. No game states an hourly rate or a minimum wage, and only FNaF1's advertisement gives a shift, "12 am
   to 6 am". An hourly figure therefore needs an assumption about which hours are paid, and a year needs a
   wage table from outside the games, with the further assumption that the company paid the legal minimum.
   The archive's evidence is the games and Scott's words, so the model takes no date from the sums. It takes
   no order from them either: the order of the two games is fixed by other records (R12), and no record says
   that pay rose with time. [Added in round 4c: no ruling covered it]

**Confidence.** (1), (2) high. (3) high for both bounds; medium that the clipping is FNaF1's own. (4) medium that
the line is loose talk. (5) medium-low for which closing. (6) the lean is medium-low; that no year can be fixed
is high. (7) medium-high for the ceiling, low-medium for the floor. (9) high that the records carry no date and
no order in the pay. [Revised after review: "not before November 1988" was medium and was credited to the
clipping]

**Records for.** (1) `fnaf4-fredbear-and-friends-1983`, `fnaf2-paycheck-1987`, `fnaf2-overtime-robots-scrapped`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-1979`, `secret-of-the-mimic-credential-form-1979`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-net-earnings-1965-1975`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-memo-1974`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-mcm-order-forms-1973-1977`, `fnaf1-news-kids-vanish`, `fnaf1-paycheck-night5`,
`fnaf1-call1-bite-of-87`, `into-the-pit-past-year-1985`, `hw2-fall-fest-1970-poster`, `codb-fallfest-83-banner`,
`sl-private-room-keypad-1983`, `hw2-puppet-master-1983`, `hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger`,
`hw2-mystic-hippo-gift-distant-past`. (2) `sd-ness-blood-eagle-calendar`, `sd-which-board-unknown` (their `when`
fields say so). (3) `fnaf1-paycheck-night5`, `fnaf2-paycheck-1987`, `fnaf1-news-closing`. (4)
`fnaf1-call1-twenty-years` (the record's own note: "Offhand/hyperbolic phrasing"),
`secret-of-the-mimic-rnd-proto-pizzeria`; for how old the four are said to be in 1987, `fnaf2-news-originals-kept`,
`scott-fnaf2-steam-old-and-aging`, `fnaf2-call2-older-models`, `fnaf2-help-wanted-ad` ("Vintage pizzeria given new
life!"). (5) `scott-fnaf3-steam-description`, `scottgames-site-fnaf3-source-30-years-later-only-one`,
`fnaf3-coming-soon-newspaper` ("decades-old"), `fnaf3-it-burns-newspaper` ("an ancient pizzeria chain"). (7)
`secret-of-the-mimic-rnd-proto-pizzeria`, `secret-of-the-mimic-mockup-pizzeria-layout`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-we-need-to-go`.

**Records against.** For (3): the clipping may be the old restaurant's (R12), in which case nothing but the
cheques dates FNaF1. For (4): a count from the characters' earlier life as costumes could begin before 1979 (a
chicken costume was in use while Fiona Murray lived, `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-chicas-party-world`), but the line
is about machines that sing. For (7): the prototypes are at a contractor's; nothing says no restaurant had the
four before them. `fnaf2-call1-summer-job` and `scott-2014-fnaf1-store-summer-job` say "summer job" over cheques
dated November. Scott's FNaF1 store text is sales copy, and the game wins. FNaF2's is a line of the game itself,
spoken on Night 1: there the game contradicts its own cheques, and the model prefers the cheques because they are
the only dates the game prints (R8 lists the line against its June argument). For (1): the Secret of the Mimic
documents were read from wiki images of props.

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** Keep the compressed convention with invented years (FNaF1 c. 1988,
FNaF3 c. 2018), because a timeline with numbers is easier to read. It loses because it sits at the earliest edge
of what the cheques allow, because "twenty years" and the clipping both point later, because its far end was
propped on Special Delivery's delivery dates, and because the site's own rule is that a displayed date says how
it is supported.

**Would change if.** Any game printed a year after 1987; for (9), a game stated an hourly rate or a minimum
wage.

Review: group B's fourteen findings on this ruling and on section 3 are accepted, one of them in part. "Not
before November 1988" is high and comes from the cheques. "Twenty years" no longer contradicts its own "records
against": it is loose talk that points away from 1988, with the lean and not against it. "Thirty years" names
all three closings. The five get a printed bracket in place of "mid-1980s", which had no record but a story's
logo. "Derived" is kept for arithmetic. The part not taken: for FNaF4's week the reviewer proposed "c. 1983 to
1985"; the model prints "c. 1983" as an estimate and gives 1985 only as a cap that holds if the restaurant is
the diner of the 1987 call, because Scott's own page source and Sister Location's keypad both repeat 1983 and no
record points at a later year. The changes to the table itself are listed in section 3.

**Depends on.** R2, R8, R9, R12. **Carries.** Section 3; `dates.ts`.

| **Who the "she" is who brought Freddy underground; who wrecked Bonnie; where the Pizzaplex's staff went** | Open. Staff were removed (medium); by whom, unknown. Monty is the likeliest hand in Bonnie's end (medium-low) | as stated | `security-breach-freddy-been-here-before`, `security-breach-msg-all-staff-meeting`, `security-breach-msg-job-security`, `security-breach-msg-missing-bonnie`, `security-breach-msg-understudy`, `ruin-glamrock-bonnie-remains-hidden-room` |
| **The Afton children's mother** | No game shows, names or mentions her. The model says nothing about her, and identifies no machine with her | high that the games are silent | `fnaf4-no-parent-shown`, `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father` |
| **Left open by Scott on purpose** | Which FNaF3 ending happened; whose suit the player is in on Sister Location's Night 4; the two unnamed misconceptions; what the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex was for; what his Security Breach story was | high that they are open | `scott-2018-interview-fnaf3-ending-unanswered`, `scott-2018-interview-sl-suit-question-unanswered`, `scott-2017-sl-cleared-fnaf4-misconception`, `scott-2024-interview-burntrap-never-meant-to-move`, `scott-2024-interview-sb-story-not-as-intended` (all but the third are transcripts) |

**Strongest alternatives, and what would change each.**
- *Phone Guy.* That the tapes' voice is another employee with the company's stock greeting, or that the fourth
  recording simply breaks off and he lives. The first is not beaten, only out-weighed (medium). The second loses
  on the same voice and greeting in three games, on "I'll probably
  take the night shift myself" answered by "I actually worked in that office before you", and on the fifth message, which is
  no longer his (`fnaf1-call5-garbled`). Would change if a game placed him after FNaF1's week.
- *Candy Cadet.* That each story maps onto named people. It loses because the three stories give the one who
  joins the five three different natures (a woman, a man, a boy) and no game supplies a key. Would change if a
  game said whom a story is about.
- *"I was the first".* First victim of all, which would contradict R7; or first of something else. Not excluded:
  the line does not say first of what (`ucn-withered-chica-toysnhk`, notes). The model's reading is the one
  that agrees with R7 and with three weak pointers. Would change if a game ordered the five.
- *The gravestones.* That the hidden fifth stone or the hill stone belongs to the crying child, the Cassette Man
  or the volunteer. The image cannot exclude it. It loses on Help Wanted 2's six graves set against the four, the
  yellow bear and the Puppet (`hw2-pq4-graveyard-order`, `hw2-memory-dolls`). Would change if a game showed the
  fifth or sixth inscription.
- *Old Man Consequences.* R16.
- *The box.* That it still holds one definite thing Scott means to reveal. It loses on his own later statements.
  Would change if he opened it.

Review: group B's four findings are accepted. The old man's row says what is constant (the lake, the seat, and
in FNaF World a listener who is the player) and no longer credits FNaF World with "rest". "I was the first" has
three pointers, since the order of Help Wanted 2's graves is used for nothing (R7; changed in the first round).
"Sister Location was his substitute for opening the box" falls to low-medium. Phone Guy's row is split: the
caller and the earlier guard at medium-high, the tapes' voice at medium on an `implied` record, with the
counter-record listed.

**Depends on.** R7, R8, R16. **Carries.** The "open questions" chapter.

### R26. Labels: which names the theory may use

**Ruling.** Three tiers, one fixed table for the rabbits, and a note on the link token.

**Tier 1: names and words printed or spoken in a game. Use them; cite the spot the first time a chapter relies
on one.**

| Name | Where it is |
|---|---|
| William Afton; "Mr. Afton"; "Afton" | `ffps-credits-william-afton`; `sl-intro-mr-afton-board`; `ucn-roster-afton` |
| Michael | `sl-golden-freddy-cutscene-father` |
| Elizabeth | `ffps-completion-connection-terminated-elizabeth` (never spoken in Sister Location) |
| Gabriel, Fritz, Susie, Jeremy | `ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones` (as names on stones; pairing them with characters is R25) |
| Mike Schmidt; Jeremy Fitzgerald; Fritz Smith | `fnaf1-paycheck-night5`; `fnaf2-paycheck-1987`; `fnaf2-pink-slip-fritz-smith` |
| Vanessa; Vanny | Security Breach throughout; `security-breach-gregory-finds-vanny-hideout`, `security-breach-freddy-vanny-vanessa-bunny` ("Vanny" is Gregory's reading of what he found in the hideout, and the game then uses it) |
| Gregory, Cassie, Luis, Ness, Bill | their games' dialogue, emails and recordings |
| Edwin Murray; Fiona; David; "Hen"; "WA" and "W.A."; M1, M2 | Secret of the Mimic's logs and messages. "Edwin Murray" is spoken in full (`secret-of-the-mimic-log-meet-up`, `secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contractor-murray`). Fiona and David have first names only in the records' quotes, and the dispatcher says "Murray's son": "Fiona Murray" and "David Murray" are the archive's forms, used for clarity, and no record gives a marriage. Two names the records use in their claims are in no quote: "Arnold" for the technician (the work order is reported to print "Arnie") and "F10-N4" for the cabinet; use them, and add a record for each (section 6) |
| Golden Freddy; Springtrap; Spring Bonnie; Fredbear; Fredbear's Family Diner | `fnaf2-custom-night-names`; `fnaf3-springtrap-name`; `fnaf3-tape5-spring-bonnie-moved`; `fnaf4-fredbear-and-friends-1983`; `fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner` |
| The Puppet; the Marionette | "the puppet" in `fnaf2-call2-foxy-puppet`; Pizzeria Simulator's Security Puppet minigame (`ffps-security-puppet-assignment`); `ucn-roster-lefty-music-box` |
| Ennard, Molten Freddy, Scrap Baby, Lefty | `ucn-roster-two-forms-at-once`; `ffps-credits-returning-voices`; `ffps-lefte-blueprint` |
| The Cassette Man | Pizzeria Simulator's cast credits (`ffps-credits-cassette-man`): a role, the only name the games give him |
| Phone Guy; Old Man Consequences; Purple Guy | `ucn-roster-noncanon-and-fnaf-world`; `fw-old-man-consequences`; `fw-purpleguy-sprite-disclaimer` |
| M.X.E.S.; the Mimic; Remnant | `ruin-mxes-hub`, `ruin-gregory-mxes-explanation`; the title of Secret of the Mimic and `secret-of-the-mimic-mimic-blueprint-acronym`; `ffps-scup-blueprint` |
| "The one you should not have killed" | the game's own words (`ucn-nightmare-freddy-toysnhk`) and Scott's label (`scott-2019-ucn-child-face-jason`) |
| "The anomaly" | the tapes' and HandUnit's word for the rabbit in the code (`hw-tape-girl-calls-it-the-anomaly`) |
| "That rabbit thing" | RUIN's words for the figure from the cabinet (`ruin-fake-gregory-signal-jammer`) |

The last two are the games' own descriptors. They need no licence, and where a sentence reports what Help Wanted
or RUIN says, they are preferred to a coined one.

Three cautions inside tier 1. RUIN itself was not verified to say "the Mimic": calling RUIN's endoskeleton by that
name is the identification of R21 (medium-high), so introduce it as "the copying endoskeleton" and name it once
the chapter has argued the link. Within Secret of the Mimic, "the Mimic" is on the title and a blueprint, and
the game does not say which of the two machines it names; the model uses it for M2, and the first machine
copies too. And "Crying Child" is printed once in a game, on FNaF World's generic weeping child
(`fw-crying-child-character`), not on FNaF4's boy: "the crying child" stays this archive's lower-case descriptor.

**Tier 2: names Scott uses in his own words that no game prints or speaks. Allowed with the first-use form
below, once in each chapter, never as if a game said them, and never as evidence of what the thing is.**

| Label | Basis | First-use form |
|---|---|---|
| Glitchtrap | `scott-2024-interview-placeholder-names-canon`, `scott-2024-interview-glitchtrap-possession-of-ai` (each a transcript); no game prints it, as far as their transcripts show (`hw-tape-girl-calls-it-the-anomaly`) | "the rabbit in the code (known as Glitchtrap, Scott's working name for it)" |
| Burntrap | the same statement and `scott-2024-interview-burntrap-never-meant-to-move` (each a transcript); by a fan wiki's account the name comes from the game's files and a patch post, and it is not in the text and audio reviewed (`security-breach-burntrap-name-not-in-game`) | "the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex (known as Burntrap, a working name that Scott has said now stands)". Until round 4d this read "(the game's files call it Burntrap, ...)", which stated the wiki's account as fact |
| Scraptrap | `scott-ucn-character-guide-afton` (written): "William Afton (AKA Scraptrap)" | "the salvaged rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator, William Afton (Scott's guide adds 'AKA Scraptrap')"; usually just "William Afton" |

Each first-use form says whose label it is. Headings, captions and anything else a reader sees first, or a
search result shows, carry the descriptor; the label may follow in brackets. In running prose after the first
use, prefer the descriptor. Scott's rule ("once someone finds them in the source code... that's the canon name
now") rests on a machine transcript, so it licenses the label and nothing else. He also says both were casual
planning names built on "-trap", so the names are no evidence that either figure is the man in Springtrap.
The site's chapter on the rules says the same in its own words: a name in this tier is "given once in a
chapter, in a form that says whose label it is. Nothing is argued from it".

**"The burnt rabbit" is never used bare.** Two figures are burnt rabbits: the one salvaged in Pizzeria
Simulator and the one under the Pizzaplex. One descriptor shared by both would argue R20 (2), which is
low-medium. So: "the salvaged rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator" (or simply William Afton), and "the burnt rabbit
under the Pizzaplex", always with its place.

**Tier 3: labels that stay out as names.** Use the plain description. A tier-3 label may stand in one place
only: as an alias of its entity in `people.ts`, marked for what it is, "Phone Dude (a fan name)" or "Tangle (a
file name)", so that a reader who searches for the label finds the entry. It stands nowhere else: not as a
display name, a heading, a caption, a link label or a word of a chapter's prose. One exception, which is a
list and not a use: the chapter that sets out this policy (`canon-rules`) may print the tier-3 labels once, as
labels the archive does not use, each beside its descriptor. [Changed in round 4b, on the
owner's decision. The first version allowed one attributed mention in a chapter ("the files call it...");
that allowance is withdrawn, since the marked alias now does that work. It also said "none" for two aliases
in the table below, against section 5.2, which already had the old names kept as aliases "that say whose
label each is"]

| Label | Use instead |
|---|---|
| Shadow Freddy, Shadow Bonnie, RWQFSFASXC | the dark bear, the dark rabbit. Two records' notes now quote Scott's character guide, "RXQ aka Shadow Bonnie" (`fnaf2-shadow-bonnie-office`, `fnaf3-shadow-bonnie-minigame`); the archive still holds no record of that line itself, only the guide's line for Afton (`scott-ucn-character-guide-afton`). With a record of the line, "Shadow Bonnie" moves to tier 2 as Scott's word; until then it stays here, and the people layer's alias says where the name comes from |
| The Blob, Tangle | the mass of parts |
| The Entity, the M.X.E.S. Entity | the rabbit-shaped figure from the M.X.E.S. cabinet; RUIN's "that rabbit thing". "The M.X.E.S. Entity" is the wiki's name for the figure, and in the RUIN dialogue checked it is "that rabbit thing" (`hw2-mimic-teaser-mxes-ending`, `ruin-the-entity`); whether Helpi's own lines use the word is still not verified. If they do, lower-case "the entity" is the game's own and moves to tier 1 |
| Tape Girl | the tape speaker |
| Phone Dude | the attraction's caller (FNaF3) |
| "Vanny mask" | the rabbit mask; in RUIN, the Security Mask |
| MCI, "Missing Children Incident", "the Bite of '83", "Shattered" animatronics, "TOYSNHK", "Vengeful Spirit" | the five; "the incident" (the clipping's word); FNaF4's bite; the wrecked animatronics; "the one you should not have killed" |
| Any book-only name or term | the archive's descriptor; never the name |

Scott's rule is not extended to every string in a game's files. He spoke of two of his own working names; file
names he did not use (Tangle, the rabbit figure's "SpringBonnieMAN") are data, not text (W7).

**The rabbits: one fixed table.** Thirty chapters by several writers will not hold these apart by tiers alone.
Each figure has one short descriptor, used the same way everywhere.

| Figure | Fixed descriptor | Label, and its tier | What it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| The springlock suit | the Spring Bonnie suit | Spring Bonnie (1) | One of two such suits, with the yellow bear (R11) |
| What the killer wore for the five | the yellow rabbit costume | none | Most likely the Spring Bonnie suit: medium (R8) |
| The killer's body in that suit | Springtrap | Springtrap (1) | William Afton, dead in the suit and walking (R1) |
| The rabbit salvaged in Pizzeria Simulator | the salvaged rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator, or William Afton | credited as William Afton (1); "Scraptrap" (2) | The same being as Springtrap (R1, link b) |
| The dark rabbit of FNaF2 and FNaF3 | the dark rabbit | Shadow Bonnie, RWQFSFASXC (3) | Unexplained (R10) |
| The figure in Help Wanted's code | the rabbit in the code; the tapes' "the anomaly" | Glitchtrap (2) | Presents as the killer; what it is, not settled (R18) |
| The woman in the rabbit costume | the rabbit-costumed woman; Vanny | Vanny (1) | Vanessa: medium (R19) |
| The figure in the station under the Pizzaplex | the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex | Burntrap (2) | A relic of the fire: low-medium (R20) |
| The figure from the M.X.E.S. cabinet | the rabbit-shaped figure from the M.X.E.S. cabinet; RUIN's "that rabbit thing" | "the Entity" (3, unverified) | Part of the guard; not identified with the rabbit in the code (R21) |
| Outside the main line | the yellow rabbit of Into the Pit; Special Delivery's "vintage Bonnie" (the email's words) | none | R23; R18 and R33 |

Bonnie, the stage rabbit, and his later versions are named in their games and are not in question.

**The link token prints the stored name. [Carried out: the table below is now a record of what was changed.]**
Writers put `{{entity-id}}` on the first mention of anyone who has a page, and the renderer prints that
entity's `name` from `people.ts`. When this ruling was written the stored names were tier 2 and tier 3 labels,
so the token was barred for eight entities. The people layer has since been changed: each of these entities
now stores its descriptor as its name and keeps the label as a marked alias, so the token may be used for all
of them. One caution remains: a descriptor is stored in lower case, so a token that opens a sentence or a
table cell prints a small first letter; recast the sentence, or write the descriptor as plain text.

| Entity id | Printed before 3 October | Prints now | Aliases kept, each marked for what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| `glitchtrap` | Glitchtrap | the rabbit in the code | "the anomaly (the tapes' word)"; "Glitchtrap (Scott's working name)" |
| `burntrap` | Burntrap | the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex | "Burntrap (a file name, by a fan wiki's account, and Scott's working name)" |
| `scraptrap` | Scraptrap | the salvaged rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator | "William Afton (the credits)"; "Scraptrap (Scott's guide)"; "Afton (Ultimate Custom Night's roster)" |
| `the-mimic` | The Mimic (M2) | the copying endoskeleton | "M2", "the Mimic" and "that thing in the sinkhole", each with its source. The old name put R21 (2)'s medium-high identification into a name |
| `tape-girl` | Tape Girl | the tape speaker | "QA (the credits)"; "Tape Girl (a fan name)" |
| `phone-dude` | Phone Dude | the attraction's caller | "Phone Dude (a fan name)" |
| `shadow-freddy`, `shadow-bonnie` | Shadow Freddy, Shadow Bonnie | the dark bear, the dark rabbit | each fan name, marked; "RWQFSFASXC (a file name)" |
| `the-blob`, `the-entity` | the Blob, the Entity | the mass of parts; the rabbit-shaped figure from the M.X.E.S. cabinet | "the Blob (a fan name)", "Tangle (a file name)"; "that rabbit thing (RUIN's words)", "the Entity (the wiki's label...)" |
| `crying-child` | The Crying Child | the crying child | none |

An alias is never printed by the link token, in a heading or in a caption. A marked alias says whose label it
is and, where the source is a fan wiki, says that too ("a file name, by a fan wiki's account").

Descriptor entities now print in lower case ("the older brother", "the purple man", "the five children"), and
their `nameStatus` is "descriptor". Two entities that the model joins keep a record's own word as their name
and carry the join as a marked alias: `f10-n4` ("M1 ... which the model reads this cabinet to be") and
`fiona-murray` ("Fiona (the name the records quote)").

**Ids.** The entity id `henrys-daughter`, the group `emily`, the tag `toysnhk` and the record ids that
contain it carry a book-only name or a fan abbreviation. They are ids, not prose. The first has been renamed
`cassette-mans-daughter`; the group key and the tag are to be renamed when the branches are merged (`cassette`,
`not-have-killed`), and neither may be printed under its old form. Record ids are a different case, settled in
round 4d: they are stable handles that other work points at, so they are not renamed, and a few still carry a
fan abbreviation or an older reading (`...-toysnhk`, `security-breach-rooftop-vanessa-alive`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-log-death`, `secret-of-the-mimic-log-m1-as-fiona`,
`secret-of-the-mimic-clock-out-real-dispatch`, `hw-glitchtrap-uses-tape-girl-voice`). Where the interface
prints one, it prints it labelled "Record id". That label does the work that "(a fan name)" does for an alias:
it marks the string as the archive's handle and not as a name or a claim, so it is within this ruling. An id
is never a heading, a caption, a link label or a word of prose, and the record's title and claim, not its id,
say what the record carries. [The first version said "do not let the UI print them"]

**Checks to make before the chapters are written.** Whether Help Wanted 2's gallery prints "Vanny", "The Mimic"
or "Glitchtrap" beside its models (the analyst could not confirm it; `hw2-credits-vanny`,
`hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger`): if it does, that name is tier 1 by this ruling's own test, and the caution
about RUIN changes. What RUIN's Helpi calls the figure from the cabinet. And the sentence at 17:47 of the 2024
interview, by ear.

**Confidence.** High for tiers 1 and 3. Medium for tier 2, because its licence is one transcribed sentence. Of
everything in this ruling, tier 2 is the first thing a chapter should be ready to lose.

**Records against.** For tier 2: no game prints either name; the common brief lists both among labels not to be
used as if the games said them. The first-use formula is what keeps the ruling inside that brief.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *Use "Glitchtrap" and "Burntrap" as plain names throughout*, since every reader knows them and Scott calls
  placeholder names canon. It loses because the licence is a machine transcript, because no game prints either,
  and because both names assert, by their "-trap", an identity with the man in Springtrap that R18 holds at
  medium and R20 at low-medium. A name that argues a contested ruling should not be the default.
- *Keep both out altogether*, as the common brief's list of labels suggests. It loses because Scott's statement,
  weak as its source is, is his own account of his own names, and because a reader who knows only those names
  must be able to find the figures. The first-use form, once per chapter, serves both.

**Would change if.** A later game printed "Glitchtrap" or "Burntrap", or Help Wanted 2's gallery is found to
(they move to tier 1); or the 2024 interview were checked by ear and found to say something else.

**Depends on.** Nothing. **Carries.** Every chapter's wording.

Review: the third review kept the three tiers and asked for eight changes; all are made. The link token would
have printed "Glitchtrap", "Burntrap", "Tape Girl" and "The Mimic (M2)" at the very place this ruling asks for
a descriptor, so the display names to change are listed here and in section 5.2. "The burnt rabbit" no longer
names two figures. Headings and captions carry the descriptor. The Burntrap form says whose label it is. The
games' own words "the anomaly" and "that rabbit thing" are in tier 1. "The Entity" is marked unverified. The
rabbits have one table. Two checks in play are listed.

### R27. Scope: what carries story weight

**Ruling.** One rule per kind of material, and four tests that make them one rule: W5's three layers for a
company product, W10 for branches, W11 for the standing of a whole work, and a test for mechanics.

| Material | Standing | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| FNaF4 version 1.1 (including the House Map cheat) | Story | `scott-2015-fnaf4-halloween-canon-split`, `fnaf4-house-map-cheat` |
| FNaF4 Halloween Edition | Outside, non-canon | `scott-2015-fnaf4-halloween-canon-split` |
| Sister Location's Custom Night gameplay | Outside, non-canon | `scott-2016-sl-custom-night-not-canon` |
| Sister Location's cutscenes, Custom Night's included | Story | `scott-2016-cutscenes-are-canon` |
| Sister Location's Private Room night (the "fake ending" branch) | The night's events are a branch the Real Ending contradicts; the room, its desk and its wiring are part of the facility on either branch, and the breaker panel is in the main run | W10; `sl-private-room-keypad-1983` (notes), `sl-breaker-panel-obsv-systems` |
| UCN's gameplay, roster, office and challenges | Staging | no ruling from Scott; "mashup" (`scott-ucn-store-once-again-trapped`); non-canon characters on the roster (`ucn-roster-noncanon-and-fnaf-world`); two forms of one being at once (`ucn-roster-two-forms-at-once`) |
| UCN's voice lines and cutscenes | Text, read as addressed to one unnamed player | W11: it is Scott's own game in the main line, and its lines answer Pizzeria Simulator's last speech. "The majority of characters only say something if (when) they kill you" (`scott-2018-ucn-voice-lines-when-they-kill-you`); one invented character runs through the lines of seven performers |
| UCN lines spoken by Halloween Edition characters (Jack-O-Chica, Nightmarionne) | Text: the speaker is an effigy either way; the words are UCN's | `ucn-jack-o-chica-burns-eternal` (notes) |
| UCN's cartoons, Candy Cadet, Mr. Hippo, Pigpatch | Tales with a teller inside the story (W6) | R25 |
| FNaF World | R17 | W11: the game calls its own adventure imaginary |
| FNaF1's and FNaF2's Custom Night slips | Light: they name a third guard and give reasons; they cannot carry an identification | R13 |
| Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear and Help Wanted 2: the levels as built | Company staging (W5): not observations of events, and by Scott's own account a mix of canon and "questionably canon" characters | `scott-2019-help-wanted-is-canon`, `hw-intro-vr-game-to-move-past-ghost-stories` |
| Staged props in those levels: the banner "FALLFEST 83'", the 1970 Fall Fest poster, the child's hat, watch and shoe inside Freddy, Springtrap at the office window | "Agrees (staged)". Each may be shown agreeing with what an unstaged record already carries. None is the only support for a fact, and none is "printed" | W5; `codb-fallfest-83-banner`, `hw2-fall-fest-1970-poster`, `hw-pns-freddy-child-items`, `hw-pizza-party-springtrap-window` |
| The frame around those levels: the tapes, HandUnit's admissions, the hidden call, the posters and rooms seen with the mask off, the endings | Story | R18, R19, R22 |
| Intrusions in those levels: the rabbit figure wherever it appears, its part in Pizza Party and on the instruction monitors, the dancing figure on the hill, Princess Quest in three games, the rabbit mask in the Corn Maze cellar, the six dolls and the arm that offers them, the White Tiger doll on code 1979, the keypad codes | Story, attributed to whatever intrudes. Each passes W5's test: it is behind a secret, it glitches, it addresses the player, or it works against the level's instructions | W5; `hw-pizza-party-ending`, `hw2-glitchtrap-corrupts-backstage`, `codb-dancing-glitchtrap`, `codb-corn-maze-rabbit-mask`, `hw2-memory-dolls`, `hw2-mimic-teaser-1979-white-tiger`, `hw2-puppet-master-1983` |
| Curse of Dreadbear | The hidden call is frame and the mask is an intrusion: both are story and are core. The fairground banner beside FNaF4's house is a staged prop that agrees with that game's year. The dancing figure is an intrusion and belongs to the chapter on the rabbit in the code. Dreadbear's laboratory, the truck, the gravestone and the game-over lines are the level as built | `codb-corn-maze-rabbit-mask`, `codb-mask-plush-voice`, `codb-mask-plush-voice-opening`; `codb-fallfest-83-banner`, `codb-fnaf4-house-on-hill`, `codb-hallway-ends-in-fnaf4-bedroom` |
| Special Delivery | The staff emails are story; their order is evidence, their dates are not (R24). The service's mechanics show that a personality rides on a swappable board: a mechanic that a tutorial email explains. Encounter lines are the service's machines speaking the originals' lines. The "vintage Bonnie" is the stated exception: staff say it is in none of their databases (R18, R33). The Dark Circus is the product as built | `sd-cpu-whole-new-personality`, `sd-rebuilds-of-original-animatronics`, `sd-vintage-bonnie-reports` |
| Security Breach's base game | Story. Its endings are branches (W10). Where a later game contradicts it on a point, the later game wins on that point (W2), and the ruling that says so names the game and the point | W2, W10 |
| Into the Pit | An adaptation the main games never place. It may be shown to agree; nothing rests on it | W11; R23 |
| Scott's teasers and page source | Evidence when they carry in-world text (the hidden news report, the ejection dialogue, "Stay the course"); release dates, gameplay previews and jokes are outside | section 4 |
| Mechanics | A mechanic counts when a character, a document or a screen in the game explains or names it. A tuning value (difficulty levels, timings, costs) does not. A scripted hidden interaction with a result of its own is a scene and is weighed as one. A mechanic may always be cited for what kind of thing a game is | the source policy lists mechanics as evidence; Scott's "because- game" answered one question, about doors and power (`scott-2015-doors-power-because-game`), and is a caution, not a rule |
| Textures and documents read outside play: the work order and credential form in the van, Murray's chart, memos and order forms, the diner poster, the 1985 logo, the 1970 poster, the earthquake newspaper | "Texture, placement unconfirmed" (W7). Each may be cited as that. A date on one is not "printed" in the dating table until someone sees it in play | `secret-of-the-mimic-work-order-1979`, `secret-of-the-mimic-credential-form-1979`, `secret-of-the-mimic-fredbears-diner-poster`, `into-the-pit-past-year-1985`, `hw2-fall-fest-1970-poster`, `ruin-pizzaplex-abandoned-earthquake` |
| Cut, unused or data-only content | Not evidence (W7), and not mentioned as agreeing or disagreeing: Help Wanted 2's unused warning lines (`hw2-unused-vanny-dont-help-it-escape`), Special Delivery's undelivered emails, the data label `ffps-midnight-motorist-later-that-night`, the unverified `ffps-hry223-label`, FNaF World's unused open box (`fw-unused-open-box`), UCN's cut Pigpatch line | policy: "files reachable in-game" |
| Scott's endorsements of fan videos | They confirm only the points he names (Miketrap is wrong; purple is not literal; 1983 against 1987 is not a real conflict). The videos' content is not imported | `scott-2017-matpat-final-theory`, `scott-2017-matpat-answered-some-things`, `scott-2015-game-theory-fnaf2-almost-right` |
| Scott's statements about the books | He ruled the first novel a separate continuity. Of the later story series he wrote that it "takes place in the FNAF universe" with stories "some connected directly to the games", and then "look to the novels to fill in some of blanks to the past"; his 2024 "yes, that is correct" is ambiguous on purpose. These explain why some game facts look unexplained. They supply no explanation here | `scott-2015-books-separate-continuity`, `scott-2018-frights-some-connected-to-games`, `scott-2020-novels-fill-blanks`, `scott-2024-books-canonicity-yes` |
| Novels, the Survival Logbook, guidebooks, films, fan theory | Not evidence, and not used to fill a gap, even silently. This is the project's source policy. It is not a ruling of Scott's: his later words do not exclude the story series, and a reader who knows them should be told so. The owner considered admitting the Survival Logbook on 1 October 2026 and did not adopt it. Questions that fandom usually settles from such material (a name for the fifth child; whether the crying child and the fifth child share the yellow bear; Mike Schmidt as Michael; the Afton children's mother; who the two men of the restaurants were to each other) are ruled here on the games alone, and each ruling says what the games do not carry (R2, R6, R8, R13, R25) | `BRIEF-common.md`, source policy |

**Tags, cast lists, markers and marks (added in round 4d).** The site's front matter (`how-to-read` and the
chapter `canon-rules`) was written after these rulings. What follows is the rulings' side of it, so that the
two say the same; items 6, 11 and 12 of the last verification are decided here.

*Tags.* Four tags mark a record of a weaker kind, in these exact forms. "(a transcript)": Scott's 2018 and 2024
interviews, which the archive holds as machine transcripts of archived video. "(read from an image)": a
document or texture read outside play and not yet seen in play. "(as described)": a scene or a sound that the
archive holds as a description and has not captured or heard. "(a fan wiki's account)": a count, a comparison
or a detail taken from a fan wiki. A line of a game's text that was read from a fan wiki's transcript is not
tagged: most of the archive's quoted lines were read that way, and checked where they could be. The tag goes
where what is argued is the wiki's own work (its count, its comparison, its identification of a sound or an
image) or rests on the layout of what it transcribed.

*Cast lists.* Every cast list in the archive was read from a fan wiki's transcription of the credits, and none
from a capture of a credits roll. Sister Location's, Pizzeria Simulator's, Security Breach's, Help Wanted's and
Help Wanted 2's are of one kind, and are cited by one rule.
1. A part's name quoted from one line of a list is quoted text, cited like any transcribed line, with no tag:
   "William Afton", "Cassette Man", "QA".
2. Whatever is argued from the shape of a list is a comparison, and takes "(a fan wiki's account)" once, where
   it is argued: that two parts stand on two lines with two performers (R19); that two machines have the
   performers of two earlier machines (R30); that a list lacks a part, or that a character has no credited
   voice.
3. Casting counts at support weight wherever it is argued from, and never alone (R19, R30).
4. R1's link c is "stated" because Scott's written guide, verified at its source, states it, and the roster
   agrees. The credit line is where a game first prints the name, and (c) would stand without it. So the flat
   citations of `ffps-credits-william-afton` stand, and the two lists are not treated differently: the tag
   follows what is argued, and Security Breach's list is argued from its shape.
What is still wanted is a capture of each credits roll (`round4.md`).

*Markers.* The site prints three markers and one verb, and each goes with a kind of claim. "Most likely" marks
a short inference: one step from what is stated or shown. The rulings' "strong", "firm inference" and
medium-high take it, and so does a medium claim that is one such step ("his own home, his sons"). "The model
reads" marks the archive's own interpretation: a join that no record states, which the model tells (medium;
medium-high where the claim is a join of several records, as in R21 (1); and low-medium where the model tells
the reading, as in R15 and R19 (4)). "May be", given with its reason,
marks a possibility that the model holds loosely and does not tell (low, and low-medium where the model does
not tell the claim: the remains and the foul rabbit in R33). Where two readings are close the model "leans"
to one (R16 (2), R3's watching, R4's waking nights, R5's toy). So the choice between "the model reads" and
"may be" is not made by the figure alone: it turns on whether the claim is the reading the model tells. That
is the answer to item 6: the voice that refuses release is the accuser's on the model's reading (R16 (3), part
(b): medium at its lower end), so "the model reads it as his" is the right marker; "may be" would be right
only for a claim the model holds at low and does not tell.

*Marks.* The site's rule is: "A chapter carries the mark of its main claim, and a further step in its answer
carries its own marker in the sentence. A beat is all telling, and carries the mark of the weakest thing it
tells." Two rulings on it (item 12).
1. A beat's mark is set by what the beat tells, not by the chapters it links to. A beat may carry a higher
   mark than a chapter in its `sections` when it tells nothing of that chapter's low claim. The five beats
   named by the verifier are covered: `s-house` (medium) tells the household at "most likely" and nothing of
   who speaks through the toy or what the Nightmares are; `s-children-stay` (medium) and `s-afton-robotics`
   (high) say only that the word Remnant is on a sheet, or nothing of it; `s-night-without-end` (medium) quotes
   the lines and says that the game does not name the one in the office; `s-pizzaplex` (medium) says that the
   game never says what the burnt rabbit is. "A beat may not be firmer than its chapters" is not the rule, and
   was never a ruling.
2. A further step in a chapter's answer needs its own marker when it is weaker than the chapter's mark. Two
   answers have an unmarked step that is firmer than the mark, medium-high in a chapter marked medium
   (`child-outside`, "and she stays on in it"; `golden`, "The children stay on in the machines"). They do
   not mislead, since a reader takes them at the chapter's mark; they do not keep the site's sentence to the
   letter. The sentence should say what is done: "a further step in its answer that is weaker than that mark
   carries its own marker in the sentence".

*A sentence about what the archive does not admit.* Three places say of the link between Mike Schmidt and
Michael Afton that "the strongest evidence usually cited is in a book, which this archive does not admit"
(`michael`, `fnaf1-restaurant`, `open`). No record can carry that sentence, and none needs to: it states the
source policy and where the fandom's case lies, it does not say what the book says, and no grade moves on it
(R13: low, not used). It may stand in that form and no further: a sentence that reported the book's content,
or gave it as a reason for or against a reading, would be using it.

**Confidence.** High where a written ruling of Scott's exists. Medium for UCN, FNaF World, the layers of a
company product and Into the Pit, where the model is setting its own convention and says so.

**Why UCN may establish and Into the Pit may only agree.** Neither has a ruling from Scott; both are
fantastical; both contain tales. W11's questions separate them. UCN is Scott's own game in the main line; its
lines answer Pizzeria Simulator's last speech; a later game repeats its lines
(`sd-springtrap-pulled-apart-put-back-together`). Into the Pit adapts a book story that the source policy
excludes; it says nothing of its own status; no main-line game refers to it. If a later game took up Into the
Pit's events, the same test would move it.

**Records against.** No statement of Scott's covers UCN, so W3 is the model's convention and not his ruling. He
called Help Wanted "one game that IS canon" without setting its levels apart (`scott-2019-help-wanted-is-canon`),
so W5 is a reading of the game's own frame, not a ruling of his. W5's test for an intrusion is the model's, and
one case sits on its line: the birthday room at the end of Pizza Party is the level as built, and only the
figure's part in it is an intrusion (R18).

**A rule that is withdrawn.** An earlier version of this ruling split Security Breach's base game into "pieces"
that carry weight and a "story" that does not, because Scott said the released story "is very different" from
his (`scott-2024-interview-sb-story-not-as-intended` (a transcript)). Its only stated basis was a machine
transcript, and as worded it licensed setting aside any joined-up event of the base game. W2 gives the model
what it needs without it. Two uses are corrected: the rooftop page is not discounted, since no later game
contradicts it (R19 (5)); and no later game says that something other than the burnt rabbit moves the burnt
rabbit (R20 (3)). His remark may be quoted in the frame chapter as his remark, marked as a transcript. No rule
is built on it.

**Cut content, both ways.** The unused Help Wanted 2 lines happen to agree with R21, and Special Delivery's
undelivered emails happen to cut against R18's two firms. Neither is evidence, and neither is mentioned as
agreeing or disagreeing. Their entries in `not-evidence` say what each item is, not which way it points.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *UCN is wholly outside*, like Sister Location's Custom Night, which Scott ruled non-canon. It loses because
  that ruling named one game's mode, because UCN's lines carry one invented figure through many speakers and
  answer Pizzeria Simulator's last speech, and because later games reuse them (`sd-springtrap-pulled-apart-put-back-together`).
- *UCN is wholly text*, roster and mechanics included. It loses on the roster itself: characters Scott ruled
  non-canon, FNaF World's cast, and two forms of one being on the same night.
- *Company recreations are observations*, so that what a VR level shows did happen. It loses on the frame: the
  company says it built those levels as "completely fictitious scenarios" to "move past these childish ghost
  stories", and the tapes hidden in them report that it had commissioned the earlier games itself.
- *Everything inside a company product is staging*, intrusions included. It loses because the games mark the
  intrusions as not the company's, and because the tapes, which are frame, describe the same thing: an anomaly
  in the code that the company did not put there.

**Would change if.** Scott ruled in writing on UCN's standing or on Security Breach's base story; or a later game
treated a staged level as a record of fact, or took up Into the Pit's events.

**Depends on.** Scott's rulings; the source policy. **Carries.** W1 to W11; section 4.

Review: the third review held the rows that rest on Scott's written rulings and asked for ten changes; all are
made. W5 gains an intrusions layer with a test. Staged props are treated alike: the banner, the poster and the
items in Freddy all "agree (staged)", and none is "printed". The Private Room rule is stated as general (W10),
which is why the rooftop page now counts. The test that lets UCN establish and Into the Pit only agree is
written down (W11). Textures not seen in play cannot be "printed"; the adjudicator applied this to Murray's own
paperwork too, which the reviewer did not list and which was read the same way. Cut content is not mentioned whichever way
it points. The transcript-based rule for Security Breach is withdrawn in favour of W2. Mechanics have a test in
place of a list. W1 says what Scott ruled and what is this project's policy. The dancing figure is an
intrusion, not set dressing.

### R28. Fire: what it ends and what it does not

**Ruling.** The thesis may keep fire as the remedy that half works, with three corrections. [CHANGE]

1. **FNaF3's fire is not anyone's remedy on the record.** The paper blames faulty wiring and does not rule out
   foul play; nobody is shown setting it.
2. **Pizzeria Simulator's fire is presented as the end, and it is the Cassette Man's end.** By that game's own
   framing it frees the children: its last image is their graves. The same game hedges it: "I believe".
3. **The later games reopen it, with unequal firmness.** A Fazbear Entertainment trades after the fire, though
   at the fire it called itself "no longer a corporate entity": the site survives under the Pizzaplex with its
   name (R20). The company scans old circuit boards into software, twice (R18); that the scanning followed the
   fire is the model's convention (R33). One voice refuses release (R16). What the model holds about the killer
   afterwards, with its leans marked as leans, is in R33.

Secret of the Mimic's fire, in one of three endings, says nothing about haunting: nothing haunts that machine. It
leaves the thesis.

**Confidence**, item by item. That the fire is presented as the end: high. That a Fazbear Entertainment trades
after the fire: medium-high (the Pizzaplex stands over a buried pizzeria with that building's name, and Help
Wanted 2 keeps that game's office as a training simulation: `hw2-first-aid-simulated-pizza-place`). That Help
Wanted's scan came after the fire: a convention (R33). That the buried pizzeria is that building: medium (R20).
That a voice refuses release: stated; that it is the accuser's is the model's reading, medium at its lower
end, and which victim he is, low (R16 (3); round 4d: this read "whose it is, low", which was the second of the
two and was taken for the first). That the killer is held afterwards: low-medium (R16). That the figure in the code is something he left behind: medium, and that it is the man himself, low
(R18). That something of him persists in at least one form: medium (R33). That the fire freed the five: medium
(R14). That the Cassette Man lit it because of the blueprint's note: medium-low. [Revised after review: the
first version put the first six under one "high"; the third review separated the company's return from the
order of the scan]

**Records for.** `fnaf3-it-burns-newspaper`, `fnaf3-call1-ventilation-hallucinations`, `fnaf3-it-burns-springtrap-hidden`;
the call: `ffps-insanity-tape-call-them-back`, `ffps-completion-labyrinth`,
`ffps-completion-not-called-by-who-you-assume`; the fire and its framing: `ffps-completion-fire`,
`ffps-completion-rest`, `ffps-completion-remaining-nearby`, `ffps-completion-peace-perhaps-more`,
`ffps-completion-achievement`, `ffps-completion-tutorial-atonement`, `ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`,
`ffps-scup-overheating-neutralize`, `ffps-completion-slide-pizza-place-condemned` (the building drawn condemned, not
razed); afterwards `ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`, `security-breach-underground-pizza-place`,
`security-breach-tangle-composition`, `hw-tape-13-fe-hired-developer`, `hw-tape-05`, `sd-circuit-board-scan-virus`.

**Records against.** `ffps-completion-achievement` ("Trapped them all."); the graves and "THE END". The game does
not show who started the fire, or whether the volunteer left. No game says which machine the scanned boards came
from (`sd-which-board-unknown`), so "the company scans what was burned" is not available. The speech's "for most
of you" is not a hedge on the children: its exception is named in the next sentence, the "old friend"
(`ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`). On the model's own reading the man in UCN's office is dead
(`ucn-mr-hippo-youre-dead`), so "the fire did not end him" says more than R16 does.

**What moves the mass under the Pizzaplex.** Not shown. R20 reads the mass as what the fire left of Molten Freddy
(low-medium). With R30 no longer placing the five in Molten Freddy, a mass that lies there and attacks is not
a showing of the five still bound, and the model does not read it as one.

**The other remedy.** For the thing in the code the games show a different cure being tried: shut it behind a
door, then destroy it there (R19, Princess Quest). One ending of Help Wanted 2 shows it crushed, and RUIN, most
likely the later of the two, still has a rabbit's shadow in the mask: low-medium that it works. The thesis may say that the thing is fought from inside the
game. It does not say that this works, or that it works "where fire did not": the fire was aimed at haunted
machines and this at a presence in code, and neither is shown to have finished its target.

**Wording for the storyline beat.** Not "gathers the last four haunted machines... and burns it". Say: "The
Cassette Man calls four haunted machines into one building and stays as it burns." His words are "I am
remaining as well. I am nearby" (`ffps-completion-remaining-nearby`); "inside" was the first version's reading of
"remaining". William Afton is among those the fire is shown to burn, and the game presents that as his end;
what persists of him afterwards is R33's question, at the confidences given there. Do not write "burned, not
ended" as a fact.

**What the fire is shown to do (confirmed and limited in round 4d).** The record as it now stands: during the
speech "a fire breaks out in the pizzeria", and three of the salvaged machines are shown burning, in interior
shots: Scrap Baby, Molten Freddy and the salvaged rabbit. Lefty is not shown burning (a fan wiki's captures of
the scene were checked, and the wiki notes the same). That the fire destroys the building is a fan wiki's
account. So "stays as it burns", "the building burns" and "a burning building" stand, in the sense that the
pizzeria is on fire while he remains: the record's own title is "the pizzeria burns during the speech". What
may not be said as shown: that the building "burns down", "is destroyed", "is razed" or "goes up in flames";
that all four machines burn (three are shown; that Lefty and the Puppet's child burn with them is the speech's
intent, "my daughter... it's time to rest", and an inference); or who lights it. The last slide draws the place
standing and condemned (`ffps-completion-slide-pizza-place-condemned`), and Security Breach finds a ruined
pizzeria with that name on its sign (R20: medium that it is this building).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** "Fire is the remedy that keeps failing": three fires (Fazbear's
Fright, the Cassette Man's, the Costume Manor's), each tried as a cure and each survived. It is the neater motif
and the current thesis's. It loses on each leg: nobody is shown choosing the first fire, the second does what its
author most wanted (the children's graves are the game's last image), and the third burns a machine nothing
haunts. What certainly survives the second fire is the site and the company, and that is what the thesis may say
flat.

**Would change if.** A game showed the five still bound after Pizzeria Simulator's fire, or showed who set
Fazbear's Fright alight and why.

Review: all seven of group B's findings are accepted. "Scans what was burned" becomes "scans old circuit
boards". "For most of you, I believe" is no longer quoted as a hedge on the children: the hedge is "I believe".
The confidence line is itemised. "With him inside" becomes "stays as it burns". "The blueprint never promised
it" is removed, because it assumed the fire applied the blueprint. The three records of the call are cited. And
the ruling says what it makes of the mass under the Pizzaplex. One change goes beyond the findings and follows
R16's re-rating: "the killer is not finished" is no longer stated flat, here or in the wording rule.

Review: in the third round three things here changed with other rulings. "And it works", said of Princess
Quest, claimed more than R19 now allows, and the comparison with fire is withdrawn. "Back in business and
scans" no longer implies that the scan followed the fire. And what persists of William Afton after the fire
is stated once, in R33.

**Depends on.** R14, R15, R16, R18, R19, R20, R30. **Carries.** The thesis; R33.

### R29. Midnight Motorist

**Ruling.** The minigame identifies nobody, and the model draws nothing from it for the Afton household. Two
readings are stated side by side: the killer's own house, or a victim's.

**Confidence.** That the driver is William Afton: low. Everything else: low. [Revised after review: the driver
was low-medium, and the minigame was "weak corroboration" for R3]

**Records for the killer's house.** The secret area's dark purple car (`ffps-midnight-motorist-secret-drive`), like
the car in `fnaf2-take-cake-to-the-children`; "I told you not to close your door... This is my house"
(`ffps-midnight-motorist-closed-door`) beside FNaF4's "He locked you in your room again" (`fnaf4-plush-locked-in-room`);
"I'll find a way in from outside" (`ffps-midnight-motorist-find-a-way-in`), echoed by UCN's cartoon lurer
(`ucn-toy-chica-hsy-find-a-window`); a rainy night, as in Security Puppet's last play.

**Records against.** The car's colour is the only link to the killer, and W8 rules a colour out as an
identification. In this minigame the ordinary traffic glows purple too (notes to
`ffps-midnight-motorist-secret-drive`). The driver's sprite is flat yellow, not the purple these games use for
the killer (notes to `ffps-midnight-motorist-closed-door`). The one behind the door has shut it himself; the
lines say only "he", and that he is a boy is the usual reading. Nobody is named and no relationship is stated.
The footprints behind the house are known from a fan wiki's descriptions, which differ, and were not seen
(`ffps-midnight-motorist-ran-off`). `ffps-midnight-motorist-later-that-night` is a data label and is not evidence (W7).

**If it is his house**, there are two readings of the boy behind the door, and the chapter gives both. He has "ran
off to that place again" (`ffps-midnight-motorist-ran-off`), which suits the older brother better than the crying
child, who is frightened of the place and is left there against his will (`fnaf4-left-behind-at-diner`). But the
figure by the television speaks in grey, the colour of the older brother's lines in FNaF4
(`ffps-midnight-motorist-grey-person`, `fnaf4-speaker-text-colours`); if that figure is the brother, the boy behind
the door is the younger child. A text colour is no better an identification than a sprite's colour (W8), so
neither reading is adopted. JR's and the mound are unexplained (`ffps-midnight-motorist-jrs`, `ffps-midnight-motorist-mound`).

**Strongest alternative.** A victim's house: the three lore minigames as three children's last stories (a girl
lured with a lie about her dog, a girl locked out, a boy who runs from a violent home to "that place"). It is
level with the other reading; the chapter states both.

**Would change if.** A game identified the driver, the house or the boy.

Review: group A's two findings are accepted. "The driver is William Afton" falls to low, because the only link is
a colour that this minigame also gives its ordinary traffic, and R3 no longer counts the minigame at all. Both
readings of the boy are now given.

**Depends on.** Nothing. **Carries.** Nothing.

### R30. Molten Freddy, Ennard, and where the five are during Pizzeria Simulator

**Ruling.** Molten Freddy is what is left of Ennard after the group threw Baby out. Where the five are between
the hidden room and the graves is not shown. One man says what became of them, in general terms; the model
reports his account step by step, in the table below, and adds no mechanism of its own. That Molten Freddy is
the machine that holds them is one of two candidates, and it is no longer the model's telling. [CHANGE: "this
archive reads it as what was left of Ennard" gains its records. Revised after review: the first version of this
ruling made "Molten Freddy holds what was taken from the five" the best available reading]

**Confidence.** Ennard's remainder: medium-high. That the children are among what answers the Cassette Man's
call: medium. That Molten Freddy is what holds them: low. [Revised after review: was low-medium]

**One account of the five, from the hidden room to the graves.** R12, R14, R15, R16, R20 and R28 all refer to
this table; where one of them once said something else, the table governs.

| Step (section 3.2) | Where the five are | Mark | Records |
|---|---|---|---|
| 8, the murders | Four are in Freddy, Bonnie, Chica and Foxy: the Puppet gives four dead children those faces, and the mascots smell, stain and walk. The fifth is given no machine in any scene; the yellow bear marks him | medium-high that the four stay in the four; medium that the yellow bear is the fifth child's; how, withheld (R8) | `fnaf2-give-gifts-give-life`, `fnaf1-news-sanitation`, `fnaf2-call2-the-smell` |
| 12 to 14, 1987 and FNaF1 | The same. The older four walk in 1987 and in FNaF1; the yellow bear comes and goes | medium-high, inferred from the hauntings | `fnaf2-old-models-walk-from-night2`, `fnaf1-golden-freddy-office-figure` |
| 15, the dismantling | Out of the machines. The killer takes the four apart; five grey, crying child figures stand among the parts, corner him, and fade when the suit closes on him | shown. That the figures are the five's ghosts, and so that the five are "out of the machines" here, is the model's reading (R12's rule). "Fade" is what the screen does. The model does not read it as release, or as their going anywhere | `fnaf3-eon-purple-guy-dismantles`, `fnaf3-n5-ghosts-safe-room`, `fnaf3-n5-springlock-death` |
| Between 15 and 20 | **Not shown.** The Cassette Man's tape is the only account: lured back to a familiar place, brought together, overpowered and robbed "again", "now set to new purpose and used in ways I never thought imaginable", and "they will never find rest now. Not like this" | stated, by one man, in general terms. What was taken, by what means and into what: not stated. That the luring and overpowering are step 15 itself is a reading, low-medium: the tape names no place | `ffps-insanity-lured-back`, `ffps-insanity-trap-again`, `ffps-insanity-small-souls-prisons`, `ffps-insanity-new-way-to-desecrate`, `ffps-insanity-tiny-breaths` |
| 17, Fazbear's Fright | Children are given cake in the attraction's hidden minigames, and in one of the game's two endings six of them leave | the scenes are shown. Whether they are an event of step 17, and which ending happened, is open (R14). The model does not reconcile this row with the one above; Pizzeria Simulator, which follows, speaks of the small souls as without rest | `fnaf3-happiest-day`, `fnaf3-bad-ending-heads`, `fnaf3-good-ending-heads`, `scott-2018-interview-fnaf3-ending-unanswered` (a transcript) |
| 20, the call | Among what answers it: "I have to call them all back. All of them. Together in one place"; "give up your spirits. They don't belong to you" | medium that they are in the machines that come. Which machine: not stated; two candidates, each low | `ffps-insanity-tape-call-them-back`, `ffps-completion-labyrinth`, `ffps-completion-give-up-spirits` |
| 20, the fire | Told as their release: "I believe there is peace"; "it's time to rest, for you, and for those you have carried in your arms". Six graves are shown after the credits | medium, on that game's framing (R14, R28). Nothing shows a release | `ffps-completion-peace-perhaps-more`, `ffps-completion-rest`, `ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones` |
| 21, afterwards | One voice refuses: "He tried to release us. But I'm not gonna let that happen" | stated. That the voice is the accuser's: the model's reading (R16 (3), part (b)). Which victim he is: a lean at low | `ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release` |
| Later | Nothing shows the five again. A mass of animatronic parts lies under the Pizzaplex and attacks; the model does not read that as the five, and what moves it is not shown (R20) | not shown | `security-breach-tangle-composition` |

**The two candidate hosts at step 20.**
- *Molten Freddy.* It is the one salvage that is many: loose eyes in a mound of wire
  (`ffps-molten-freddy-alley-wires-eyes`), and the plural voice of Scott's page source, "We outnumber you"
  (`scottgames-site-2017-source-ejection-dialogue-group`). It speaks of a reunion, "Together aga-ain!", "One bi-i-ig
  happy family!" (`ffps-molten-freddy-lines`), where the tape has "He brought them all together"
  (`ffps-insanity-lured-back`). Candy Cadet's five-into-one stories point the same way at parable weight (R25).
  Against: it does not speak as many. Its own pronouns are singular: "Thanks for letting me join the pa-a-arty",
  "I'-Im here!" (`ffps-molten-freddy-knock-knock`). The many may be the Funtime machines' own minds. And the
  reading needs a step that no record shows: something carried from the four dismantled machines into the
  Funtime machines before Michael's week, by a man who dies on the last night of the dismantling. It also needs
  R12's convention that Michael's week follows that death.
- *The Puppet's keeping.* The Cassette Man tells his daughter to rest "for you, and for those you have carried
  in your arms", and remembers "the way you lifted others into yours" (`ffps-completion-rest`,
  `ffps-completion-shut-out`); Lefty says "there is room for one more" (`ucn-lefty-lines`). Against: "carried" may
  mean no more than what Give Gifts shows, and a UCN line is an effigy's (R16).

The model picks neither.

**The count.** Four machines were taken apart, and no scene gives the fifth child a machine (R8). Where this
ruling says "the five" it follows the Cassette Man's "all of them" and the five ghosts of step 15. It does not
say how the fifth comes to be among what is called back.

**Records for Ennard's remainder.** The body is a mass of wires and eyes like the one the walker of Sister
Location's last cutscene brings up into a drain (`ffps-molten-freddy-alley-wires-eyes`,
`sl-custom-night-ennard-ejected`); the voice has Funtime Freddy's performer, as Scrap Baby's has Baby's
(`ffps-credits-returning-voices`: a comparison of two cast lists, cited with "(a fan wiki's account)", and
support only: R27, "Cast lists"); Baby, the merged body's other voice, is now separate
(`ffps-completion-connection-terminated-elizabeth`); Scott published the missing step in 2017, a group telling one
voice "We found a way to eject you" and the voice answering "I can put myself back together"
(`scottgames-site-2017-source-ejection-dialogue-group`, `scottgames-site-2017-fnafworld-source-ejection-dialogue-one`,
`scottgames-site-2017-fnafworld-iamstillhere-eyes`; no speaker is named).

**Records against.** In the archive's records Pizzeria Simulator never says "Ennard"
(`ffps-molten-freddy-alley-wires-eyes`, notes). The tape before Molten Freddy's salvage says only
that things are found in the alley "quite often" and "we aren't sure why" (`ffps-salvage-first-tape-alley`);
"we are unsure of its origins" is the later tapes', of the other three (`ffps-salvage-origins-unknown`). UCN lists Ennard and Molten Freddy as two entries, which under W3 means nothing.
Nothing says "the five are in Molten Freddy". Scrap Baby's "all of those little souls, in one place" was cited
here in the first version and is withdrawn: on the one reading now used in R15, R30 and R32, her little souls
are the children she expects at Saturday's party (`ffps-scrap-baby-completion-monologue`,
`ffps-tutorial-saturday-party`), and the Cassette Man's answer is that she will never find them
(`ffps-completion-chasing-cries`).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the five were released at Happiest Day and are in nothing at
step 20, and what answers the call holds other spirits: Elizabeth, the Puppet's child, the Funtime machines' own.
It fits R14's surviving alternative. It loses, as the telling, on the Cassette Man's "Small souls trapped in
prisons of my making", said in Pizzeria Simulator's own week of souls "now set to new purpose", and on "I have to
call them all back". It does not lose on anything this ruling can show about a machine.

**Would change if.** A game showed what happened to the dismantled four's occupants between the hidden room and
the alley.

Review: group B asked for one account of where the five are, or a plain "not shown" and the second clause cut
to low. The ruling does both: the table gives the account with a mark on every step, the interval is marked
"not shown", and "Molten Freddy holds the five" falls to low. All six findings are accepted: Scrap Baby's line
is withdrawn; "speaks as many" was wrong and is replaced by the records that make it many; the Puppet's keeping
is listed as a second candidate; "He brought them all together" and "I have to call them all back" are cited;
the carrying step and its dependence on R12 are stated; and the count of four machines for five children is
stated and not explained.

**Depends on.** R8, R12 (the interval; the convention about Michael's week), R14, R15, R16. **Carries.** R14, R20
(the mass of parts), R28.

### R31. Fazbear Entertainment's conduct

**Ruling.** The company behaves as one actor in every game that shows it, and the model treats it as one.
Nearly every official explanation in these games is a denial or a programming quirk, and each concession comes
with a denial or a disclaimer beside it. It turns its own history into product, and it scans old circuit boards
into software twice (which machines the boards came from, no game says: R18). That is a pattern the records
state over and over. The record is not unbroken: between FNaF1's restaurant and Pizzeria Simulator no act of
the company is on record, the attraction that sells its history in those years is somebody else's (below), and
one line says it is "no longer a corporate entity" (records against). Three things are not
part of the stated pattern: that the scanning came "after the fire", which is the model's convention (R33);
that the Cassette Man ran his trap through the company; and that the company knew what its machine in 1979 had
done to the men sent before.

**Confidence.** High for the pattern from 1987 on. Medium-low that the Cassette Man acted through the company.
Convention for "after the fire".

**Records for the pattern.** A missing-person report only after the premises "have been thoroughly cleaned and
bleached" (`fnaf1-call1-company-disclaimer`); "has nothing to do with our establishment", "denies any wrongdoing"
(`fnaf2-call3-rumors`, `fnaf2-call4-investigation`); "possible malfunctions" (`fnaf2-news-originals-kept`); "we are free
of liability, do as you wish", and a sealed room not to be mentioned to "insurance representatives"
(`fnaf3-tape4-do-as-you-wish`, `fnaf3-tape6-not-to-be-mentioned`); attacks on staff explained twice as a quirk
(`fnaf1-call1-stuffed-into-suit`, `fnaf2-call1-endoskeleton-glitch`). In 1979: a contractor's work claimed as
"legally ours" (`secret-of-the-mimic-dispatch-contract-legally-ours`), and, in the one ending of three where he
walks away, a technician dismissed for "misplacing company property" and held "personally responsible" by a
Dispatch voice that says the company has been trying to reach him all night
(`secret-of-the-mimic-clock-out-real-dispatch`). The dismissal is that branch's event (W10). That some or all
of the voice on his radio that night had not been the company's is the usual reading of the scene, and the
cabinet's reveal states that a machine "had impersonated Dispatch"; where the impersonation begins is not
stated (R2's rule).
Then the indie games, the VR game, the waiver, the second contractor and the lawyers' halt (`hw-tape-13-fe-hired-developer`, `hw-handunit-anomaly-fun-fact`,
`hw-waiver-consciousness-transference`, `sd-fe-legal-halt-vintage-hardware`); and a customer at the Pizzaplex: "Why
did you reopen? Everyone remembers what happened to those kids" (`security-breach-msg-it-is-happening-again`).
The concessions, at least three: "some stories associated with our name were loosely based on actual events",
in the breath before it calls "the majority of them" "total fabrications from the mind of a complete lunatic"
(`hw-intro-loosely-based-on-actual-events`, `hw-intro-complete-lunatic`); a "hilarious mistake" that "may or
may not have resulted in an undesirable anomaly", offered as "our full legal disclaimer"
(`hw-handunit-anomaly-fun-fact`); and an apology for a situation not "presented... in a completely honest
fashion", followed by a justification and not a denial, which is the company's own only if the trap was
(`ffps-completion-tutorial-atonement`).

**Not the company's: Fazbear's Fright.** The attraction of FNaF3 belongs to a "Local amusement park"
(`fnaf3-coming-soon-newspaper`; "A new local attraction based on an ancient pizzeria chain":
`fnaf3-it-burns-newspaper`). It is other people selling the company's history, and it is not counted in the
pattern. Between FNaF1's restaurant and Pizzeria Simulator no act of the company is on record.

**If the trap was the company's (medium-low).** Four Pizzeria Simulator items are the company's conduct only if
the Cassette Man acted through it; otherwise they are his cover and his voice. The franchise with its
disappearance disclaimer (`ffps-tutorial-rebranded-disclaimer`); the franchisee discredited for having "prevented a
tidy resolution to a messy problem" (`ffps-insanity-certificate`, `ffps-blacklisted-ending`); "atoning for past
mistakes and tying up loose ends" (`ffps-completion-tutorial-atonement`); and the trap's own hardware, LEFTE and
RASC, marked Fazbear Entertainment, Inc, where only the S.C.U.P. sheet is marked Afton Robotics, LLC (`ffps-lefte-blueprint`,
`ffps-rasc-blueprint`, `ffps-scup-blueprint`). They are kept apart from the pattern for that reason.

**What 1979 shows and what it does not.** Bodies are found in character suits in Murray's building
(`secret-of-the-mimic-previous-technicians`; held as described, with no capture on file). That they were
technicians Fazbear had sent, and that it "will send another", is said by the voice on the radio and by the
cabinet that speaks as Dispatch (`secret-of-the-mimic-f10n4-fazbear-will-send-another`).
So: men had been sent before him and had not come back, by that voice's account. That the company knew they
were dead, or sent this one knowing it, is inferred. What the company certainly said that night is the call on
the road, the two work orders and the dismissal (R2).

**Not conduct.** "They come to life at night. And if you die, they hide your body and never tell anyone"
(`fnaf4-pigtail-girl-hide-your-body`) is a child's rumour, and its "they" are the machines. It shows the rumour
was already current around 1983. It is not a record of what the company did. And the "gas leaks" given for the
cancelled opening of Circus Baby's Pizza World (`scottgames-site-sl-news-gas-leak-cancellation`) are the other
