firm's excuse, not Fazbear's; a chapter that uses it says so.

**Records against.** `ffps-completion-tutorial-atonement` also says the company is "no longer a corporate entity";
Help Wanted has it trading. Either that did not last, or was not true, or Help Wanted's company comes before
that line was spoken (R33: the order is a convention).

**Strongest alternatives, and why they lose.**
- *A new owner of an old brand*, so that the conduct is not one actor's across fifty years. "No longer a
  corporate entity" supports it. It loses as a description of behaviour: the later company knows enough to
  commission a cover story, to write the waiver it writes, and to have its lawyers halt work on "vintage
  hardware" by name. Whether the shareholders changed is not recorded and does not alter the pattern.
- *The Pizzaplex's conduct is not wholly the company's own.* An unqualified head of security is appointed
  because "the recommendation came from the top", on an internal reference whose name is withheld
  (`security-breach-msg-marked-for-deletion`; `security-breach-msg-no-questions-asked`, `implied`). Either the
  company's top serves the thing in the network, or the thing has reached the company's top. On the second
  reading some of what the Pizzaplex does is done to the company and not by it. The records do not choose; the
  chapter should put the two readings side by side.

**Would change if.** A game showed a change of ownership together with a real ignorance of the past.

**Depends on.** R2 (the voice on the radio); R33 (the order). **Carries.** One recurring thread from 1979 to RUIN;
the frame chapter.

Review: the third review held the pattern for the modern company and asked for six corrections; all are made.
"Every" becomes "nearly every", with the two admissions named. The Pizzeria Simulator items stand on their own
line, conditional on a medium-low reading. For 1979 the dismissal is cited, and "sent to their deaths" becomes
what the records carry. The child's rumour leaves the list of conduct. "After the fire" is marked the model's
convention. The appointment "from the top" is cited as a test of "a continuous actor", and the gas-leak excuse
is given to the firm that made it. After the fact-check (round four) the concessions are three, Fazbear's
Fright is set outside the pattern as a local amusement park's, and "a continuous actor" becomes "one actor in
every game that shows it", with the gap in the record stated.

### R32. Afton Robotics: what the Funtimes were for

**Ruling.** The Funtime animatronics were designed to lure a child and take it, and the one child the games show
them take is their maker's own daughter. After the pizzeria built for them never opened, the same machines were
hired out to children's parties by the day. The current chapter's "The first child taken is his own daughter" is
not supported as an order and goes. The two "companion blueprints" in the current chapter are not Afton's.
[CHANGE]

**Confidence.** High that the designs are for luring and taking a child. That rests on three things together and
not on the sheets alone: the labels, what Baby did, and a father who kept his own daughter away from her. That
the machines were built to hold children is inferred from "Storage Tank" and the claw: medium. High that the
daughter was taken by Baby. Medium-high that she persists in the machine. High that the same machines were
hired out to private parties (stated). Low-medium that the rentals were a way to take children: no victim is on
record. [Revised after review: the first version said "the blueprint text says so" and gave one "medium" to the
last two]

**Records for.** The board's question about "design choices" (`sl-intro-mr-afton-board`). The blueprints, each
marked Afton Robotics, LLC: Funtime Freddy's "Parental Tracking", "Voice Mimic/ Luring" and "Storage Tank",
Funtime Foxy's "Parental Voice Sync & Replay" and "Variable Scent Release", Ballora's "Deter & Misdirect", and on
Baby's an ice cream dispenser (`sl-blueprint-funtime-freddy`, `sl-blueprint-funtime-foxy`, `sl-blueprint-ballora`,
`sl-blueprint-circus-baby`). No sheet says "children" or what the tank stores, and Baby's sheet has no claw; the
Funtime Freddy record's own note is "The sheet draws no such conclusion itself". The daughter forbidden to go
near Baby (`sl-girl-daddy-let-me-play`). Baby's one day (`sl-baby-on-stage-one-day`, `sl-baby-ice-cream-story`,
`sl-baby-minigame-girl-grabbed`). What she became (`sl-baby-something-bad-inside`, `sl-baby-how-to-pretend`,
`ffps-completion-connection-terminated-elizabeth`). What the machines say they were made for: at Saturday's
party, Scrap Baby takes "all of those little souls, in one place" for "a gift", and says "Now we can do what we
were created to do, and be complete" (`ffps-scrap-baby-completion-monologue`, `ffps-scrap-baby-make-you-proud-daddy`,
`ffps-tutorial-saturday-party`); the Cassette Man answers that they have been "chasing the cries of children in
some unseen chamber" and will never find them (`ffps-completion-chasing-cries`). Her little souls are the children
she expects at the party. That is the one reading of the record used in R15, R30 and here. The cancelled opening
and the rentals (`scottgames-site-sl-news-gas-leak-cancellation`, `scottgames-site-sl-news-kids-tested`,
`scottgames-site-sl-source-rental-schedule`, `scottgames-site-sl-source-client-directory`,
`scottgames-site-sl-source-bright-future-circus-baby`, `sl-handunit-rented-for-private-parties`). Two more
paragraphs of the same hidden report: large equipment taken out of the building at night under tarps, and the
building for sale a few weeks later (`scottgames-site-sl-news-equipment-removed`; where it went is not said); and
"the local entrepreneur who financed the venture" (`scottgames-site-sl-news-entrepreneur`; not named). The
machines' own motive for the scooping (`sl-real-ending-they-always-put-us-back`,
`sl-real-ending-if-we-looked-like-you`).

**Records against.** No child other than the daughter is shown taken by these machines. The rental business has
no victim on record. "I know it was an accident" (`sl-fake-ending-girl-voice`) carries no weight here, as it
carries none in R3: it is spoken by a machine that takes Michael for his father, and the game does not explain
it. That her father tried to keep her away rests on her own lines before each night. LEFTE and RASC are marked
Fazbear Entertainment, Inc and belong to the Cassette Man's trap, not to this chapter (`ffps-lefte-blueprint`,
`ffps-rasc-blueprint`, `ffps-rasc-audio-central-server`).

**Strongest alternative, and why it loses.** That the designs are safety or entertainment features read darkly
(tracking parents so as to return a lost child; a tank for ice cream), and Elizabeth's death was a malfunction. It
loses on the labels themselves ("Luring", "Deter & Misdirect", "Parental Voice Sync & Replay"), on Baby's own
account of what she did when she was alone with one child, and on "Now we can do what we were created to do",
said of children gathered in one place. The board's question about the designs is the game pointing at them.

**Would change if.** A dated record for the Pizza World (R12), or a second victim.

Review: all five of group B's findings are accepted. The sheets do not say what they are for, and the ruling no
longer claims they do: "high" now rests on the labels together with what Baby did and a father's prohibition,
and "holding" is marked as inferred. Scrap Baby's line has one reading across R15, R30 and this ruling. The
join between the designs and the rentals is split into what is stated and what is not. The two uncited
paragraphs of Scott's hidden report are cited. And "I know it was an accident" is given the same weight here as
in R3: none.

**Depends on.** R1, R12. **Carries.** The "machines" part.

### R33. William Afton after the fire: what the model holds when its rulings are taken together

New after the third review. Four later things each carry "something of him", each in its own ruling, and the
model had never added them up. This ruling adds nothing to the records; it says what R16, R18, R20 and R28 come
to jointly, so that the thesis and the chapters tell one thing.

**Ruling.**

1. **The game of the fire presents it as his end.** Pizzeria Simulator shows him burn with the others, and its
   speaker sends the "old friend" to Hell. No record from a game after UCN carries his name.
2. **Four later things carry something of him. Each is ruled on its own.**

| | What is shown or stated | What the model reads into it | Confidence | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| a | In UCN a voice refuses release and holds someone in a night without end | The one held is the killer | low-medium, a lean | R16 |
| b | A personality from old company boards is in the VR game's code and presents as the killer | It is a leaving of the man, not a copier that learned him | presents as him: medium-high; a leaving of him: medium | R18 |
| c | Customers report a foul old rabbit that is in none of the service's databases | It is his body, abroad after the fire | low-medium, and only on the convention in 4 | R18 |
| d | Burnt rabbit remains stand on a frame under the Pizzaplex and seize a machine | They are what the fire left of him | low-medium | R20 |

3. **Taken together.**
   - **His image returns after the fire, in more than one form.** This the later games show, and the model says
     it flat.
   - **Something of the man himself persists, in at least one of those forms.** It needs only one of a to d to be
     what it looks like. The model holds this, and tells b as the form it prefers.
   - **All four as one history** (he is held in a night, a leaving of him is in the code, his body walked
     abroad, his remains stand under the Mega Pizzaplex) the model does **not** tell. The four fit together
     only on a picture no record gives: the man held in a night, a leaving of him in the code, and that leaving,
     not he, moving the old shell abroad and under the Pizzaplex. Each joint of that picture is low (R16; R20
     (3)). Without it a and d pull against each other: if he is held in a night, what Freddy calls "he" under
     the Pizzaplex is not he; and if the remains act of themselves, he is not wholly held.
4. **The order "fire, then scan" is the model's convention.** No record places Help Wanted's scan against
   Pizzeria Simulator's fire. What is inferred, at medium, is only that the Pizzaplex comes after the fire.
5. **What a chapter may say.** Below.

**Confidence.** That his image returns: high. That something of the man persists in at least one form: medium.
All four as one history: low. The order of the fire and the scan: a convention.

**Records for 1.** `ffps-completion-fire`, `ffps-completion-old-friend-hell`, `ffps-lorekeeper-gravestones`,
`ffps-completion-achievement`. Afterwards: no "Afton" in Security Breach's dialogue, messages, recordings or
cutscenes (`security-breach-burntrap-name-not-in-game`); Special Delivery's Springtrap is never called William or
Afton (`sd-springtrap-did-not-save-the-others`); no tape or email names him (`hw-tape-05`, `sd-which-board-unknown`).
UCN's roster does (`ucn-roster-afton`), which is why the line is drawn after UCN.

**Records for 3, first clause.** `hw-rabbit-figure-appearance`, `hw-mobile-princess-quest-graveyard`,
`hw2-memory-dolls`, `sd-vintage-bonnie-reports`, `security-breach-burntrap-appearance`,
`security-breach-freddy-take-control`, `ruin-gregory-comics-depict-endings`, `hw2-fazerblast-fire-springtrap`.

**The order: what bears on it.** Fitting "the scan came after the fire": six stones on Princess Quest's route
(`hw-mobile-princess-quest-graveyard`), like the six that close Pizzeria Simulator, though when those were set is
not said; and the model's own reading of b, since boards that carry something of him are most simply boards
from what the fire left. That second point cannot be counted, because no game says which boards
(`sd-which-board-unknown`): b leans on the convention, and the convention cannot lean back on b. Fitting "the
scan came first, or at the same time": a foul old rabbit at large (`sd-vintage-bonnie-reports`) beside
"something outside that's been trying to get in for months" (`ffps-tutorial-something-outside`); a company with
"lawsuits pending" in Help Wanted (`hw-intro-complete-lunatic`) and "no longer a corporate entity" at the fire
(`ffps-completion-tutorial-atonement`); "the newly re-branded Freddy Fazbear's Pizza" (`ffps-tutorial-rebranded-disclaimer`)
beside the tape's "This isn't just an attempt to rebrand" (`hw-tape-13-fe-hired-developer`); a "buyout" under way
(`hw-tape-10`). None decides it. What is inferred is narrower: the Pizzaplex stands over a buried pizzeria with
that building's name (R20 (1): medium), and the woman the VR game took is at the Pizzaplex (R19), so the
Pizzaplex follows both the fire and the scan.

**What hangs on the convention.** If the scan came first: b is something of him taken while he was still at
large, and "a leaving on boards" has no wreck to come from; c is the rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator's alley
before the fire, which that game already implies; a and d are untouched. R31's "after the fire" and step 22 of
section 3.2 are marked accordingly.

**What a chapter may say.**

| Statement | How to say it |
|---|---|
| The fire is presented as his end | Flat, always |
| A figure in the killer's costume is in the company's code; burnt rabbit remains stand under the Pizzaplex and seize a machine; customers report a foul old rabbit in no database | Flat: each is shown or stated |
| The figure in the code presents as the killer | Flat (medium-high) |
| The figure in the code is something he left behind | "The model reads" (medium) |
| The remains under the Pizzaplex are his; the foul rabbit is his body after the fire | Only with "may be" and the reason (low-medium each) |
| He is the one held in UCN's night | Only inside the UCN chapter, as its lean (low-medium), with the Michael reading beside it |
| Any sentence that puts him in two of these places at once | Not as fact. If a chapter sets out the picture in 3, it says that the picture is the model's and that each joint is low |
| "Afton survived the fire"; "burned, not ended"; "he is back" | Never |

**Records against "something of the man persists".** The fire's own game says the opposite and ends on graves
and "THE END". No later game names him. Every sign under b is surface that a copier which had learned him would
wear too (R18). What moves the remains under d is not said.

**Strongest alternatives, and why they do not win.**
- *Nothing of him persists: the fire ended him, and every later rabbit is a copier's doing or the company's.*
  This is the modern era's single-villain reading (R21) joined to Michael in UCN's office (R16). The records
  allow each of its parts, and together it is low-medium. It does not win because the mechanism this era states,
  a personality carried on an old board, reaches a leaving of the killer in one unrecorded step, where a copier
  needs two (R18).
- *He survived whole: the man himself is in the code and under the Pizzaplex.* It is the reading the line "I
  always come back" invites. No game says it; the line in Help Wanted is a fan decode, and a copy would say it
  too. Low.

**Would change if.** A game named him after the fire; said which board, or whose remains; or dated the scan
against the fire.

**Depends on.** R16, R18, R20, R28. **Carries.** The last third of the thesis; the chapters on UCN, the rabbit in
the code and the Pizzaplex.

Review: this ruling answers the third review's sixth point on the thesis and the editor's request. The four
strands were each at medium or below and were never added up; Special Delivery's rabbit was "the copy" in one
place and the original in another; and "fire, then scan" was listed as inferred where no record bears on it.

## 3. Dating table

Kinds follow the site's `DateKind`: **printed** (the date is on screen or spoken in a game or in Scott's own
text), **derived** (follows from printed dates by a stated step), **estimated** (the model's bracket), **relative**
(placed only by order), **teaser**, **unknown**. A "printed" date needs a non-implied record. After review,
"derived" is kept for arithmetic from printed dates. A date that depends on a reading (which building, which
closing, which clipping) is marked **inferred** here; `dates.ts` has no such kind, so enter those rows as
`estimated` or `relative` and state the basis. After the third review two more marks are used. A date on a
prop inside a company product is **agrees (staged)** (W5): it may agree with a date that an unstaged record
already carries, and is never "printed". A date read from a texture whose place in play nobody on the project
has confirmed is **texture, placement unconfirmed** (W7), and is not "printed" until it is seen in play.

### 3.1 Every date the model uses

| # | Event | Date or interval | Kind | Records | Reasoning |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### 3.2 The order of events

**F** = fixed by records (high or medium-high). **I** = inferred (medium, unless the mark gives another figure).
**C** = the model's convention: the records allow other positions, and the chapter must say so. After the first
review, steps 6 to 9, 11 and 12 mark each inference inside the step separately. After the second, steps 2, 9, 10,
14, 15, 17, 20, 21, 22 and 25 were re-marked: each had an F that rested on an implied record, an undated
clipping or a reading. After the third, steps 1, 3, 4, 5 and 22 to 28 were re-marked or reworded, and the
place of the scan against the fire became a convention. Nothing in a step is F unless the mark says so.

**Round four (3 October): steps 6 and 7 changed places.** The order now tells the 1983 week (step 6), then the
child outside (step 7), then the five (step 8), as the storyline and the timeline do. No record orders the
first two, so both places are conventions; the one convention the model argues, the child outside before the
five, now stands next to what it is before. The order of telling dates nothing: the bracket for the five
stays 1980 to 1986. Documents written before this round that say "step 6" mean the child outside, and "step 7"
the 1983 week.

| # | Step | Mark |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | A costume company run by Edwin Murray, with Fiona Murray, the mother of his son, as designer, works under contract for Fazbear Entertainment. It makes springlock suits, some "for the diner", and a client the logs call "Hen" orders changes | F for the contract and the suits; I, medium-high, that the diner is Fredbear's; low that Hen is the Cassette Man: the model notes the possibility and does not tell it |
| 2 | Murray builds M1, an endoskeleton, to mind his son David. The boy's mother is gone: Fiona Murray, on the reading the recordings support, dead after she left to help at a Fall Fest installation. A voice speaking as Fiona is then heard in Edwin's logs, and the model reads it as M1's | F for M1 and its purpose, for "his mother is gone", and for a voice that speaks as Fiona. I, strong, that the mother is Fiona and that she is dead: the log does not speak her name (ROUND 4D: this was "F for her death"). I that she died at the installation: no record states the cause. The model's reading, medium-high, that the voice is M1's: the logs name no speaker. No record orders the building of M1 against her going |
| 3 | David dies. A voice speaking as Fiona urges Murray to "try again. Build another", and he builds M2, which an unsigned message calls "a copy of my program". He loses his temper with it; it "mimics rage" | F for the two machines and for each quoted line. David's death is implied, never stated (a party cancelled for "recent tragic events"; "It wouldn't be him"). The model's reading, medium-high each (ROUND 4D: the first two were told flat): that the urging voice is M1's; that the program copied is M1's (the logs name no speaker, and the message is unsigned); that M2 follows the death and the urging (the log has the urging, and no record orders Edwin's logs); that the rage it mimics is his. The year, low-medium |
| 4 | A party signing "W.A." tells Murray that it now owns his land, bids for his software and his whole catalogue, and asks an insider for his staff's contact details. Murray shuts himself away | F for the land, the bid and the request, which the letters state; I, medium-high, that the party is Fazbear Entertainment; I, medium, that W.A. is William Afton |
| 5 | 1979, most likely 7 September: a Fazbear technician is sent to Murray's shuttered workshop for the blueprints. A machine in the building, the cabinet F10-N4, speaks as Dispatch on his radio (stated at its reveal: it "had impersonated Dispatch"; where its voice takes over is not stated: R2's rule; that it is M1's mind is the model's reading: R21). Bodies are found in character suits (as described: no capture is on file). Three endings | F for the night and the errand (the day: medium-high) and for the bodies, as described. I (medium) that the family is Afton's; I, medium-high, that he dies (R6: implied, never stated). That he was watched from that desk in 1983 is low-medium and is not part of the step: it needs a watching post older than step 11's business (R3). Its place before steps 7 and 8 is C: no record orders the week against the child outside or the five, and a date for the five before 1983 is allowed |

Its place before step 8 is **C**, low-medium, on one sentence; its place relative to steps 1 to 6 is C |
| 8 | June 26th of a year that is not printed (the model's bracket: 1980 to 1986): at the older Freddy Fazbear's Pizza, William Afton, most likely in the Spring Bonnie suit, lures children into the hidden back room. I, strong, that the man in the costume was Afton (R1, link e). I for the place and the suit. I (medium) that the bodies went into the mascots, which no record shows. The Puppet's part is a reading of one minigame. Who the caught man was is open, and the model does not tell him as Afton |

Where step 8 falls among them is not stated: the model reads the Night 5 tape's two reminders as the aftermath of step 8, so the five come before that tape, on either side of the retirement (I, low-medium). **Orderings the records fix:** 5 before everything Fazbear does after 1979; 8 before 12 (medium-high); 12 before
13; 13 before 14; 15 before 17; 17 before 18; 17 before 20 (medium-high); 11 before 15 and before 16; 16 before 18;
20 before 21 (medium-high); 22 before 23 before 26; 26 before 28.

**Orderings that are inferred:** 14 before 15 (medium, lower end: R12); 18 before 20 (medium: only if the volunteer
is Michael); 20 before 25 (medium: only if the buried pizzeria is Pizzeria Simulator's building, R20); 27
between 26 and 28 (medium-high: R22; an earlier version of this list had it among the orderings the records
fix).

**Orderings that are the model's convention:** 6 (the 1983 week) relative to 7 and to 8; 7 (the child outside)
relative to 1 through 5; 7 before 8 (low-medium, on one sentence: R7); 11 relative to 8 through 14; 16 relative to 12 through 15; 20 before 22 and before
24; 24 relative to 23 (the emails interleave both threads; by delivery order only the first email about Ness's
searches precedes the scan). Step 9's
place after 8 and before 10 is inferred, not fixed, and its place before 12 is R11's assumption. On 20 before
22: an earlier version of this list called it inferred, medium, because the company calls itself "no longer a
corporate entity" at the end of step 20 and is trading at step 22. That fits either order, and so does every
other record that bears on it (R33). The model keeps "fire, then scan" as the order it tells, and marks it.

**Three sequences the model does not tell.** "The child of step 6 was watched from the facility of step 11": the
order has the facility later (R3). "The man caught at step 8 was Afton, who then pitches Circus Baby's at step
11": each half is allowed, and the pair is the least likely sequence in the list (R8, point 6). R12's convention
is the other party to both. And, after the third review, a third: "after step 20 the killer is held in a
night, is copied into code, walks abroad and stands under the Pizzaplex". Each of the four is a ruling at
medium or below, and R33 says what the model holds of them together: not one history.


## 4. Outside the story

`outsideStory` lists the records that are **of a kind that carries no story weight, and are cited nowhere**.
Those are two different facts about a record, and after the third review this section keeps them apart.

**Kind.** Every record has a kind, and its kind is what it is, whatever the model would like it to be: a story
record; a joke; a mechanic; a staged prop; a tale with a teller; promotion; development commentary; biography;
a non-canon release; a declared-imaginary game; an unplaced adaptation; or cut, unused and data-only content.
A record's kind never changes because a chapter uses it.

**Use.** A chapter may cite a record of any kind except the last, and when it cites one that is not a story
record it prints the kind beside the citation: "a joke of Scott's", "a staged prop", "a tale", "a mechanic the
tape explains". A joke that is quoted is still a joke.

Four rules decide membership before any group is consulted:

1. A record cited by a chapter, listed as counter-evidence, or filed under a chapter's `further` is **not**
   listed outside. Counter-evidence is never outside. That is a fact of use, and the list says so: it is the
   list of records nobody cites, sorted by kind.
2. The kind is decided by what the record is, not by what it would do to the model. A record that cuts against
   a ruling is filed with that ruling.
3. When two records hold the same fact (the store texts and three teasers recorded in both `fnaf123.json` and
   `scott.json`; the Security Breach page-source dialogue in both `hw.json` and `scott.json`; Scott's Help Wanted
   post in two files), cite one and file the other under the same chapter's `further`. Duplicates are not outside.
4. Inside a company product, decide the layer first (W5). The level as built can go outside; the frame and the
   intrusions cannot.

| id | Title | Reason (for the `reason` field) | Which records are of this kind |
|---|---|---|---|
| `non-canon-per-scott` | Releases Scott ruled non-canon | Scott said FNaF4's Halloween Edition is not canon and that Sister Location's Custom Night gameplay is not canon. | Records of what happens only in those two releases: the three `fnaf4-halloween-*` records, and any record of Sister Location Custom Night play. Not its cutscenes, which he ruled canon. Not UCN lines spoken by Halloween Edition characters, which are UCN's text (R27) |
| `declared-imaginary` | FNaF World's adventure | The game calls its own adventure "an imaginary game", and its second update talks about itself as a product that failed. | `fw-*` records of the adventure, its bosses, its ordinary endings and Update 2's self-commentary. The frame-voice records (opening, clock quest, clock ending), Old Man Consequences and the desk-man teaser are story records at support weight (R17) |
| `unplaced-adaptation` | Into the Pit | An adaptation of a book story that no main-line game refers to and Scott has not placed. It may be shown to agree with the model; nothing rests on it. | Every `into-the-pit-*` record, its three secret minigames included. The few that the Into the Pit section cites as agreeing or not agreeing (R23) are cited there with the kind printed; the rest are listed here. Replaces the group `stories-within`: Candy Cadet's stories and UCN's cartoons are tales with a teller (W6) and are cited in the "tales inside the story" passage (R25) |
| `company-set-dressing` | The level as built, inside Fazbear's own products | Scenery in a company recreation shows what the company staged. | Level furniture of Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear, Help Wanted 2 and Special Delivery: Dreadbear's laboratory, the truck's lights, the game-over graveyard, "Jimmy's cake", the Dark Circus, Candy Cadet's sales pitch. Not the intrusions: the dancing figure on the hill and the rabbit's part in Pizza Party are story records and belong to the chapter on the rabbit in the code. A staged prop that a chapter shows agreeing (the 1983 banner, the items in Freddy, Springtrap at the window) is cited there as "a staged prop" |
| `mechanics-and-rosters` | Mechanics and rosters | A tuning value or a control scheme tells no story. A mechanic counts only when a character, a document or a screen in the game explains or names it (R27). | How to play: difficulty levels, controls, power and doors, roster blurbs that only instruct, office skins, challenge presets, collectible counts, achievements without text. Mechanics that pass R27's test are story records and are cited as "a mechanic" (`fnaf2-golden-freddy-night6-only`, `ucn-roster-scrap-baby-controlled-shock`, `fnaf3-audio-lure-mechanic`); `ucn-death-coin-fredbear` is a scripted scene; `ucn-roster-two-forms-at-once` is cited for what kind of thing UCN's roster is |
| `not-evidence` | Cut, unused, data-only or unverified | Under the source policy only what can be reached in play, or what Scott said, is evidence. These records are kept so that nobody re-adds them as facts. | Unused audio and assets, data labels, file names never shown on screen, and wording that could not be verified: `hw2-unused-vanny-dont-help-it-escape`, `ffps-midnight-motorist-later-that-night`, `ffps-hry223-label`, `fw-unused-open-box`, and the like. Each entry says what the item is and not which way it points (W7). Textures whose place in play is unconfirmed are not of this kind: they are cited with that status |
| `development-commentary` | How the games were made | Out-of-universe: Scott on tools, schedules, patches, working names and what each game was for, where a chapter does not use the statement as a ruling. | Development posts, patch notes, the making-of extras, the developer's notes printed in FNaF3's newspaper margins, "Freddybear". Statements that work as rulings (what Sister Location was made to fix, what Pizzeria Simulator was made to resolve, the retcon post) are cited by the frame chapters. One statement about working names is cited too: Scott's account of "Glitchtrap" and "Burntrap" is the licence for R26's tier 2 and is cited by the labels note |
| `promotion` | Promotion and release logistics | Sales copy, release dates and gameplay previews make no claim about the story. | Store text that only sells, teasers that show a character or a mechanic and nothing else, countdowns, years on teasers that are release dates, thank-you images. A teaser with in-world text (the hidden news report, the ejection dialogue, "Stay the course", "I am still here") is a story record. So is the "1979" that ends the Secret of the Mimic teaser (`secret-of-the-mimic-teaser-1979`): the teaser came out in 2024 and the game in 2025, so that year is not a release date, and it is the game's setting (R2) |
| `jokes-and-trolls` | Jokes, trolls and hoaxes | Not offered as story by their author. | Fake cancellations and delays, the "lite" cover story for Pizzeria Simulator, mock reviews, joke answers, the random joke lawsuits, UCN's comic one-offs. A chapter that quotes one to make a point (the "dream theory" mock quote; the lawsuit about a bitten head as the game joking about the Bite) cites it as "a joke" |
| `community-and-merchandise` | Scott and the community; merchandise; films | About fans, creators, products and adaptations, not about the games' story. | Early-access and dedication posts, replies about merchandise, film and script remarks, and endorsements of fan videos beyond the points he names. The named points (Miketrap; purple; 1983 against 1987) are cited by chapters. `scott-2024-no-new-lore-on-products` is cited by R23 |
| `author-context` | Scott himself | Biography. It explains the author, not the story. | The faith and career records. If the moral-map chapter is kept and cites some inline, those are cited; the rest are listed here |

**What is deliberately not a group.** "Colour" is not a kind: atmosphere that is in-world (rare screens,
posters, the soap opera, gameplay advice from Phone Guy) is filed under the nearest chapter's `further`.
Statements about the books that work as rulings are cited in the ground-rules chapter.

Review: the third review found that rule 2 was contradicted by three group definitions, which let a record
leave a group when a chapter used it. Kind and use are now separate, and a cited record prints its kind. Into
the Pit's secret minigames go with Into the Pit. The dancing figure and the rabbit's part in Pizza Party are
intrusions and leave the set dressing. One clause each covers the working names that R26 relies on and the
"1979" of a teaser that is not a release date.

## 5. Changes to conclusions

Written for the changelog and for whoever updates `people.ts`, `relations.ts`, `timeline.ts`, `deaths.ts`,
`dates.ts`, `places.ts`, `home.ts` and the community verdicts.

[Round 4d: this section is now a log. It lists what these rulings changed in the theory of 1 October 2026 and
what each layer was asked to do. The site has since been rewritten to the rulings, and every item here has been
carried out or overtaken. Its rows keep the wording they had when they were written, except where a later
round marks a change. Where this section and a ruling differ, the ruling governs; where either differs from a
record as it now stands, the record governs for what is shown or stated.]

The night is in **1979, most likely 7 September**, read from a work order's texture whose place in play is unconfirmed | R2 | chapter `partners` (retitle: no record says "partners"; the game says "old friend"); beat `s-workshop`; `timeline.ts` and `dates.ts` Murray entries; persons `hen`, `wa-signatory`, `cassette-man`, `william-afton` (5.2) |

"Twenty years" is loose talk that points away from 1988, and no year is printed from it. FNaF1 is not before November 1988 (derived, high, from the cheques). The five fall in a bracket, 1980 to 1986. No year is printed for FNaF1, FNaF3 or later | R24 | chapter `timeline-math`; `dates.ts` (`t-sd-virus`, `digital`, `special-delivery`); `t-missing-children`, `t-fnaf1`, `t-fazbears-fright` and every later year label |

### 5.2 Errors of fact the analysts found (to be corrected wherever they appear)

**In `theory.ts`.**
- `plush`: the Night 6 promise is not "spoken over nothing but two white dots" (that screen is FNaF World's); the
  plush does not track the child with its eyes outdoors; the "different colour" of "You're broken" does not survive
  measurement. Records: `fnaf4-plush-night6-pupils-only`, `fnaf4-night6-fade-order`, `fnaf4-plush-eyes-by-scene`,
  `fnaf4-speaker-text-colours`, `fw-opening-put-you-back-together`.
- `golden`: FNaF World's clock-ending trophy is not FNaF4's child (`fw-crying-child-character`). "An edited photo
  of his son with no lore meaning": Scott said the first half and implied the second.
- `bite`: "a flatline plays" is stated as fact from an `implied` record (`fnaf4-flatline`); the lawsuit about a
  bitten head is a joke among random joke lawsuits, not evidence that "bites kept happening"
  (`ffps-lawsuit-head-bitten`); in 2024 Scott did not say he "never pursued a specific object" (`scott-2024-box-interview`).
- `murders`: any pull quote from `fnaf1-news-closing` must read "the tragedy that took place there many years
  ago" (the record's earlier "struck there" was the wiki's transcription and is now corrected); "haunted" is asserted of the older models with no citation; the child's hat, watch and shoe found
  "inside Freddy" are props in a level of a company product (`hw-pns-freddy-child-items`); the 1987 events are listed out of
  the calls' order.
- `timeline-math`: `fnaf2-call5-fredbears-family-diner` does not say the diner "was running in 1983".
- `retcon`: "not really a retcon" is in Scott's original post, not a later addition, and the cited record does not
  contain the phrase; cite `scott-2017-clarification-is-not-retcon`.
- `canon-rules`: Scott's sentence about the game developer is quoted without "supposedly" and "may or may not be a
  cover up" (`scott-2019-only-game-developer-existence-canon`); "Help Wanted is canon" omits "questionably canon"
  characters (`scott-2019-help-wanted-is-canon`).
- `ucn`: the Orville line is spliced; it reads "He tried to release you. He tried to release us."
  (`ucn-orville-he-tried-to-release`). The roster is not "every machine his family built".
- `ffps`: "the franchise is a cover" is cited to a record that says only that there is a franchise; Lefty's
  striped arm is the weakest Lefty record (`ffps-lefty-alley-puppet-arm`); lead with `ffps-lefte-blueprint`,
  `ffps-lefte-suit-seal`, `ucn-roster-lefty-music-box`.
- `partners`: W.A.'s signed message offers one insider one franchise location in return for the staff list; the
  double-pay job offers are in an unsigned message (`secret-of-the-mimic-mail-staff-contacts-wa`,
  `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-moving-on-poached`). `ffps-hry223-label` and `secret-of-the-mimic-teaser-1979` do not
  belong in its counter-evidence. "Partners" is the chapter's own word: no record says partner, founder or
  owner, and the letters signed W.A. never say "Fazbear" (R2). "'NEW MENU ITEMS!': a place already open" is
  more than a poster of unchecked placement can carry.
- `vanny`: Help Wanted 2 never names the mask; RUIN's V.A.N.N.I. is the network, and the assistant is Helpi; the
  therapy "notes" are recordings.
- `burntrap`: "a human-looking jawbone" is a look, not a finding; "the Tangle" is a file name.
- `mimic`: the babysitter is M1, the violent machine M2; the M.X.E.S. terminal is in the Inventor's House and the
  matching cabinet in the R&D security office, not "Murray's basement". The copying machine was not what
  Fazbear was paying for: the client learned of M2 through a leak. The sentence about an endoskeleton "that can
  adapt to any costume" is the voice on the radio's and is not quoted as Fazbear's (R2, R21).
- `thesis`: nobody is shown setting FNaF3's fire (`fnaf3-it-burns-newspaper`).
- `afton-house`: `ffps-midnight-motorist-later-that-night` is a data label; `fnaf4-kid-who-hides-under-table` and
  `fnaf4-plush-locked-in-room` are not counter-evidence.
- `open`: there are five stones in the row, the fifth hidden, and a sixth apart, so "four names and five masks" is
  not a mismatch; Gregory has no record in Fazbear's systems, not "anywhere".

**In `people.ts`.**
- *Display names.* The link token `{{entity-id}}` prints an entity's `name` as stored, so eight names must
  change before writers use the token for them (R26 has the table): `glitchtrap` "Glitchtrap" to "the rabbit in
  the code"; `burntrap` "Burntrap" to "the burnt rabbit under the Pizzaplex"; `scraptrap` "Scraptrap" to "the
  salvaged rabbit of Pizzeria Simulator"; `the-mimic` "The Mimic (M2)" to "the copying endoskeleton"; `tape-girl`
  "Tape Girl" to "the tape speaker"; `phone-dude` "Phone Dude" to "the attraction's caller"; `shadow-freddy`
  "Shadow Freddy" to "the dark bear"; `crying-child` "The Crying Child" to "the crying child". The old names
  become aliases that say whose label each is: "(Scott's working name)" for tier 2, "(a fan name)" or "(a file
  name)" for tier 3, and a tier-3 label stands nowhere but in such an alias (R26, as changed in round 4b). `glitchtrap`, `scraptrap` and `the-mimic` are marked
  `nameStatus: "canon-name"` and are descriptors. Other descriptor entities ("The Older Brother", "The Purple
  Man", "The Missing Children", "The UCN Protagonist") print with capitals that read as names in mid-sentence
  and need a lower-case form.
- `cassette-man`: the alias "HRY223 tape label (reported)" is non-evidence under W7 and goes; "Hen (SotM,
  theory)" becomes a note that the possibility is low (R2); the theory text calls him "William Afton's business
  partner at Fredbear's Family Diner", which no record carries. `william-afton`: its theory text has "the
  partner who ordered the springlock suits"; the ordering client in the logs is "Hen", and "partner" is the
  model's reading at medium. `hen`: status "Theory: the Cassette Man" becomes "possibly the Cassette Man (low)".
  `wa-signatory`: "offer Murray staff the management of franchise locations" (one insider is offered one
  location, for the staff list).
- `glitchtrap`: "a purple rabbit figure" (it is yellow); status "Crushed by Vanny (HW2 secret ending)" (one
  ending of Help Wanted 2 shows a tiny copy of it crushed, and RUIN, most likely the later of the two, still
  has a rabbit's shadow in the mask); its theory text
  "Afton-derived code" becomes the two readings of R18. `burntrap`: "Tangle drags it" (the mass of parts; one
  ending). `the-mimic`: "built to entertain Murray's son" (that is M1, and the sentence is the voice on the
  radio's); status "At large after RUIN's escape" (RUIN's endings are branches). `f10-n4`: "in Murray's
  basement". `edwin-murray`: "Paralysed by an explosion" (the "detonation" is a journal entry M2 recites; he
  says he lost his temper and cannot feel his legs; the cause is not stated).
- `fredbear-plush`: "At the end only its two dots remain". `jeremy-hw`: "Presumed dead" (not stated).
  `ucn-protagonist`: its theory text argues for Michael. The missing-children entry states "the fifth being
  Golden Freddy" and the yellow suit as the disguise flat, and cites `hw-pns-freddy-child-items`, which is a
  staged prop. The entity id `henrys-daughter` and the group `emily` carry book-only names.

**In `games.json`.** Three `settingYearLabel` values contradict the model. Into the Pit: "the past era is 1985
(calendar seen in-game)"; no calendar was found in the game, and the record says the calendar belongs to the
book the game adapts (`into-the-pit-past-year-1985`). This is a book detail printed as a game fact; write "a
logo graphic reads 1985 (placement unconfirmed)". Secret of the Mimic: "Undated here... in-game dates not
verified by this research"; write "1979, most likely 7 September (a work order in the van; R2)". FNaF4:
"Undated (no year is shown in-game)"; the television shows "Fredbear & Friends 1983"
(`fnaf4-fredbear-and-friends-1983`); write "c. 1983 (a show's title card; R24)".

**In `relations.ts`.** `ucn-protagonist` same-as `michael-afton`; `crying-child` same-as
`one-you-should-not-have-killed`; `older-brother` same-as `michael-afton` (UCN records in its evidence); `vanny`
killed `glitchtrap` (canon); `missing-children` possesses `freddy-fazbear` (cites `hw-pns-freddy-child-items`);
`the-mimic` killed `edwin-murray` (strong; his fate is not shown). `cassette-man` killed `william-afton` stays,
with its wording checked against R28: the game does not show who lights the fire, and presents it as his end.
After the third review: `hen` same-as `cassette-man` cites `ffps-hry223-label`, which is not evidence, and is
low on role alone (R2). `cassette-man` partner-of `william-afton` stays at theory, medium; two of its four
records are the Hen and W.A. letters, which carry it only through R2's inferences, and "partner" is the
model's word. `wa-signatory` same-as `william-afton`: its note says "a franchise-building business owner",
which no record says of either. `glitchtrap` same-as `william-afton`: its note "Afton-derived code" becomes
"presents as the killer; a leaving of him (medium) or a copier that learned him (low-medium)", and its
evidence should lead with what is shown, not with the fan decode (R18). `burntrap` same-as `william-afton`:
"Human jawbone" is a look (R20). `edwin-murray` created `the-mimic` cites the log about M1 and a line of the
voice on the radio; cite `secret-of-the-mimic-mail-received-5-min-ago` and
`secret-of-the-mimic-log-try-again-build-another` for M2 (R21).

**In `timeline.ts` and `dates.ts`.**
- Murray entries: `t-springlocks-for-diner` is at 1978 with the label "before the diner opens" (the message does
  not say the diner is unopened; whether it was written while Fiona was alive is only medium, R2; and the entry
  folds in W.A.'s later land claim); `t-fiona-dies` at 1978.5 and `d-david` at 1979.0 must precede M2, the walk-out and the last 1977 order;
  `t-fiona-dies` offers the 1983 fairground banner as possibly the same festival, which the 1979 night rules out;
  `t-mimic-born`, `d-edwin`, `d-murray-technicians`, `d-arnold` say "undated (teaser shows 1979)" where the
  night's year is also on a work order in the van (R2: a texture, placement in play unconfirmed); `dates.ts` says of the Costume Manor that "the only year shown is the 1979 teaser"; the founding
  era's range should start by 1965.
- `t-diner-closes` joins the tapes' springlock failures at "a sister location" to the diner's closing; the tapes
  never mention the diner.
- `t-missing-children`: "the fifth being Golden Freddy" and "the yellow suit is the disguise" stated flat; its
  date basis rests on "many years ago", which counts back from an undated FNaF1.
- `t-fnaf1`: November 1988 is the earliest the cheques allow (derived, high), not a date.
- Any entry that has FNaF1's restaurant close "by year's end": that rests on reading FNaF1's undated clipping as
  FNaF1's own news (R12: inferred, medium). No timeline entry for that closing exists yet.
- `t-dismantle-and-death`: "The safe rooms are then sealed".
- `t-happiest-day`: "This frees the five".
- `t-help-wanted`: "the anomaly merges". Its place after the fire, and the place of every later entry up to the
  Pizzaplex, is the model's convention (R33); label it so.
- `t-sd-virus` and the `dates.ts` entries `t-sd-virus`, `digital`, `special-delivery`: marked printed. The emails
  carry no date; what the records hold are the days on which they reached players. Print no year for them and
  measure no interval by them: only their order counts (R24, point 2).
- `t-hw2-crush`: "Vanny crushes Glitchtrap" as canon at 2022.5, with "the Vanny mask". One ending shows a tiny
  copy of the rabbit crushed; the figure that does it is a silhouette the scene does not identify.
- `dates.ts` on Into the Pit: "No on-screen year" and "The commonly cited 1985 is not from the game". A logo
  graphic in the game reads 1985; where it is shown is unconfirmed (R23).
- Any `dates.ts` entry marked printed whose only support is a staged prop or a texture not seen in play: the
  1970 Fall Fest poster, the "FALLFEST 83'" banner, the 1985 logo, and Secret of the Mimic's work order until it
  is checked (section 3.1, rows 2, 9, 31 and 38).

**In `deaths.ts`.** `d-edwin` ("paralysed after an explosion"); `d-hw-jeremy` (death not stated: "grave injury
implied, fate unknown"); `d-save-them` (state the lean of R9); `d-sb-vanny-fall` and `d-sb-vanny-disassembled` are
branch endings, and no later game is shown to follow either (R19 (4)); `d-desk-man` belongs to FNaF World's
outside layer. `d-ffps-fire` keeps William Afton among the fire's dead: the game presents it as his end. An
earlier version of this line said "he is burned, not ended"; that told R16's lean and R18's reading as facts, and
it is withdrawn. What persists of him is those rulings' question.

**In `places.ts`.** The original Freddy Fazbear's Pizza and FNaF1's restaurant are probably one building (R12);
the clippings are tagged to the second while the first's summary cites them. Pizzeria Simulator's building and the
buried "Freddy Fazbear's Pizza Place" are probably one site (R20).

**In `home.ts` and the community verdicts.** "The games are the cover-up" is strong, not canon. Verdicts that
touch R3, R4, R8, R9, R11, R16, R18, R20 and R21 need re-scoring: in particular "William is UCN's player" moves
to a lean in its favour at low-medium, with "Michael is UCN's player" kept as a live alternative, and "Burntrap is
the Mimic" moves against at medium. After the third review, verdicts on what the rabbit in the code is should
show two live readings (a leaving of the killer, medium; a copying program, low-medium), and any verdict that
"Vanny killed Glitchtrap" or that "Vanessa was freed" is one ending's, at low-medium. "FNaF4's nights are only a dream"
does not move against, as an earlier version of this line said: after review R4 holds only that the bedroom is a
real place, and rates "waking events, not nightmares" low-medium, so that verdict stays open with a slight lean
against.

**Display.** The UI renders `verifiedVia` as a link; many records hold several URLs or free text there, and some
of the wiki pages they point at have book-only names in their titles. Do not print the field as text.

## 6. Open risks

The rulings most likely to be wrong, in the order a reviewer should attack them.

[Round 4d: the table is as it stood on 2 and 3 October. Since then the cast credits were added and R19 (1)
lowered to medium (round 4c), the "PROTOTYPE" label was viewed in a capture, and the records were re-worded to
say only what is on the screen or the tape. The checks still to be made, in play and by ear, are listed below
and in `round4.md`.]

| # | Ruling | Why it is at risk | What would flip it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **R16: UCN's player is William Afton** (low-medium: the model's lean) | It reverses the last audit, on a margin the second review cut down. It is conditional on W3, which goes beyond Scott's one precedent. It needs the old man's listener to be someone other than the man in the office. "You won't die", "a victim that can't perish", "He tried to release you", "I don't hate you" and Nightmare Fredbear's three lines all sit better with Michael. What holds the lean is "This time, death cannot save you" and the register of wickedness and misdeeds | A reading of `ucn-nightmarionne-lines` that fits Michael; a line naming the player; any game showing Michael after the fire; a statement from Scott on UCN's standing. Two of the three records this row once named as the test, `ucn-puppet-lines` and `ucn-withered-chica-toysnhk`, already have a Michael reading |
| 2 | **R16: the accuser is the fifth child** (low: a lean) | It follows from (1) and from R8, and two of UCN's own links run the other way, to FNaF4's Nightmares and to Fredbear. It is also the fandom's standard answer, whose usual support this archive does not admit, so the records alone may not have chosen it | A record tying the fifth child to FNaF4; or tying the crying child to the yellow bear, which would reopen "one presence that is both" |
| 3 | **R11: "a yellow one" is the yellow bear, not Spring Bonnie** (low-medium) | It stands on one assumption, that FNaF3's tapes predate November 1987, and the only reason on file for that date is the suit itself. It needs a second: that the bear was not sealed in with the rabbit, though one tape retires both together. It also needs the tapes' building to be the old location. Every later game puts the killer in the rabbit. Step 9 of the order and the Night 5 tape pairing in R8 rest on the same assumption | Evidence that the tapes belong to FNaF1's era, or that Spring Bonnie was taken out before the sealing |
| 4 | **R12: the killer dies after FNaF1's restaurant closed, in the old building** (medium, lower end) | It rests on one thing, R9's living culprit in November 1987. He dies inside a room the tapes say was walled up before then, and no game shows him opening it. The rival placement, while the old restaurant "rotted" before 1987, explains the empty wet building equally well, and has the faceless older models of 1987 and Scott's trailer caption "dismantled" on its side. Step 14 and four dating rows also hang on reading FNaF1's undated closing clipping as FNaF1's own news | A game showing the four machines rebuilt after a dismantling; a date for the sealing, or for the clipping |
| 5 | **R12 and section 3.2: where Circus Baby's venture and Michael's week fall** (low; convention) | One sentence of HandUnit's carries the venture's place, and nothing carries the week's. The usual fan order (Elizabeth before the five) is neither supported nor excluded | Any dated record for the Pizza World or the rental business |
| 6 | **R3, R4, R5 against R12: the watching post and the year** (low-medium for "watched in 1983") | Found in review. The Private Room belongs to a business that the model's own order starts after 1983. "The child was watched in 1983" (R3), "the nights are waking events" (R4) and "the toy was spoken through" (R5) each want a post that existed that week. Either the post is older than the business that houses it, or R12's convention is wrong and Circus Baby's venture came before 1983, or the rooms on the monitors are a set built later. The model picks none. The same convention is why the man caught after the five is no longer read as Afton (R8, point 6: open, low for either reading) | A date for the facility or for the room; a record of the Private Room before the rental business; a later game mentioning the arrest, the charge or a release |
| 7 | **R9: SAVE THEM is not a second set of murders** (low-medium) | FNaF2 by itself reads as a second killing: bodies in the 1987 building, a purple figure with a badge-like mark, "tragic", an investigation. The model overrules it from later games' counts, and has no act to put in its place: what was done in November 1987 is not recorded | Any trace of 1987 victims in a later game |
| 8 | **R18, R20, R21: the modern era's three figures** (medium down to low) | The rival reading is a copying program that reached the company's hardware, and it is not answered by the concrete: it is answered only by three absences (no Murray software on a Fazbear board; no source named but old hardware the client sent; no Murray mark in Help Wanted, Curse of Dreadbear or Special Delivery). Everything that makes the rabbit in the code look like Afton is surface that a copier would wear too. The burnt rabbit as that fire's relic rests on the site. "The copier has taken up the rabbit's part" is the model's own bridge across a gap the games leave open. The machine behind the concrete is shown to have had the building's audio, so "sealed" is not "cut off" | A game stating which board, whose body, or how the endoskeleton came to the Pizzaplex; any record of Murray's software on company hardware; a statement in play that the endoskeleton was the anomaly all along |
| 9 | **R3: the house on the monitors is Afton's own home** (medium) | Everything shown is equally true of a household he studied that was not his, and of rooms built to be observed. The family's shape is compatible and no more: it needs a third Afton child whom no game mentions. The keypad's digits rest on wiki text. Neither game says that the 3D rooms on the monitors are the 8-bit house where the family lives: the facility's map draws them as two clusters, and the one shared fixture is a clock read from a dark still | A game showing that house with another family, or the Private Room's views as a built set |
| 10 | **R2, R23 and R26: rulings that rest on how something was read** | Secret of the Mimic's date was read from wiki images of props, not from play; month-first order and the meaning of "Aug 3rd" are inferences; the research-floor memo strains two derived dates. The 1985 logo's place in Into the Pit is unconfirmed. Tier 2 of the labels ruling rests on one transcribed sentence | The checks listed below this table |
| 11 | **R19: one woman, and which ending** (medium; low-medium) | The rooftop page shows a woman in Vanessa's cap and uniform above the unmasked woman, and the model has no account of it. Security Breach's cast list gives the guard and the rabbit-costumed woman to two performers (`security-breach-credits-vanessa-vanny-two-parts`, read from the wiki's transcription and not from the credits roll). Two things carry one woman: the recordings and the name. Everything that says Vanessa was freed is seen through the mask or inside a simulation | A capture of the credits roll; a later game showing Vanessa after that night with the mask off; two women shown together; any third record for two women |
| 12 | **R33: what persists of Afton, and "fire, then scan"** (medium; convention) | Four strands at medium or below. They fit together only on a picture no record gives, and two of them pull against each other without it. The order of the fire and the first scan rests on no record, and the model's preferred reading of the rabbit in the code leans on that order | A date for the scan against the fire; a game naming him after the fire; a game saying which board or whose remains |

**Records to add.** None of these exists in the archive, and the model rules on none of them until it does.
- Security Breach's cast credits: added on 3 October as `security-breach-credits-vanessa-vanny-two-parts`, from
  the wiki's transcription, and entered under "Records against" in R19 (1). What is still wanted is a capture
  of the credits roll itself.
- Security Breach's two collectible posters of Fredbear's Family Diner, one of which reuses the artwork of
  Secret of the Mimic's poster (reported by the reviewer as seen on the wiki).
- The small orange-eyed scrap machines reported in Secret of the Mimic's walk-away route (from a wiki stub,
  unseen). It bears on "Every copy has failed" and on R21 (2).
- What Help Wanted 2's gallery prints beside its models ("Vanny", "The Mimic", "Glitchtrap").
- What RUIN's Helpi calls the figure from the M.X.E.S. cabinet.

**Checks to make in play.** The work order and credential form in the van of Secret of the Mimic: whether they
can be read, and what they say (row 9 is "printed" only after this). Murray's chart, memos and order forms,
which were read from the same kind of image (rows 1, 4, 4a and 7). Where the diner poster hangs. Where the
1985 logo appears in Into the Pit. Where the 1970 Fall Fest poster hangs. The floor plan of the buried
pizzeria against Pizzeria Simulator's building. The last scene of Help Wanted 2's main ending, which was
described from a summary and not watched.

**A check to make by ear.** Seven sentences of the 2024 interview carry tier 2 of R26 and several asides, and
each has a timestamp in its record: "possession of AI or possession of machines or circuitry" (17:14); "that's
the canon name now" (17:47); the M.X.E.S. passage with "that was a character" (18:53); "it is very different
than the one that got in the game" (20:29); "Originally, Burntrap was never supposed to move" (21:56); "I
don't think it's the last that we've seen of the Mimic" (23:45); "It was still just supposed to be a novelty"
(41:43). Ten minutes of listening would settle them. Until then tier 2 is the first thing a chapter should be
ready to lose; after the third review no rule of scope and no ruling's direction rests on any of the seven.

Two risks of a different kind:

- **Under-connection.** R6 leaves the crying child nowhere and R10 leaves the dark rabbit unexplained. After the
  second review R30 also declines to say where the five are between the dismantling and the Cassette Man's call:
  its table says "not shown". The owner asked for a theory that connects everything. The model declines to
  connect these because the records do not; the chapters should say so plainly and give the best lead.
- **Dependence.** R16 carries R6's and R18's weakest clauses and the moral map. The reviewers did not overturn
  it; they cut it to a lean. If a later record flips it, restore the Michael reading in the UCN chapter only: R3 no longer depends on it, and that independence is the
  most durable change in this pass. R4 is not as free: its clause 2 and the word "illusion" as applied to the
  child lean on R16 (see R4's review line), and with Michael in UCN's office the nights would read as the
  brother's.
